**5.1.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Having performed the marriage, the Kuru heroes — their own side gladdened on account of Abhimanyu — rested for four dawns and then, refreshed, proceeded to the hall of Virāṭa.

**5.1.2**  That hall of the lord of the Matsyas was splendid — adorned with the finest gems and precious stones, furnished with seats, garlanded, and fragrant. Into it entered those foremost of noble kings.

**5.1.3**  Then the two foremost lords of men, Virāṭa and Drupada, took their seats in front; and the elder, held in honour among the lords of earth, the Grandsire, took his seat alongside Rāma and Janārdana.

**5.1.4**  Near the king of the Pāñcālas sat the hero of the Śini clan together with the son of Rohiṇī; and very close to the king of the Matsyas sat Janārdana himself and Yudhiṣṭhira.

**5.1.5**  All the sons of king Drupada, Bhīma and Arjuna, the two sons of Mādravatī, and Pradyumna and Samba — both heroes in battle — and the son of Virāṭa together with Abhimanyu.

**5.1.6**  And the young princes, the sons of Draupadī — all heroes equal to their fathers in valour, form, and strength — took their seats upon the excellent seats embellished with gold.

**5.1.7**  As those great car-warriors sat there, resplendent in their garments and ornaments, that royal and prosperous hall shone like the sky adorned with bright planets.

**5.1.8**  Then those foremost of men, having engaged in various assembled conversations, stood silent for a moment in thought, those kings gazing upon Kṛṣṇa.

**5.1.9**  And having reached the end of conversation, stirred together by the Mādhava for the sake of the Pāṇḍava cause, those lion-like kings assembled and heard words of great import and great consequence.

**5.1.10**  Kṛṣṇa said: It is known to all of you how Yudhiṣṭhira was defeated at dice by the son of Subala through trickery, and how his kingdom was taken away, and how the compact was made for a further period of exile.

**5.1.11**  Though capable of conquering the earth by force, and steadfast in truth, those foremost of the Bharatas, the sons of Pāṇḍu, fulfilled that severe vow — thirteen years in exile.

**5.1.12**  And this thirteenth year, most difficult to endure — spent unrecognized in your midst, enduring unbearable hardships — how it was passed is known to all of you.

**5.1.13**  Things being so, reflect on what would be beneficial to the son of Dharma as king, and to Duryodhana as well — what is righteous, proper, and honour-winning for the Kurus and Pāṇḍavas.

**5.1.14**  Even the sovereignty of the gods, if tainted by unrighteousness, the king of righteousness would not desire; but lordship over the earth, if coupled with righteousness and right purpose, he would wish to possess even in the smallest village.

**5.1.15**  The ancestral kingdom is known to all kings — how it was wrested away by the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra through deceitful means; and yet through all this, great and unbearable hardship has been endured.

**5.1.16**  Nor has Pārtha been defeated in battle by the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra through their own prowess; and yet the king, together with his friends, wishes for nothing but their well-being.

**5.1.17**  What the sons of Pāṇḍu themselves won by conquest, subjugating the lords of the earth and bringing it all together — that is what these foremost of men, the sons of Kuntī and the sons of Mādravatī, now seek.

**5.1.18**  But these heroes, though foe-conquering, were set upon by those wicked and violent men using various stratagems, seeking to kill them and seize the kingdom — all of which is fully known to you.

**5.1.19**  Considering the deep greed of those men, and the righteous nature of Yudhiṣṭhira, and also the kinship between them — deliberate, both together and separately, and form your judgment.

**5.1.20**  These men, ever devoted to truth, have fulfilled the compact as agreed; yet if treated otherwise by those others, they could destroy the assembled sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra.

**5.1.21**  Hearing of the wrong done by them, the friends of the king would surround them; they would press upon those men with battle; and being attacked by them in war, they would slay them in return.

**5.1.22**  Yet lest the opinion among you be that these men are insufficient for victory over them — let all together, united with their friends, strive for the destruction of those opponents.

**5.1.23**  The intention of Duryodhana is not truly known — what will he do? And when the intent of the other side is unknown, what course is most to be undertaken in your judgment?

**5.1.24**  Therefore let there go from here a man of righteous character, pure, well-born, and vigilant — a capable envoy to seek peace with them and the granting of half the kingdom to Yudhiṣṭhira.

**5.1.25**  Hearing the words of Janārdana — righteous, purposeful, sweet, and impartial — his elder brother then took up the reply, honouring those words highly, O king.

**5.2.1**  Baladeva said: You have heard the words of the elder brother of Gada — how they are righteous and purposeful, and beneficial alike to Ajātaśatru and equally to king Duryodhana.

**5.2.2**  For the heroes, the sons of Kuntī, strive after giving up half the kingdom on his account; and the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, by granting half, would rejoice greatly in happiness together with us.

**5.2.3**  For having obtained the kingdom, those foremost of men — if the other side conducts themselves rightly — would assuredly find peace and enter into happiness; and their peace is the welfare of the subjects.

**5.2.4**  To learn also the mind of Duryodhana, and to speak the words of Yudhiṣṭhira — it would please me if someone were to go there for the sake of peace between the Kurus and Pāṇḍavas.

**5.2.5**  He should address Bhīṣma, that hero of the Kurus, and the great-souled son of Vichitraveerya, and Droṇa with his son, and Vidura and Kṛpa, and the king of Gāndhāra together with the charioteer's son.

**5.2.6**  And all the other sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra who are foremost in strength and foremost in scriptural learning, who are established in their own respective duties — heroes among men, mature in learning and age.

**5.2.7**  When all these are assembled together, and the elders of the city are gathered, let the envoy speak words accompanied with humble salutation, such as would serve the interest of the son of Kuntī.

**5.2.8**  And in all circumstances it was not through deceit on their part but by force that Yudhiṣṭhira's interest was seized — the kingdom was taken by trickery when Yudhiṣṭhira was captivated by the pleasure of the game.

**5.2.9**  Though restrained by all the Kuru heroes and friends, this descendant of Ajamidha, being himself ignorant of the game, challenged the skilled dice-player son of the Gāndhāra king to play.

**5.2.10**  There were thousands of other skilled dice-players whom Yudhiṣṭhira could have endured and beaten; but setting them aside, he challenged Śakuni alone, and by him was defeated at dice.

**5.2.11**  He played in return, with the dice always turning away from him in ill luck, and being overcome by force in his eagerness — in that matter, no fault belongs to Śakuni.

**5.2.12**  Therefore let the envoy speak humbly and with much conciliation to the son of Vichitraveerya; for thus is the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra capable of being induced by that man to act in his own proper interest.

**5.2.13**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Even as the hero of the Madhu clan spoke thus, the hero of the Śini clan suddenly sprang up, and censuring that speech, took up these words with indignation.

**5.3.1**  Sātyaki said: As a man's inner self is, so he speaks. As your inner self is shaped, so shaped are your words.

**5.3.2**  Surely there are brave men and there are cowardly men as well; both these firm tendencies are seen to exist among men.

**5.3.3**  In one and the same family are born both a eunuch and a great chariot-warrior, just as a tree may have branches bearing fruit and branches without fruit.

**5.3.4**  I do not blame you for speaking such words, O bearer of the plough-banner; but those who listen to your words — them I blame, O Mādhava.

**5.3.5**  How can one who speaks even slightly of the faults of the king of righteousness dare to utter such words in the midst of this assembly without fear?

**5.3.6**  Skilled dice-players challenged that great soul, who was not knowledgeable in dice and whose trust was exploited — where is the righteousness in such a victory over him?

**5.3.7**  If they had come to the home of the son of Kuntī playing with his brothers and conquered him there — that would have been a victory by dharma for them.

**5.3.8**  But having summoned the king who is ever devoted to the Kṣatriya duty and defeated him by trickery — what good fortune can there be for them beyond that?

**5.3.9**  How should he bow down who, having made the supreme wager, has now been freed from forest exile and has attained the ancestral station?

**5.3.10**  Even if Yudhiṣṭhira were to covet the wealth of others, even then he would not deserve to beg from his enemies to this extreme degree.

**5.3.11**  And how can those who are righteous and not desirous of seizing the kingdom say that the sons of Kuntī, having completed their exile, are recognized and known?

**5.3.12**  Though conciliated by Bhīṣma and the great-souled Droṇa, they do not resolve to give the sons of Pāṇḍu their ancestral property.

**5.3.13**  But I, conciliating them with sharp arrows, shall by force in battle make them fall at the feet of that great-souled son of Kuntī.

**5.3.14**  If they do not resolve to bow down before the wise one, they shall go along with their ministers to the abode of Yama.

**5.3.15**  For they are not capable of withstanding the fury of the wrathful and battle-eager Yuyudhāna, just as mountains cannot withstand the velocity of the thunderbolt.

**5.3.16**  Who indeed can endure in battle the wielder of the Gāṇḍīva bow, or the wielder of the discus weapon? And who can endure me, and who can endure Bhīma, who is difficult to approach?

**5.3.17**  And the twin brothers, wielders of firm bows, of great splendour, comparable to the god of death — what man wishing to live would approach them, or Dhṛṣṭadyumna, the son of Pṛṣata?

**5.3.18**  These five Pāṇḍavas, and the five sons of Draupadī who enhance her glory, equal in measure to the sons of Pāṇḍu, equal in valour, drunk with battle-ardour —

**5.3.19**  — and the great archer Saubhadra, difficult to bear even for the immortals, and Gada, Pradyumna, and Samba, comparable to the thunderbolt of Death, unassailable —

**5.3.20**  — we, having slain in battle the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra together with Śakuni and Karṇa, shall consecrate the Pāṇḍava king.

**5.3.21**  There is no unrighteousness whatsoever in slaying enemies who are aggressors; it is unrighteous and dishonourable to beg from enemies.

**5.3.22**  Fulfil diligently whatever desire lies in his heart; let the Pāṇḍava obtain the kingdom that was surrendered by Dhṛtarāṣṭra.

**5.3.23**  Today let Yudhiṣṭhira the son of Pāṇḍu obtain the kingdom, or let all of them be slain in battle and sleep upon the surface of the earth.

**5.4.1**  Drupada said: So it shall be, O mighty-armed one — there is no doubt. For Duryodhana will not give up the kingdom through gentle persuasion.

**5.4.2**  And Dhṛtarāṣṭra, fond of his son, will follow him; and Bhīṣma and Droṇa out of weakness; and the son of Rādhā and the son of Subala out of folly.

**5.4.3**  Baladeva's speech, in my judgment, is not appropriate; for this is indeed what should be done first by a man who desires sound policy.

**5.4.4**  Gentle words, however, should by no means be spoken to the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra; for in my view that evil-minded one is not to be won over by mildness.

**5.4.5**  One might show gentleness to a donkey and employ sharpness toward cattle; but whoever would speak mild words to Duryodhana of wicked mind —

**5.4.6**  — the wicked man would think the gentle speech springs from weakness; the fool would consider the matter won when met with mildness.

**5.4.7**  This therefore we shall do, and let effort be made here: let us send word to our allies and let them mobilise forces for us.

**5.4.8**  To Śalya, and to Dhṛṣṭaketu, and to Jayatsena, and to all the Kekayas — let swift messengers go.

**5.4.9**  For Duryodhana will surely send word everywhere; and those who have been previously approached tend to side with the one who solicited them first.

**5.4.10**  Therefore make haste to solicit the lords of men before he does; it is my firm conviction that a great task is to be accomplished.

**5.4.11**  Let word be sent swiftly to Śalya and the kings who follow him, and to king Bhagadatta who dwells by the eastern sea.

**5.4.12**  To Amitojas and to the fierce Ugra, and to Hārdikya and Ahuka, and to the far-sighted Malla and to Rocamāna, O lord.

**5.4.13**  Let Bṛhanta and king Senābindu be brought, and Papajit and Prativindhya, and Chitravarma and Suvastuka.

**5.4.14**  Bahlika and Munjakesa, and the lord of the Cedis, and Suparshva and Subāhu, and Pourava the great chariot-warrior.

**5.4.15**  The kings of the Śakas, the Pahlavas, and the Daradas; the Kāmbojas, the Rishikas, and those of the western riverine regions.

**5.4.16**  And Jayatsena and the king of Kāśi, and likewise the kings of the five rivers, and the irresistible son of Krātha, and the mountain kings.

**5.4.17**  And Jānakī and Suśarmā and Maṇimān and Pautimatsyaka, and the lord of the Pamsurashra, and the valiant Dhṛṣṭaketu.

**5.4.18**  And Audra and Daṇḍadhāra, and the valiant Brhatsena, and Aparajita and the Niṣāda king, and Śreṇimān and Vasuman.

**5.4.19**  And Bṛhadbala and Mahaujas, and Bahu and Parapuramjaya, and king Samudrasena together with his valiant son.

**5.4.20**  Adāri and Nadīja and the king Karṇaveṣṭa, and Samartha and Suvīra and Mārjāra and likewise Kanyaka —

**5.4.21**  and Mahāvīra and Kadrū, and Nikara, Tumula, Krātha, and Nīla and Vīradharman and the valiant Bhūmipāla —

**5.4.22**  Durjaya and Dantavaktra and Rukmī and Janamejaya, Āṣāḍha and Vāyuvega and the king Pūrvapālī —

**5.4.23**  Bhūritejā and Devaka and the son of Ekalavya, the kings of Kārūṣa and the valiant Kṣemadhūrti —

**5.4.24**  Udbhava and Kṣemaka and king Vāṭadhāna, and Śrutāyu and Dṛḍhāyu and the valiant son of Śalva —

**5.4.25**  and Kumāra, lord of the Kaliṅgas, who is maddened in battle — let swift messengers be sent to all of these; this indeed is my desire.

**5.4.26**  And let my own priest, this brahmin, be swiftly sent, O king, to Dhṛtarāṣṭra; let the message be entrusted to him.

**5.4.27**  Let him know how Duryodhana is to be addressed, and how the son of Śantanu the king, and how Dhṛtarāṣṭra is to be addressed, and Droṇa the foremost of the wise.

**5.5.1**  Vāsudeva said: This counsel is fitting, O foremost bearer of the Somakas' yoke — it will bring about the success of the mighty Pāṇḍava king.

**5.5.2**  And this is the proper first course of action for us who seek the right policy; for a man who acts otherwise in such matters would be utterly foolish.

**5.5.3**  Yet our kinship and bond is equal with both the Kurus and the Pāṇḍavas, who both conduct themselves as they please.

**5.5.4**  We were all brought here for the sake of the wedding, even as you were, O king; when the marriage is done, we shall return home joyfully.

**5.5.5**  You are the eldest among kings in age and in learning; we are all here as your disciples — of this there is no doubt.

**5.5.6**  Dhṛtarāṣṭra too holds you in constant high regard; and you are the friend of both the preceptors, of Droṇa and of Kṛpa.

**5.5.7**  Therefore let you now send forth the word that will serve the Pāṇḍavas' cause; all of us are certain that whatever you send forth, so it shall be.

**5.5.8**  If the bull of the Kurus should make peace through righteous means, then the great destruction of Kurus and Pāṇḍavas as brothers will not come to pass.

**5.5.9**  But if the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, filled with pride and delusion, should refuse, then after sending to the others, you may summon us in turn.

**5.5.10**  Then that dull-witted Duryodhana, foolish together with his ministers and kinsmen, will meet his end when the wielder of the Gāṇḍīva bow is enraged.

**5.5.11**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Then Virāṭa, lord of the earth, having duly honoured the Vārṣṇeya, sent him homeward together with his retinue and kinsmen.

**5.5.12**  When Kṛṣṇa had departed for Dvārakā, Yudhiṣṭhira and his brothers, along with king Virāṭa lord of the earth, made all preparations for war.

**5.5.13**  Then Virāṭa together with his kinsmen, and king Drupada lord of the earth, sent messengers to all the rulers of the land.

**5.5.14**  At the word of the lion-like Kurus and of the Matsyas and Pāñcālas, those mighty lords of the earth came together, filled with joy.

**5.5.15**  Hearing of the gathering of the sons of Pāṇḍu, a great host assembled; and the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra likewise assembled kings to his side.

**5.5.16**  O king, the entire earth became filled with turmoil on account of the Kurus and Pāṇḍavas, as the lords of the earth set out on their march.

**5.5.17**  The armies of those heroes came from every direction, shaking, as it were, this very earth, goddess adorned with mountains and forests.

**5.5.18**  Then the king of Pāñcāla, with Yudhiṣṭhira's approval, sent his own priest — venerable in wisdom and years — to the Kurus.

**5.6.1**  Drupada said: Among living beings, those that breathe are the highest; among those that breathe, the highest are those who live by intellect; among the intelligent, men are highest; and among men, the twice-born are supreme.

**5.6.2**  Among the twice-born, those learned in the Vedas are better; and among Vedic scholars, those of perfected understanding are best. You, good sir, are the foremost among those of perfected understanding — so it is my judgment.

**5.6.3**  You are distinguished by lineage, by age, and by sacred learning; and in wisdom you are not inferior to Śukra and Aṅgiras themselves.

**5.6.4**  You also know fully how the Kaurava has conducted himself, and how the Pāṇḍava, Yudhiṣṭhira the son of Kuntī, has conducted himself.

**5.6.5**  With Dhṛtarāṣṭra's knowledge the Pāṇḍavas were deceived by their enemies; and even though entreated by Vidura, he only follows his son.

**5.6.6**  For Śakuni, himself expert at dice, deliberately challenged the son of Kuntī — who was ignorant of dice but stood firm in the warrior's code, a man of purity.

**5.6.7**  Having cheated Yudhiṣṭhira the son of Dharma in that manner, they will never of their own will restore the kingdom under any circumstances.

**5.6.8**  But you, speaking righteous words to Dhṛtarāṣṭra, will certainly turn the minds of his warriors.

**5.6.9**  And Vidura will further your message and will sow discord among Bhīṣma, Droṇa, and Kṛpa.

**5.6.10**  And when the counsellors are divided and the warriors turned away, it will be hard work for them to reunite those forces.

**5.6.11**  In that interval the sons of Pṛthā, with undivided attention and ease, will accomplish their military tasks and the gathering of resources.

**5.6.12**  And while their own ranks are divided and you delay, they will not be able to carry out their military preparations with the same effect — of this there is no doubt.

**5.6.13**  This is the chief purpose to be gained here; and through the combined effect, Dhṛtarāṣṭra may even act on your righteous counsel.

**5.6.14**  Therefore let you, devoted to righteousness, deal righteously with those compassionate ones, recounting to them the sufferings of the Pāṇḍavas.

**5.6.15**  Speaking of the family duty as observed by the ancestors to those elders, you will divide their minds — of this I have no doubt.

**5.6.16**  And you have no reason to fear them: you are a brahmin, learned in the Vedas, skilled in the office of envoy, and an elder besides.

**5.6.17**  Therefore let you depart swiftly to the Kauravas under the auspicious lunar asterism Puṣya and in a victorious moment, for the accomplishment of the Kauteya's purpose.

**5.6.18**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Thus instructed by the great-souled Drupada, the priest, a man of excellent conduct, set out for the city called the Abode of the Serpent.

**5.7.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: When Kṛṣṇa the Mādhava had departed for Dvāravatī along with Baladeva and all the Vṛṣṇis and Andhakas and hundreds of Bhojas as well —

**5.7.2**  the king, son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, had his spies and secret agents bring him full intelligence of all that the Pāṇḍavas were doing.

**5.7.3**  Having heard that the Mādhava had departed with fine horses matchless as the wind, Duryodhana went with a not-too-large force to the city of Dvārakā.

**5.7.4**  On that very same day, Dhanaṃjaya the son of Kuntī, the delight of the Pāṇḍus, swiftly went to the beautiful city of Ānarta.

**5.7.5**  Those two tiger-like men, both delights of the Kurus, went to Dvārakā and found Kṛṣṇa asleep; and they drew near as he lay resting.

**5.7.6**  Then, while Govinda lay sleeping, Suyodhana entered and sat down on a splendid seat at Kṛṣṇa's head.

**5.7.7**  Then the diademed one, that high-minded hero, entered after him and stood humbly at Kṛṣṇa's feet, hands joined in reverence.

**5.7.8**  When the Vārṣṇeya awoke, he saw the diademed Arjuna before him; having welcomed them both fittingly and honoured them as they deserved, the Slayer of Madhu asked the reason for their coming.

**5.7.9**  Then Duryodhana, speaking as if with a laugh, said to Kṛṣṇa: In this conflict, O noble one, it is fitting that you render assistance to me.

**5.7.10**  For your friendship with me and with Arjuna is equal, and likewise our kinship with you is equal on both sides, O Mādhava.

**5.7.11**  And I have come to you first this day, O Slayer of Madhu; the good honour the one who comes first, favouring those who arrive before others.

**5.7.12**  And you are today the most honoured among the righteous in the world, O Janārdana, always held in high esteem — uphold then the conduct of the good.

**5.7.13**  Kṛṣṇa said: That you came first, of this I have no doubt; but it was Pārtha Dhanaṃjaya whom I saw first upon waking, O king.

**5.7.14**  On account of your having come first, and likewise on account of his having been seen first, I shall render assistance to both of you, Suyodhana.

**5.7.15**  But tradition holds that the younger should be offered first choice; therefore it is Pārtha Dhanaṃjaya who deserves to be offered first selection.

**5.7.16**  A great host of ten crores of cowherds equal to me in bodily strength, known by the name of Nārāyaṇas — all of them battle-fighters —

**5.7.17**  let those warriors, formidable in battle, be the soldiers of one party; while on the other side stands I alone, not fighting in the battle, my weapons laid aside.

**5.7.18**  O Pārtha, whichever of these two alternatives seems more pleasing to your heart, choose that first; for by right you are to be offered the first choice.

**5.7.19**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Thus addressed by Kṛṣṇa, Dhanaṃjaya the son of Kuntī chose Keśava — the one who would not fight in the battle.

**5.7.20**  O Bhārata, obtaining a thousand thousands of warriors and knowing that Kṛṣṇa was thereby taken away from him, Duryodhana reached the highest joy.

**5.7.21**  Then king Duryodhana, taking all that army, went thereafter to Rauhiṇeya of tremendous might and fearsome strength.

**5.7.22**  He laid before him the full reason for his coming, and then the Śauri, the descendant of Śūra, replied to the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra with these words.

**5.7.23**  O tiger among men, all that I said formerly at the wedding ceremony of Virāṭa's daughter — all of it ought to be known to you.

**5.7.24**  Though the Hṛṣīkeśa was restrained and addressed on your behalf, O delight of the Kurus, by me saying again and again 'our kinship is equal,' O king —

**5.7.25**  yet Keśava did not accept those words when spoken; and I do not presume to stand apart from Kṛṣṇa even for a moment.

**5.7.26**  I shall not be the ally of the Pārthas, nor indeed of Duryodhana — this is my firm resolve, having weighed Vāsudeva's decision.

**5.7.27**  You are born in the Bhārata lineage, honoured by all kings; go, fight in accordance with the warrior's dharma, O bull of the Bharatas.

**5.7.28**  Thus addressed, Duryodhana then embraced the wielder of the plough; and knowing that Kṛṣṇa had been secured away from the battle, he reckoned the victory already won.

**5.7.29**  The king, son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, then approached Kṛtavarman, and Kṛtavarman gave him at that time a full akṣauhiṇī division of the army.

**5.7.30**  Escorted by that formidable combined army, the delight of the Kurus returned joyfully, gladdening the hearts of his friends.

**5.7.31**  When Duryodhana had gone, Kṛṣṇa then spoke to the diademed Arjuna: I who shall not fight in the battle — on what reasoning have you chosen me?

**5.7.32**  Arjuna said: You are capable of slaying all of them — of this there is no doubt; and I too am capable of slaying them alone, O highest of men.

**5.7.33**  But you are celebrated in the world, and that glory will go to you; and I too am a seeker of fame — therefore you have been chosen by me.

**5.7.34**  And it has always been my heart's desire that you should act as my charioteer — that long-cherished wish, O noble one, it is fitting that you fulfil.

**5.7.35**  Vāsudeva said: This is fitting, O Pārtha, that you would vie with me in this way; I shall be your charioteer — let your desire be fulfilled.

**5.7.36**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Thus gladdened, Pārtha, accompanied by Kṛṣṇa and escorted by the foremost of the Dāśārhas, then returned to Yudhiṣṭhira.

**5.8.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Having heard the messengers, Śalya, surrounded by a great army, advanced toward the Pāṇḍavas, O king, together with his sons, those great warriors.

**5.8.2**  His encampment extended for about one and a half yojanas, for that bull among men commanded so large an army.

**5.8.3**  The warriors wore varied armor and bore varied banners and bows; all were adorned with varied ornaments and rode in varied chariots and vehicles.

**5.8.4**  Heroes clad in the dress and ornaments of their own countries, in hundreds of thousands, became the leaders of his army — those bulls among kshatriyas.

**5.8.5**  As if distressing all creatures and making the earth tremble, he slowly rested his army and marched toward where the Pāṇḍava was.

**5.8.6**  Then Duryodhana, hearing that the great warrior with his great army was approaching, ran out and honoured him in person, O Bharata.

**5.8.7**  Duryodhana caused to be built for his honour rest-houses adorned with jewels and well decorated, in pleasant spots along the way.

**5.8.8**  Reaching those rest-houses, honoured by Duryodhana's ministers in each locality as befitted him — like a god — he came at last to another hall that shone like a divine abode.

**5.8.9**  There, surrounded by auspicious pleasures beyond human measure, he thought himself superior to all and held even Indra the destroyer of cities in contempt.

**5.8.10**  Delighted, that bull among kshatriyas then asked his attendants: 'Who are the men of Yudhiṣṭhira who built these halls? Let the builders be brought here; they deserve gifts from me, I think.'

**5.8.11**  There Duryodhana, who had kept himself hidden, revealed himself to his maternal uncle. On seeing him, the king of Madra understood the effort he had made, and embracing him with affection, said: 'Excellent! Choose any boon you wish.'

**5.8.12**  Duryodhana said: Be true to your word, O auspicious one, and grant me this boon: you should become the commander-in-chief of all my armies.

**5.8.13**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Śalya replied 'Done!' and said, 'What else shall I do?' And the son of Gāndhārī replied again and again, 'It is done, it is done!'

**5.8.14**  Having thus taken leave of Śalya, he returned to his own city. Śalya went to the sons of Kuntī to inform them of this event.

**5.8.15**  Going to Upaplavya and entering the encampment, Śalya saw all the Pāṇḍavas assembled there.

**5.8.16**  Meeting with the sons of Pāṇḍu, the mighty-armed Śalya received water for his feet, the honour-gift, and the cow, all according to proper rite.

**5.8.17**  Then the king of Madra, that destroyer of foes, filled with the greatest affection, first exchanged words of welcome and embraced Yudhiṣṭhira.

**5.8.18**  Likewise he greeted the joyful Bhīma and Arjuna, and both of his sister's sons, the twins. Then, seated on a seat, Śalya spoke to the son of Pṛthā.

**5.8.19**  Are you well, O tiger among kings, O joy of the Kurus? By good fortune you are freed from your forest exile, O foremost among the victorious!

**5.8.20**  A very difficult thing has been accomplished, O king — dwelling in the uninhabited forest together with your brothers and with this Kṛṣṇa (Draupadī), O lord of kings.

**5.8.21**  The terrible year of incognito living was also endured with difficulty. Where indeed is happiness for one bereft of his kingdom? Only suffering, O Bharata.

**5.8.22**  From this great sorrow caused by the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, you shall attain happiness, O king, having slain your enemies, O scorcher of foes.

**5.8.23**  The truth of the world is known to you, O great king, O lord of men; therefore, my dear, nothing caused by greed is found in you.

**5.8.24**  Then the king told him of his encounter with Duryodhana — all the hospitable service he had received and the granting of the boon, O Bharata.

**5.8.25**  Yudhiṣṭhira said: Well done, O king! What you, O hero, promised by word to Duryodhana was done with a rejoicing heart. Yet one thing I wish to be done, may it go well with you, O lord of the earth.

**5.8.26**  You, O great king, are the equal of Vāsudeva in battle. When the single combat between Karṇa and Arjuna takes place, O foremost of kings, you should act as charioteer for Karṇa — there is no doubt about this.

**5.8.27**  In that situation Arjuna is to be protected, O king, if you wish what is dear to me. And you must also undermine the spirit of the son of the charioteer, bringing victory to our side. Though this is improper, you should do it, O uncle.

**5.8.28**  Śalya said: Hear me, O Pāṇḍava, may it go well with you! You speak of me as one who will crush the spirit of that wicked-souled son of the charioteer in battle.

**5.8.29**  I shall certainly be his charioteer in battle. For he always considers me the equal of Vāsudeva.

**5.8.30**  To him, when he desires to fight in battle, I shall certainly speak discouraging and harmful words, O tiger of the Kurus.

**5.8.31**  So that he will become robbed of his pride and robbed of his spirit, O Pāṇḍava, and easily slain — this I tell you truly.

**5.8.32**  I shall do this exactly as you have told me, my dear. And whatever else I am able to do, I shall do what is pleasing to you.

**5.8.33**  The suffering that was inflicted on you at the dice game together with Kṛṣṇa, and the harsh words spoken by the son of the charioteer —

**5.8.34**  The affliction from Jatasura, and from Kīcaka — all that was obtained by Draupadī, O greatly resplendent one, just as Damayantī suffered misfortune —

**5.8.35**  All this suffering, O hero, shall bear fruit as happiness. You must not be angry about this, for fate is more powerful.

**5.8.36**  For great souls encounter sufferings, O Yudhiṣṭhira; even by the gods have sufferings been encountered, O lord of the earth.

**5.8.37**  It is heard, O king, that Indra, together with his wife, that great soul, the king of the gods, experienced great suffering, O Bharata.

**5.9.1**  Yudhiṣṭhira said: How did Indra, together with his wife, that great soul, O king of kings, come to experience extreme and terrible suffering? I wish to know this.

**5.9.2**  Śalya said: Hear, O king, this ancient history of former times — how Indra together with his wife came to experience suffering, O Bharata.

**5.9.3**  Tvaṣṭṛ was a lord of creatures (Prajāpati), the foremost of the gods, of great austerity. He created a son named Triśiras (Three-headed) out of enmity toward Indra.

**5.9.4**  He, Viśvarūpa of great brilliance, coveted the station of Indra, with his three terrible faces blazing like the sun, fire, and lightning.

**5.9.5**  With one mouth he studied the Vedas; with another he drank Sura (liquor); and with the third he gazed upon all the directions as if drinking them in.

**5.9.6**  This ascetic was gentle and self-controlled, devoted to duty and austerity; he performed great and severe austerities that were very hard to undertake, O subduer of foes.

**5.9.7**  Seeing the power of his asceticism and the might of one of immeasurable brilliance, Śakra fell into anxiety, thinking: 'May this one not become Indra.'

**5.9.8**  'How can he be made to attach himself to pleasures and not perform great austerity? As Triśiras grows, he might devour all three worlds.'

**5.9.9**  Thinking in many ways, that wise one, O bull of the Bharatas, commanded the apsaras (celestial nymphs) to go and tempt the son of Tvaṣṭṛ.

**5.9.10**  'So that Triśiras may be deeply attached to the pleasures of desire, quickly do this, go forth, seduce him — do not delay!'

**5.9.11**  'Adorned in amorous dress, O fair-hipped ones, equipped with enchanting ways, seduce him — may it go well with you! Dispel my fear.'

**5.9.12**  'For I perceive that I am not at ease within myself, O lovely women. Quickly destroy this great and terrible fear, O you gentle ones.'

**5.9.13**  The apsaras said: We shall make such effort to seduce him, O Śakra, that you will no longer feel fear from him, O destroyer of Bala.

**5.9.14**  That treasure of austerity who sits as if burning everything with his eyes — to seduce him, O god, we go together; we shall strive to bring him under control and to remove your fear.

**5.9.15**  Śalya said: Permitted by Indra, they went to the presence of Triśiras. There those lovely women tried to seduce him with various moods, displaying their dancing and likewise the grace of their limbs.

**5.9.16**  They roamed about delightfully, yet they did not reach him — the great ascetic, having subdued his senses, was like a full ocean (unagitated).

**5.9.17**  Having made their utmost effort, they returned to Śakra; and all of them, with palms joined, spoke then to the king of the gods.

**5.9.18**  He is not capable of being moved from his steadfastness, O lord — he is very difficult to assail. Whatever is your purpose, O greatly fortunate one, let that be done next.

**5.9.19**  Śakra, that great-minded one, honoured the apsaras and dismissed them, and then began to contemplate a means of slaying that great soul.

**5.9.20**  That hero, the powerful king of the gods, deliberating in silence, the wise one with mind made up, decided on the slaying of Triśiras.

**5.9.21**  'I shall hurl my thunderbolt at him today; he will quickly cease to be. An enemy grown powerful must not be ignored, even by the strong, even if he be weak.'

**5.9.22**  Having determined this with the reasoning of scripture and having firmly made up his mind to slay, Śakra, enraged, then hurled the thunderbolt — resembling fire, of terrible form, inspiring fear — at Triśiras.

**5.9.23**  He fell, slain, struck hard by that thunderbolt, like the peak of a mountain shattered to the surface of the earth.

**5.9.24**  But seeing him struck by the thunderbolt and lying motionless like a mountain, the lord of the gods found no comfort, being scorched by his brilliance; even slain, he blazed with radiance and appeared as if still alive.

**5.9.25**  There the lord of Śacī (Indra, Pākaśāsana) saw a carpenter at work nearby and hastily said to him: 'Quickly cut off his heads! Do my bidding.'

**5.9.26**  The carpenter said: This axe is very large — it will not succeed (in cutting through his great trunk). And I am not able to perform a deed censured by the good.

**5.9.27**  Indra said: Do not be afraid; quickly do my bidding. By my favour your tool shall become equal to the thunderbolt.

**5.9.28**  The carpenter said: Whom should I know you to be — you who would have me do this terrible deed today? I wish to hear this truly; tell me in truth.

**5.9.29**  Indra said: I am Indra, the king of the gods, O carpenter — let this be known to you. Do exactly as I have told you, O carpenter; do not deliberate.

**5.9.30**  The carpenter said: Are you not ashamed of this cruel deed, O Śakra? Having slain this son of a rishi, do you not fear the sin of brahmin-slaughter?

**5.9.31**  Indra said: Afterwards I shall undertake the very difficult dharmic practices for purification. This enemy was of great power; he has been struck down by my thunderbolt.

**5.9.32**  Even now I am troubled, O carpenter, and I fear him. Quickly cut off his heads; I shall bestow a favour upon you.

**5.9.33**  Men will give you the heads of animals as your share in sacrifices — this is your favour, O carpenter. Quickly do what pleases me.

**5.9.34**  Śalya said: Having heard that speech of the great Indra, the carpenter then cut off the heads of Triśiras with his axe.

**5.9.35**  When those heads were severed, from Triśiras as he lay came forth partridges, francolin-partridges (tittira), and sparrows (kalavinka) on all sides.

**5.9.36**  From the mouth with which he had studied the Vedas and also drunk Soma, kapiñjala birds quickly flew forth.

**5.9.37**  From the mouth with which he had gazed upon all directions as if drinking them in, O king, tittira birds flew forth from his mouth, O Pāṇḍava.

**5.9.38**  And from the mouth of Triśiras that had drunk liquor, kalaviṅka (sparrow) birds flew forth, O bull of the Bharatas.

**5.9.39**  Then, with those heads cut off, Maghavan (Indra) was free from fever and went joyfully to the three heavens; the carpenter too went to his own home.

**5.9.40**  Tvaṣṭṛ the Prajāpati, hearing that his son had been slain by Śakra, with eyes reddened by rage, spoke these words:

**5.9.41**  'Because you have slain my son — ever performing austerity, patient, self-controlled, with conquered senses, blameless —

**5.9.42**  Therefore I shall create Vṛtra for the purpose of slaying Śakra! Let the worlds behold my might and the great power of my austerity — and let that wicked-minded, evil-hearted lord of the gods behold it too!'

**5.9.43**  Then that ascetic of very great renown, enraged, sipped water for purification and offered oblations into the fire. He created the terrible Vṛtra and said: 'O enemy of Indra, grow mighty through the power of my austerity!'

**5.9.44**  He grew, propping up the sky, blazing like the sun and fire together. Rising like the sun of doom, he said: 'What shall I do?' And when told 'Kill Indra,' he set off for the highest heaven.

**5.9.45**  Then a fierce and terrible battle broke out between Vṛtra and Vāsava — both enraged — that continued without pause, O best of the Kurus.

**5.9.46**  Then the mighty Vṛtra seized the lord of gods, the hundred-sacrificed Śakra; opening wide, Vṛtra swallowed him in rage.

**5.9.47**  When Śakra had been swallowed by Vṛtra, the gods were seized with panic; those great beings then emitted the yawning power that would destroy Vṛtra.

**5.9.48**  As Vṛtra yawned and his mouth gaped open, the slayer of Bala contracted his own limbs and slipped out. From that time onward, the yawn has dwelt among living creatures in the world.

**5.9.49**  All the gods rejoiced on seeing Śakra come out. Then the terrible battle between Vṛtra and Vāsava began again, both furious, lasting a long while, O bull of the Bharatas.

**5.9.50**  When Vṛtra, empowered by the strength of Tvaṣṭṛ's austerities, grew mightier in battle, the wise Śakra then turned back.

**5.9.51**  When he retreated, the gods fell into deep despair. Coming together with Śakra, bewildered by the power of Tvaṣṭṛ, they all took counsel with the sages, O Bhārata.

**5.9.52**  Deliberating on what must be done, O king, and bewildered by fear, they all went in their minds to the imperishable great-souled Viṣṇu; they all gathered on the peak of Mandara, all desiring the slaying of Vṛtra.

**5.10.1**  Indra said: 'O gods, this entire imperishable world has been pervaded by Vṛtra. There is nothing comparable that could serve to oppose him.'

**5.10.2**  'Formerly I was capable; now I am not. How then can I secure your welfare? My view is that he is extremely difficult to overcome.'

**5.10.3**  'He is radiant, great-souled, and of boundless might in battle; he could devour all three worlds together with gods, asuras, and men.'

**5.10.4**  'Therefore, O dwellers of heaven, hear this resolution of mine: let us go to the abode of Viṣṇu, meet with that great-souled one, and through counsel with him discover the means of slaying that evil being.'

**5.10.5**  Śalya said: When Maghavat had spoken thus, the gods along with the hosts of sages went to Viṣṇu, the mighty god who is refuge for the refugeless.

**5.10.6**  Tormented by fear of Vṛtra, they all addressed Viṣṇu, lord of gods: 'O lord, you have traversed the three worlds with your three strides.

**5.10.7**  'O Viṣṇu, you brought back the nectar of immortality, the daityas were slain in battle, and having bound the great daitya Bali, Śakra was made lord of the gods.

**5.10.8**  'You are the lord of all worlds; by you all this is pervaded. You, O god, are the great god, revered by all the worlds.

**5.10.9**  'Be the refuge of the gods together with Indra, O best of immortals, O slayer of asuras. This entire world is pervaded by Vṛtra.'

**5.10.10**  Viṣṇu said: 'I must certainly do what is supremely beneficial for you. Therefore I shall declare the means by which that one shall cease to exist.'

**5.10.11**  'Go — sages and gandharvas — to where that bearer of the universal form dwells; employ conciliation toward him, and thereby you shall overcome him.'

**5.10.12**  'O gods, I shall be Śakra's refuge through my own power. Becoming invisible, I shall enter the thunderbolt, that foremost of weapons, which he shall hurl.'

**5.10.13**  'Go, O best of gods, together with the sages and gandharvas; without delay, make peace between Vṛtra and Śakra.'

**5.10.14**  Śalya said: Thus addressed by the god, the sages and the thirty gods went together, placing Śakra in the lead.

**5.10.15**  Drawing near, all of them, mighty in splendour, beheld that one blazing with radiance and scorching the ten directions —

**5.10.16**  — as though swallowing the three worlds, like the sun and the moon. The gods, along with Śakra, saw Vṛtra there.

**5.10.17**  Then the sages approached Vṛtra and spoke pleasing words: 'O unconquerable one, this entire world is pervaded by your splendour.

**5.10.18**  'Yet you have not been able to conquer Vāsava, who is of vast might; and a very great time has passed here while the two of you have fought.

**5.10.19**  'All living creatures are tormented — gods, asuras, and men alike. O Vṛtra, let friendship be established between you and Śakra forever. You shall obtain happiness and the eternal worlds of Śakra.'

**5.10.20**  Having heard the words of the sages, that extremely powerful Vṛtra, the asura, bowed his head to them all and then spoke:

**5.10.21**  'You are all of great fortune, and the gandharvas as well in every respect. All that you say has been heard by me. Now hear me also, O sinless ones.

**5.10.22**  'How can there ever be peace between me and Śakra? How, O gods, can friendship come to pass between two such forces of power?'

**5.10.23**  The sages said: 'Fellowship with the good is to be sought even once; whatever is to be thereafter will be as it must be. One should not overstep fellowship with good men. Therefore fellowship with the good is always to be sought.'

**5.10.24**  'Fellowship with the good is firm and ever enduring; the wise man speaks truth in difficult situations of meaning. Fellowship with a good person is of great significance. Therefore a wise man should not seek to harm the virtuous.'

**5.10.25**  'Indra is honoured by the virtuous and is the abode of great souls; he is a speaker of truth, undepressed, a knower of righteousness, and ever resolute.'

**5.10.26**  'Let there be with him — with Śakra — an everlasting peace. Come to this trust; let your mind not be otherwise disposed.'

**5.10.27**  Śalya said: Hearing the words of the great sages, the one of great splendour addressed them: 'Certainly, O blessed ones, the ascetics are to be honoured by me.

**5.10.28**  'O gods, let all that I say be done here; and I shall do all that the foremost of twice-born have said to me.'

**5.10.29**  'Not by that which is dry, not by that which is wet, not by stone, not by wood, not by weapon, not by the thunderbolt, not by day, and not by night —

**5.10.30**  '— under these conditions may I be slain by Śakra together with the gods, O foremost of brahmins. Such a peace with Śakra pleases me forever.'

**5.10.31**  'So be it,' the sages said to him, O bull of the Bharatas. With peace thus established, Vṛtra became joyful.

**5.10.32**  But Śakra, the slayer of Bala and Vṛtra, was ever vigilant and filled with resentment, constantly thinking of means for killing Vṛtra, perpetually anxious and watching for an opening.

**5.10.33**  Once he saw that great asura at the ocean's edge at the time of twilight, that moment both beautiful and dreadful.

**5.10.34**  Then the blessed god reflected on the boon of the great-souled one: 'This is the fearful twilight — neither night nor day. And Vṛtra, this enemy who plunders everything, must certainly be killed by me.'

**5.10.35**  'If I do not slay Vṛtra today, having deceived this great asura of enormous strength and massive body, no good will come to me.'

**5.10.36**  Even as Śakra was thus deliberating and calling Viṣṇu to mind, he then saw in the ocean a mountain-like mass of foam.

**5.10.37**  'This is neither dry nor wet, nor is it a weapon as such. I shall hurl this at Vṛtra — in an instant he shall perish.'

**5.10.38**  Then along with the thunderbolt he swiftly hurled that foam at Vṛtra; and Viṣṇu, having entered that foam, destroyed Vṛtra.

**5.10.39**  With Vṛtra slain, the directions became free of darkness, an auspicious wind blew, and all creatures rejoiced.

**5.10.40**  Then the gods together with gandharvas, yakṣas, rākṣasas, and serpents, and the sages too, praised great Indra with various hymns.

**5.10.41**  Saluted by all beings, soothing all beings, his enemy slain and his heart overjoyed, Vāsava, together with the gods, honoured Viṣṇu, the foremost of the three worlds — Vāsava, who knows righteousness.

**5.10.42**  Then, after mighty Vṛtra, the terror of the gods, was slain, Śakra became overwhelmed by falsehood and utterly despondent — beset also by the guilt of the three-headed one, and by brahminicide from before.

**5.10.43**  Taking refuge at the edge of the worlds, bereft of consciousness and sense, the lord of gods could not be found, overwhelmed by his own sins; hidden away, he dwelt in the waters like a snake coiling within.

**5.10.44**  Then, with the lord of gods gone and tormented by the dread of brahminicide, the earth appeared devastated — treeless, its forests withered, rivers with broken courses, and lakes without water.

**5.10.45**  There was also a tumult among living beings caused by drought; the gods were greatly frightened, as were all the great sages.

**5.10.46**  The entire world, kingless, was overcome by calamities; and the gods became afraid, wondering: 'Who will be our king?'

**5.10.47**  In heaven, the divine sages too were bereft of their king of gods, and none among the gods turned their mind toward kingship.

**5.11.1**  Śalya said: Then all the sages and the gods, lords of the thirty, spoke: 'Let this glorious Nahuṣa be consecrated to the kingship of the gods.' They all went and said to him: 'Be our king, O lord of earth.'

**5.11.2**  Nahuṣa addressed those gods and the hosts of sages together with the ancestors, O king, seeking what was beneficial for himself:

**5.11.3**  'I am weak; I have not the strength to protect you. A king is born from strength, and strength ever resides in Śakra.'

**5.11.4**  All the gods, with the sages at their head, spoke to him again: 'Endowed with the power of our austerities, rule the kingdom in heaven.'

**5.11.5**  'Without doubt a terrible mutual fear prevails among us. Be consecrated, O lord of kings; be king in heaven.'

**5.11.6**  'You shall absorb the energy of gods, dānavas, yakṣas, sages, rākṣasas, ancestors, gandharvas, and beings that fall within range of your sight — and thus you shall become powerful.'

**5.11.7**  'Always placing righteousness foremost, be lord over all worlds. Protect the brahmin sages and the gods in heaven.'

**5.11.8**  Having obtained that most rare boon and attained the kingship of heaven, having been virtuous of soul continually, he now became enslaved to desire.

**5.11.9**  In all the divine gardens and in the groves of Nandana, on Kailāsa and the slope of the Himālayas, on Mandara, Śvetaparvata, Sahya, Mahendra, Malaya, in the oceans and in the rivers —

**5.11.10**  — surrounded by apsarases and encircled by divine maidens, Nahuṣa, now king of the gods, sported in many ways.

**5.11.11**  He listened to various divine tales delightful to the ear, to all manner of musical instruments, and to singing with sweet voices.

**5.11.12**  Viśvāvasu and Nārada, the hosts of gandharvas and apsarases, and the six seasons in bodily form attended upon the lord of gods; a fragrant, charming, cool and pleasant wind blew.

**5.11.13**  While the great-souled Nahuṣa was thus sporting, there came into his sight the beloved queen of Śakra, the goddess.

**5.11.14**  Seeing her, the corrupt-souled one said to all those in his assembly: 'Why does the divine queen of Indra not attend upon me?'

**5.11.15**  'I am Indra of the gods, and likewise lord of the worlds; let Śacī come to my dwelling swiftly today.'

**5.11.16**  Hearing this, the goddess, dejected in heart, said to Bṛhaspati: 'Protect me from Nahuṣa, O brahmin; I have come to you for refuge.'

**5.11.17**  'You address me, O brahmin, as one endowed with all auspicious marks, the beloved of the king of gods, partaker of supreme bliss,

**5.11.18**  'as one joined to a living husband, a devoted wife true to one husband. You have spoken words to me before — make those words true now.'

**5.11.19**  'Never before have you spoken anything false, O blessed lord. Therefore let what you have said to me come true, O best of twice-born.'

**5.11.20**  Then Bṛhaspati said to Indrāṇī, bewildered by fear: 'What I have said to you, O goddess, will certainly come true.

**5.11.21**  'You will see the king of gods, Indra, coming here soon. You need have no fear of Nahuṣa — this I truly tell you. I shall reunite you with Śakra before long.'

**5.11.22**  Then Nahuṣa heard that Indrāṇī had taken refuge with Bṛhaspati, son of Aṅgiras, and that king became enraged.

**5.12.1**  Śalya said: Knowing Nahuṣa to be enraged, the gods, with the sages at their head, addressed Nahuṣa, king of the gods, terrible in aspect:

**5.12.2**  'O king of gods, O lord of the world, abandon your anger. When you are enraged, the world is frightened — asuras, gandharvas, kiṃnaras, and great serpents alike.'

**5.12.3**  Abandon this anger, O virtuous one — those of your stature do not grow wrathful. She is the wife of another, that divine lady. Be appeased, O lord of the gods.

**5.12.4**  Turn your mind away from sin, from the violation of another man's wife. You are the king of the gods — may it be well with you. Rule your subjects by righteousness.

**5.12.5**  Addressed thus, he did not heed those words, for he was deluded by desire. Then the lord of the gods spoke thus to the gods, with regard to Indra:

**5.12.6**  Ahalyā, the illustrious wife of a sage, was violated of old by Indra while her husband still lived — why did you not restrain him then?

**5.12.7**  Many cruel and unrighteous deeds, many deceits, were committed by Indra in former times — why did you not restrain him then?

**5.12.8**  Let the goddess come to attend upon me — this is the highest good for her. And for you, O gods, there shall always be welfare if it is so.

**5.12.9**  The gods said: We shall bring Indrāṇī to you as you desire, O lord of heaven. Abandon this anger, O hero. Be pleased, O lord of the gods.

**5.12.10**  Śalya said: Having spoken thus, the gods, together with the seers, O Bhārata, went to Bṛhaspati to speak to Indrāṇī those inauspicious words.

**5.12.11**  We know that Indrāṇī has come to you for refuge, into your home, and that you have granted her freedom from fear, O best of the twice-born, O foremost of divine seers.

**5.12.12**  The gods, together with the gandharvas and the seers, O greatly resplendent one, entreat you — let Indrāṇī be given over to Nahuṣa.

**5.12.13**  Nahuṣa surpasses Indra and is the great-resplendent king of the gods — let this woman of beautiful hips and fair complexion choose him as her husband.

**5.12.14**  When this was said, that divine lady, weeping with a cry, shed her tears and spoke these piteous words to Bṛhaspati:

**5.12.15**  I do not desire Nahuṣa as my husband — I will wait upon that lord, my husband. I have come to you for refuge, O Brahman; save me from this great fear.

**5.12.16**  Bṛhaspati said: I would never abandon one who has sought refuge — this is my firm resolve, O Indrāṇī. You who know dharma and are devoted to dharma — I shall not forsake you, O blameless one.

**5.12.17**  I have no wish to do what ought not be done, especially being a brahmin who is learned in dharma, who is devoted to truth, and who knows the teaching of righteousness.

**5.12.18**  I shall not do this. Depart, O best of gods. And in this matter, hear what was sung of old by Brahmā:

**5.12.19**  His seed does not sprout at the time of sowing; the rain does not fall for him at the time of rain — one who surrenders a frightened refugee to the enemy finds no refuge himself when he desires protection.

**5.12.20**  He obtains his food in vain, devoid of wisdom; he falls from the heavenly realm, his efforts lost — one who surrenders a frightened refugee: the gods do not accept his oblations.

**5.12.21**  His offspring perish untimely; his ancestors always make him homeless — one who surrenders a frightened refugee: the gods together with Indra hurl the thunderbolt upon him.

**5.12.22**  Knowing this to be so, I shall not give away this Śacī, this Indrāṇī renowned in the world, the beloved queen of Śakra.

**5.12.23**  Whatever would be for her welfare and whatever would be for my welfare — let that be done, O best of gods. I shall not give up Śacī.

**5.12.24**  Śalya said: Then the gods spoke to him, the foremost among the sages of the Aṅgiras line: "How then may right policy be achieved? Deliberate upon it, O Bṛhaspati."

**5.12.25**  Bṛhaspati said: Let the auspicious lady request of Nahuṣa a little interval of time — this is indeed for the good of Indrāṇī, and likewise it shall be for our good as well.

**5.12.26**  Time is the creator of many obstacles; time will lead time onward. Nahuṣa, though arrogant and powerful by reason of the boon he received, will be brought down by time.

**5.12.27**  Śalya said: When he had spoken thus, the pleased gods addressed him: "O Brahman, what you have said is excellent and for the welfare of all the dwellers in heaven. O best of the twice-born, it is just so — and let this goddess too be persuaded."

**5.12.28**  Then all the gods, with Fire at their head, spoke to Indrāṇī calmly, desiring the welfare of the worlds:

**5.12.29**  By you this entire world, both the moving and the unmoving, is upheld. You are faithful to one husband and truthful — go now towards Nahuṣa.

**5.12.30**  Nahuṣa, who lusts for you, O goddess, will swiftly be destroyed, and Śakra shall regain his sovereignty over the gods.

**5.12.31**  Having thus resolved upon this plan for the accomplishment of the purpose, Indrāṇī, overcome with shame, went toward Nahuṣa of terrible aspect.

**5.12.32**  Seeing her, endowed with youth and beauty, Nahuṣa of wicked soul, his mind overwhelmed by desire, was greatly thrilled.

**5.13.1**  Śalya said: Then, looking at her, Nahuṣa the king of gods spoke to her: "I am Indra of all three worlds, O bright-smiling one. Accept me, O lady of beautiful hips and fair complexion, as your husband."

**5.13.2**  Addressed thus by Nahuṣa, the devoted wife, agitated with fear, trembled like a plantain tree in the wind.

**5.13.3**  Reverencing Brahmā with joined palms raised to her head, she then spoke to Nahuṣa, that king of gods of terrible aspect:

**5.13.4**  "I desire to obtain a little time from you, O lord of the gods. For it is not known whether Śakra has met some end, or where he has gone."

**5.13.5**  "Having ascertained this truth, if he is indeed not to be found, O lord, then I shall attend upon you — this I tell you truly." Addressed thus by Indrāṇī, Nahuṣa became pleased.

**5.13.6**  Nahuṣa said: So be it, O lady of beautiful hips, as you have spoken to me. And having ascertained the matter, remember that your coming to me is an obligation of truth.

**5.13.7**  Śalya said: Dismissed by Nahuṣa, that auspicious and ascetic woman departed and went to the dwelling of Bṛhaspati.

**5.13.8**  Hearing her words, the gods with Fire at their head deliberated with single-minded purpose for the sake of Śakra, O best of kings.

**5.13.9**  Meeting with Viṣṇu, the god of gods, the all-pervading one, those skilled in speech spoke to him in great distress:

**5.13.10**  "Śakra, the lord of the host of gods, is indeed overwhelmed by the sin of brahmin-slaughter. You are our refuge, O lord of gods, the firstborn, the master of the universe, who has assumed the state of Viṣṇu for the protection of all beings."

**5.13.11**  "By your valor Vṛtra was slain, and Vāsava is now beset by the guilt of brahmin-slaughter. O foremost among the hosts of gods, prescribe his liberation."

**5.13.12**  Hearing those words of the gods, Viṣṇu said: "Let Śakra worship me alone — I shall purify the wielder of the thunderbolt."

**5.13.13**  "Having worshipped me with the meritorious horse-sacrifice, the chastiser of Pāka shall once again attain the lordship over the gods, free from all fear."

**5.13.14**  "And Nahuṣa of wicked mind shall come to ruin through his own deeds. Bear with this state for a little while, O gods — remain vigilant without delay."

**5.13.15**  Hearing that auspicious, true, and nectar-like speech of Viṣṇu, all the hosts of gods, together with their teachers and with the seers, approached the place where Śakra, afflicted with fear, was hiding.

**5.13.16**  There a most grand horse-sacrifice of the great-souled Mahendra was performed for the purpose of purification — the destroyer of the sin of brahmin-slaughter, O king.

**5.13.17**  Distributing the guilt of brahmin-slaughter among trees, rivers, mountains, the earth, and women, O Yudhiṣṭhira —

**5.13.18**  — distributing it among all beings and releasing it, the lord of the gods became free of fever, purified of sin; Vāsava became himself again.

**5.13.19**  Seeing Nahuṣa, immovable in his position and by reason of the boon he had received, the slayer of Bala beheld him as one who diminishes the splendor of all beings and who is difficult to overcome.

**5.13.20**  Then the heroic lord of Śacī vanished once more, became invisible to all beings, and wandered about awaiting the right time.

**5.13.21**  When Śakra had thus disappeared, Śacī, filled with grief, lamented in great distress, crying out: "Ah, Śakra!"

**5.13.22**  "If I have given gifts, if I have offered oblations, if I have pleased my elders, if it is true that I have been faithful to one husband alone, if there is any truth in me —

**5.13.23**  — I shall worship this sacred, divine Night-goddess who moves during the northward course of the sun. May my heart's desire be fulfilled."

**5.13.24**  With ritual purity she worshipped the Night-goddess there, and through the power of her vow of devotion to her husband and through truth, she summoned the goddess Upaśruti.

**5.13.25**  "Show me the place where that king of gods abides" — thus the goddess called upon Upaśruti: "Let truth be revealed through truth."

**5.14.1**  Śalya said: Then Upaśruti, taking a beautiful form, appeared before that virtuous lady. Seeing the goddess arrived, endowed with youth and beauty —

**5.14.2**  — Indrāṇī, greatly delighted, honored her and then asked: "I wish to know who you are. Tell me, O fair-faced one."

**5.14.3**  Upaśruti said: I am Upaśruti, O goddess, come to your presence. I have obtained the vision of you and am gratified by your truthfulness.

**5.14.4**  "You are devoted to your husband and disciplined in restraint and vows. I shall show you Śakra, the god who slew Vṛtra. Follow quickly — may it be well with you; you shall see the foremost of the gods."

**5.14.5**  Śalya said: Then Indrāṇī followed behind that goddess as she set forth, crossing the divine forests and many mountains; crossing the Himavān, she reached the northern slopes.

**5.14.6**  Reaching the ocean, extending for many leagues, she came to a great island covered with diverse trees and creeping plants.

**5.14.7**  There she beheld a divine lake, surrounded by many kinds of birds, a hundred leagues wide and equally long — beautiful.

**5.14.8**  There, O Bhārata, thousands of divine lotuses of five colors bloomed in full blossom, humming with bees that sang around them.

**5.14.9**  Piercing the stalk of a lotus, she entered together with her guide; and there, hidden within the filament of the lotus, she beheld the hundred-sacrificed Śatakratu.

**5.14.10**  Seeing the lord dwelling there in a most minute form, the goddess too assumed a minute form, as did Upaśruti.

**5.14.11**  Indrāṇī praised Indra with accounts of his renowned former deeds. Being thus praised, the god Puraṃdara then spoke to Śacī:

**5.14.12**  "For what purpose have you come here, and how did you find me?" Then she proceeded to tell him all the deeds of Nahuṣa:

**5.14.13**  "Having obtained the lordship over the three worlds, filled with the intoxication of valor, that wicked-souled one, seized by arrogance, spoke to me, O Śatakratu: 'Attend upon me,' the cruel one said, and he set a time-limit upon me."

**5.14.14**  "If you do not rescue me, O mighty one, he will bring me under his power. Tormented by this, I have come to you, O Śakra. Slay Nahuṣa, that fierce, long-armed one whose resolve is evil."

**5.14.15**  "Reveal yourself, O slayer of the daityas and dānavas. Regain your splendor, O mighty one. Rule over the kingdom of the gods once more."

**5.15.1**  Śalya said: Addressed thus by Śacī, that blessed one replied again: "This is not the time for valor — Nahuṣa is the more powerful."

**5.15.2**  "And he has been strengthened by the seers through oblations to the gods and to the ancestors, O passionate one. Here I shall devise a strategy, O goddess — you must carry it out."

**5.15.3**  "This must be kept secret by you and disclosed nowhere, O auspicious one. Go and speak to Nahuṣa in private, O slender-waisted one."

**5.15.4**  "Tell him: 'Come to me drawn by a divine vehicle borne by seers, O lord of the universe — then, pleased, I shall come under your power.'"

**5.15.5**  Addressed thus by the king of gods, that wife of lotus eyes said, "So be it," and went toward Nahuṣa.

**5.15.6**  Nahuṣa then, seeing her, spoke in surprise: "Welcome to you, O lady of beautiful hips. What may I do for you, O bright-smiling one?"

**5.15.7**  "Accept me who am devoted to you, O auspicious lady. What do you desire, O wise one? Whatever needs to be done for you, O fair-waisted one, I shall do it."

**5.15.8**  "And you need have no shame before me, O beautiful-hipped one. Trust me. I swear by truth, O goddess, I shall do your bidding."

**5.15.9**  Indrāṇī said: I am awaiting the time you have set for me, O lord of the universe. After that, you shall indeed be my husband, O lord of the gods.

**5.15.10**  "And the desire I hold in my heart — hear it, O king of gods. I shall tell it to you: if you, O king, will do what is dear to me, this word spoken with affection — then I shall become subject to your will."

**5.15.11**  Indra has swift horses, elephants, and chariots as his conveyances. But I desire for you, O lord of the gods, a vehicle unprecedented here — one that belongs neither to Viṣṇu, nor to Rudra, nor to the asuras, nor to the rākṣasas.

**5.15.12**  Let the assembled sages carry you, O great king, O mighty lord, all together in a palanquin, O king — this is what pleases me.

**5.15.13**  You should not be considered equal to the asuras or the gods — you ought to surpass them all. Draw upon the splendor of all by the mere sight of your valor. No powerful being would dare to stand before you.

**5.15.14**  Śalya said: Thus addressed, Nahuṣa was filled with great delight at that time. He too spoke these words to that blameless queen of the lord of the gods.

**5.15.15**  This unprecedented vehicle you have described, O fair-complexioned one, has deeply pleased me, O goddess. I am under your command, O beautiful-faced one.

**5.15.16**  Indeed, one who makes sages serve as his bearers is no man of meager valor. I am an ascetic, powerful, the lord of the past, the future, and the present.

**5.15.17**  Were I to be angered, the world would cease to exist — in me all things are established. Gods, dānavas, gandharvas, kiṃnaras, serpents, and rākṣasas alike.

**5.15.18**  When I am enraged, all the worlds together are not sufficient to withstand me, O bright-smiling one. Whomever I behold with my eyes, I steal away their radiance.

**5.15.19**  Therefore I shall carry out your word, O goddess — of this there is no doubt. The seven sages and all the brahmarṣis shall bear me. Behold our greatness and our prosperity, O fair-complexioned one.

**5.15.20**  Having spoken thus and dismissed that lovely-faced goddess, he yoked to the celestial vehicle the sages who were devoted to their vows.

**5.15.21**  Disregarding the brāhmaṇas, drunk with power and intoxicated by the pride of the boon, acting as he willed, that wicked-souled one made those sages carry him.

**5.15.22**  Dismissed by Nahuṣa, she said to Bṛhaspati: The term Nahuṣa set for me here is nearly exhausted. Seek out Śakra quickly — have mercy on me, your devoted one.

**5.15.23**  The venerable Bṛhaspati said to her: Very well. You need not fear Nahuṣa, O goddess, evil-minded though he is.

**5.15.24**  This vilest of men shall not endure long — he has already fallen. Ignorant of righteousness, he is as good as slain by having made the great sages serve as his bearers, O auspicious one.

**5.15.25**  I shall perform a sacred rite for the destruction of this evil-minded one, and I shall find Śakra. Do not be afraid — may all be well with you.

**5.15.26**  Then the immensely radiant Bṛhaspati, kindling the fire according to ritual injunction, offered the highest oblation for the purpose of recovering the lord of the gods.

**5.15.27**  From that fire the venerable god Agni, the oblation-eater, himself assumed a wondrous female form and suddenly vanished.

**5.15.28**  Searching through the cardinal directions and intermediate quarters, the mountains and forests, the earth and the atmosphere, moving faster than thought, he returned to Bṛhaspati in the mere twinkling of an eye.

**5.15.29**  Agni said: O Bṛhaspati, I cannot find the king of the gods anywhere. Waters are all that remain — and I am not able to enter the waters at all. There is no path for me there, O brahmin. What else shall I do for you?

**5.15.30**  Śalya said: The preceptor of the gods said to him: Enter the waters, O greatly resplendent one.

**5.15.31**  Agni said: I am not capable of entering the waters — my extinction would come about therein. I have taken refuge in you. May all be well with you, O greatly resplendent one.

**5.15.32**  Fire arose from the waters, the warrior class arose from the brahmin, iron arose from stone — their all-pervading splendor is quenched in their own original sources.

**5.16.1**  Bṛhaspati said: You, O Agni, are the mouth of all the gods; you are the carrier of oblations. You dwell hidden within all beings and move about as their witness.

**5.16.2**  The wise call you one, and again they call you threefold. Were you to abandon it, this world would instantly perish, O consumer of oblations.

**5.16.3**  The learned, having paid homage to you, attain the eternal path won by their own deeds, together with their wives and sons.

**5.16.4**  You alone, O Agni, are the carrier of oblations; you alone are the highest oblation. It is you alone that men worship with sacrificial sessions and with rites at the supreme sacrifice.

**5.16.5**  Having created these three worlds, O carrier of oblations, at the appointed time you cook them once more, being kindled. You are the origin of this entire universe, O Agni, and you become its resting place once again.

**5.16.6**  They call you, O Agni, the giver of rain, and lightning too is nothing but you. It is from you that the seasons emerge and go forth to consume all beings.

**5.16.7**  All the waters are deposited in you; in you rests this entire universe. There is nothing whatsoever unknown to you in the three worlds, O purifier.

**5.16.8**  Every being seeks out its own source — enter the waters without any apprehension. I shall strengthen you with the eternal brahminical mantras.

**5.16.9**  Śalya said: Thus praised, the venerable Agni, carrier of oblations, the supremely wise one, spoke these excellent words to Bṛhaspati with gladness: I shall show you Śakra — this I tell you truly.

**5.16.10**  Fire then entered the waters — oceans, lakes, and ponds alike — and, concealed, came to that lake where the hundred-sacrificer lay hidden.

**5.16.11**  Then, searching through the lotus plants there, O bull of the Bharatas, Agni perceived the lord of the gods standing concealed within the lotus stalks.

**5.16.12**  Coming back swiftly, he reported to Bṛhaspati that the lord was abiding within the lotus filaments, his body reduced to the size of an atom.

**5.16.13**  Going there, accompanied by divine sages and gandharvas, Bṛhaspati then praised the god who had slain Bala with ancient hymns of praise.

**5.16.14**  The great asura Namuci the terrible was slain by you, O Śakra, and likewise Śambara and Bala too — both of dreadful might.

**5.16.15**  Grow in strength, O performer of a hundred sacrifices; annihilate all your enemies. Rise up, O wielder of the thunderbolt, and behold the assembled divine sages who have come.

**5.16.16**  O great Indra, the worlds were saved by you when you slew the dānavas, O mighty one. Employing the foam of waters, strengthened by the power of Viṣṇu, you formerly slew Vṛtra, O king of the gods, O lord of the universe.

**5.16.17**  You are the most excellent and the most praiseworthy among all beings. There exists no creature here equal to you. By you are all beings upheld, O Śakra; you have established the greatness of the gods.

**5.16.18**  Protect the gods and those who dwell in their worlds, O great Indra; regain your strength. Thus praised in this manner, he grew in power slowly but surely.

**5.16.19**  Resuming his own form, he became endowed with full strength. And the god addressed his teacher Bṛhaspati who stood before him.

**5.16.20**  What task remains for you all? The great asura, son of Tvaṣṭṛ, has been slain — Vṛtra of the immense body, who had sought to swallow the worlds.

**5.16.21**  Bṛhaspati said: The human king Nahuṣa, by virtue of the radiance of groups of divine sages, has attained sovereignty over the gods and is tormenting us all greatly.

**5.16.22**  Indra said: How did Nahuṣa attain the kingship of the gods, which is so difficult to obtain? By what asceticism, or with what power? What kind of valor does he possess, O Bṛhaspati?

**5.16.23**  Bṛhaspati said: The frightened gods longed for Śakra. That great position of Indra having been abandoned by you, then all the gods, the ancestors, the atharvan sages, and the assemblies of gandharvas came together.

**5.16.24**  And going there, O Śakra, they said to Nahuṣa: Be our king, the protector of the universe. Nahuṣa said to them: I am not capable. Nourish me with your asceticism and your splendor.

**5.16.25**  Thus addressed and strengthened by the gods, Nahuṣa of terrible might became king. And having attained sovereignty over the three worlds, that wicked-souled one made the ascetics serve as his bearers and traverses the realms.

**5.16.26**  He steals splendor and his gaze is poison — he is utterly dreadful. You must never behold Nahuṣa at any time. All the gods, terrified of Nahuṣa, move about in disguised forms, not revealing themselves to him.

**5.16.27**  Śalya said: While the foremost of the descendants of Aṅgiras, Bṛhaspati, was speaking thus, the world-guardian Kubera, and ancient Yama the son of Vivasvat, the god Soma, and Varuṇa came there.

**5.16.28**  They, having come together, said to the great Indra: By good fortune Vṛtra the son of Tvaṣṭṛ has been slain. By good fortune we behold you well and unharmed, and your enemy destroyed, O Śakra.

**5.16.29**  Śakra addressed them all in due order and, urging them on, said: Nahuṣa of dreadful form has become king of the gods — let your assistance be given to me in this matter.

**5.16.30**  They replied: Nahuṣa is of dreadful form and his gaze is poisonous — we fear him, O god. But if you, O king, were to defeat Nahuṣa, then we deserve a share, O Śakra.

**5.16.31**  Indra said: So be it — let the lord Varuṇa be the lord of the waters; let Yama and Kubera this day receive the great consecration together with him. Let us conquer our enemy Nahuṣa of the terrible gaze.

**5.16.32**  Then Agni too said to Śakra: Grant me a share and I shall render you assistance. Śakra said to him: There shall be your share also, O Agni — in the great sacrifice, one share shall belong jointly to Indra and Agni.

**5.16.33**  Having deliberated thus, the venerable great Indra, the chastiser of Pāka, appointed Kubera as the lord of all yakṣas and lord of all wealth.

**5.16.34**  And Śakra, the bestower of boons, duly honoring them, granted Vaivasvata lordship over the ancestors and Varuṇa lordship over the waters.

**5.17.1**  Śalya said: Now, while the wise king of the gods was contemplating a means for the destruction of Nahuṣa together with those world-guardians, the venerable ascetic Agastya appeared there.

**5.17.2**  He, after honoring the lord of the gods, said: By good fortune you are prospering — through the destruction of Viśvarūpa and by the slaying of the asura Vṛtra.

**5.17.3**  By good fortune Nahuṣa too has fallen from the kingship of the gods, O destroyer of citadels. By good fortune I see you, your enemies slain, O slayer of Bala.

**5.17.4**  Indra said: Welcome to you, O great sage. I am gratified by the sight of you. Please accept from me water for your feet, water for rinsing the mouth, a cow, and the guest-offering.

**5.17.5**  Śalya said: When that foremost of sages, that bull among brāhmaṇas, had been honored and was seated, the lord of the gods, filled with joy, asked him:

**5.17.6**  This I wish to hear recounted, O venerable one, O foremost of the twice-born: how Nahuṣa, of wicked resolve, fell from heaven.

**5.17.7**  Agastya said: Hear, O Śakra, these glad tidings — how the wicked-souled king, the ill-conducting Nahuṣa, intoxicated with power, fell from heaven.

**5.17.8**  The greatly fortunate divine sages and the spotless brahmarṣis, weary from carrying that evil-doing Nahuṣa, asked him a question in doubt, O god — O foremost among conquerors.

**5.17.9**  The brāhmaṇas who recite these mantras at the sprinkling of cows — are these authoritative for you or not, O Vāsava? Nahuṣa, his mind clouded by darkness, said to them: No, they are not.

**5.17.10**  The sages said: You are engaged in unrighteousness; you do not accept what is righteous. This is authoritative for us — it was declared of old by the great sages.

**5.17.11**  Agastya said: Then while he was disputing with the sages, O Vāsava, he, oppressed by unrighteousness, struck me on the head with his foot.

**5.17.12**  By that act he became bereft of splendor and stripped of fortune, O husband of Śacī. Then I spoke to him as he stood trembling and seized with fear:

**5.17.13**  Because you have corrupted what is sacred, which was established of old and practiced by the brahmarṣis — and because you have touched me on the head with your foot —

**5.17.14**  And because you, O fool, have made sages equivalent to brahmin priests, those who are difficult to assail, serve as your bearers — by this you are cast from heaven, your splendor extinguished.

**5.17.15**  Fall, O sinner, utterly fallen, your merit exhausted, to the surface of the earth. For ten thousand years you shall roam in the form of a great serpent, and when that time is fulfilled, you shall regain heaven.

**5.17.16**  Thus did that wicked-souled one fall from the kingship of the gods, O subduer of enemies. By good fortune we flourish, O Śakra — the thorn in the brāhmaṇas' side has been destroyed.

**5.17.17**  Return to heaven, O husband of Śacī; protect the worlds — your senses mastered, your enemies conquered, praised by the great sages.

**5.17.18**  Śalya said: Then the gods were greatly delighted, surrounded by hosts of great sages, as were the ancestors, the yakṣas, the serpents, and the rākṣasas as well.

**5.17.19**  The gandharvas and the daughters of the gods, all the hosts of apsarases, the lakes, the rivers, the mountains, and the oceans, O lord of the people —

**5.17.20**  All came forward and said: By good fortune you flourish, O slayer of enemies. The sinful Nahuṣa has been slain — by good fortune, through the wise Agastya. By good fortune the evil-behaving one has been made a serpent on the earth.

**5.18.1**  Śalya said: Then Śakra, being praised by hosts of gandharvas and apsarases, mounted Airāvata, that king among elephants, endowed with auspicious marks.

**5.18.2**  And Agni of great radiance, and the great sage Bṛhaspati, and Yama and Varuṇa, and Kubera the lord of wealth —

**5.18.3**  Śakra the slayer of Vṛtra, that mighty lord, surrounded by all the gods, and accompanied by gandharvas and apsarases, traversed the three worlds.

**5.18.4**  He, the king of the gods, the performer of a hundred sacrifices, the sovereign of the gods, being reunited with Mahendrāṇī, filled with the highest joy, governed his realm.

**5.18.5**  Then the blessed Aṅgiras appeared there and worshipped the lord of the gods with the mantras of the Atharvaveda.

**5.18.6**  Then the blessed Indra was greatly pleased and granted a boon to that Atharvāṅgiras.

**5.18.7**  "In this Veda shall arise the name Atharvāṅgirasa — for this is the illustration — and you shall receive a share of the sacrifice."

**5.18.8**  Having thus honoured Atharvāṅgiras, O great king, the lord of the gods, performer of a hundred sacrifices, then dismissed him.

**5.18.9**  Having honoured all the thirty gods and likewise the sages rich in austerity, O king, Indra, greatly rejoiced, protected the people with righteousness.

**5.18.10**  In this way Indra too had come to suffer, dwelling incognito together with his wife, desiring the slaying of his enemies.

**5.18.11**  You should not harbour anger that you have suffered in the great forest, O lord of kings, together with Draupadī and your noble brothers.
