**5.88.9**  Those boys, bereft of their father, ever pampered by me — how did they live in the great forest, unable to see their own parents?

**5.88.10**  The Pāṇḍavas, O Keśava, used to be awakened from childhood by the sounds of conches and kettledrums, by mṛdaṅgas and flutes.

**5.88.11**  Those who were always awakened at home by the trumpeting of elephants, the neighing of horses, and the rumbling of chariot wheels —

**5.88.12**  — honored by the twice-born with the blowing of conches and beating of kettledrums mingled with the sound of flutes and vīṇās and the chanting of auspicious recitations —

**5.88.13**  — honoring the twice-born with garments, jewels, and ornaments, and with the auspicious songs of the great-souled Brāhmaṇas —

**5.88.14**  — worshipped by those worthy of worship and praised by those who praise, they used to awaken on the upper floors of palaces, sleeping on beds of soft deer-skin.

**5.88.15**  Those who do not deserve such a fate, upon hearing the howling of wild beasts in the great forest, surely could not fall asleep, O Janārdana.

**5.88.16**  By the sounds of kettledrums and mṛdaṅgas, by the notes of conches, flutes, and vīṇās, and by the sweet singing of women, O Madhusūdana —

**5.88.17**  — those who were awakened by bards, māgadhas, and sūtas singing praises, how could they be awakened in the great forest by the cries of wild beasts?

**5.88.18**  He who is modest, firm in truth, self-controlled, compassionate to all beings, having conquered desire and hatred, follows the path of the righteous —

**5.88.19**  — the path of Ambarīṣa, Māndhātṛ, Yayāti, Nahuṣa, Bharata, Dilīpa, and Śibi the son of Uśīnara —

**5.88.20**  — the hard-to-bear yoke of the ancient royal sages he carries, endowed with good character and conduct, knowing dharma, and true to his vows.

**5.88.21**  He who would be fit to be king of all the three worlds, endowed with every virtue — Ajātaśatru, whose soul is dharma, with the pure lustre of refined gold —

**5.88.22**  — foremost among all the Kurus in dharma, learning, and conduct, pleasing to behold, long-armed — how does Yudhiṣṭhira fare, O Kṛṣṇa?

**5.88.23**  He whose vital force equals ten thousand elephants, who has the speed of the wind — Vṛkodara the Pāṇḍava, ever impatient, dear to his brother and ever doing what his brother loves —

**5.88.24**  — who was the slayer of Kīcaka and his kinsmen, O Madhusūdana, and the heroic slayer of those dominated by wrath — of Hiḍimba and Baka —

**5.88.25**  — Bhīma, equal to Śakra in valor, equal to Vāyu in speed, equal to Maheśvara in wrath, the foremost of strikers —

**5.88.26**  — he who, though the scorcher of enemies, having set aside his wrath, strength, and impatience, the self-mastered yet impatient Pāṇḍava, abides by his brother's command —

**5.88.27**  — that great-souled mass of splendor, flood of strength, of immeasurable might, the fearsome Bhīmasena — O Janārdana, O Vārṣṇeya, tell me about him: how does Vṛkodara fare today?

**5.88.28**  That middle Pāṇḍava of club-like arms dwells there, O Acyuta. He who is Arjuna — O Kṛṣṇa — though having but two arms, always rivals Arjuna who has the strength of a thousand arms; and even the past, O Keśava.

**5.88.29**  He who with one stroke shoots five hundred arrows — that Pāṇḍava in archery is the equal of the king Kārtavīrya.

**5.88.30**  Resembling the sun in splendor, comparable to a great sage in self-restraint, equal to the earth in forbearance, and equal to Mahendra in valor —

**5.88.31**  — the great, blazing, celebrated sovereignty of the Kurus over all kings was won by whose valor, O Madhusūdana —

**5.88.32**  — whose terrible strength of arm the Kauravas venerate — he, that Pāṇḍava of true valor, is the foremost of all chariot-warriors.

**5.88.33**  He who is the refuge of the Pāṇḍavas as Vāsava is of the gods — he is both your brother and friend — how does Dhanaṃjaya fare today?

**5.88.34**  Compassionate to all beings, restrained by modesty, knowing great weapons, gentle, tender, righteous, and dear to me —

**5.88.35**  — Sahadeva, the great archer, heroic, glorious in battle, devoted in service to his brothers, O Kṛṣṇa, skilled in dharma and artha, and youthful —

**5.88.36**  — the brothers of Sahadeva, O Madhusūdana, always honor the conduct of that great-souled one of auspicious conduct.

**5.88.37**  O Vārṣṇeya, tell me of Sahadeva, that hero who reveres his eldest brother, lord of warriors, devoted in service, my Mādrī's son.

**5.88.38**  Tender, youthful, heroic, and handsome — that Pāṇḍava is dear to all his brothers, O Kṛṣṇa, the life-breath that roams outside them.

**5.88.39**  Nakula, the versatile warrior, the great archer, of great strength — is he well, O Kṛṣṇa, that child of mine who was raised in comfort?

**5.88.40**  O great-armed one, shall I ever see Nakula again — he who is accustomed to ease, undeserving of sorrow, tender, and a great chariot-warrior?

**5.88.41**  Bereft of Nakula for the time it takes an eye to blink — even now I find no happiness, O hero; see how I merely survive.

**5.88.42**  Draupadī, most beloved by all my sons, O Janārdana, is of noble lineage, endowed with virtue, and complete in every quality.

**5.88.43**  That truth-speaking one, choosing the world of her husbands over the world of her sons, abandoned her beloved children and followed the Pāṇḍavas.

**5.88.44**  Born of great lineage, well-honored with every comfort, mistress of all blessings — how does Draupadī fare, O Acyuta?

**5.88.45**  Draupadī, attended by five heroic husbands like fires, strikers of blows, great archers — she has become a sharer in suffering.

**5.88.46**  This is the fourteenth year that I have not seen Draupadī, O subduer of foes — the truth-speaking one worn down by anguish over her sons.

**5.88.47**  Surely a person does not attain happiness through meritorious deeds, if Draupadī, who has conducted herself thus, does not enjoy imperishable happiness.

**5.88.48**  Neither Bībhatsu nor Yudhiṣṭhira nor Bhīmasena nor the twins was dear to me when I saw Kṛṣṇā (Draupadī) dragged into the assembly.

**5.88.49**  Nothing has ever been more painful to me than seeing Draupadī, who had sought shelter with her elders-in-law, who had gone near her fathers-in-law —

**5.88.50**  — dragged in by that ignoble one who followed anger and greed, while all the Kurus watched her, clad in a single garment, brought into the assembly.

**5.88.51**  There too were Dhṛtarāṣṭra, the great king Bāhlika, Kṛpa, Somadatta, and likewise the Kurus — all overcome with shame.

**5.88.52**  In that entire assembly, I honor the Kṣattṛ (Vidura); for one becomes noble by conduct, not by wealth, not by learning.

**5.88.53**  O Kṛṣṇa, the character of that Kṣattṛ — of great intellect, profound, and great-souled — stands as an ornament pervading all the worlds.

**5.88.54**  She, both grieving and rejoiced upon seeing Govinda arrived, recounted all the sorrows of every kind she had endured.

**5.88.55**  "That which was practiced by wicked kings of old, O subduer of foes — the dice game and the hunting of deer — has it brought happiness to these?"

**5.88.56**  This burns me — that Kṛṣṇā (Draupadī), in the assembly in the presence of the Kurus, was tormented by the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra as if it were no harm at all.

**5.88.57**  The banishment from the city, the exile, O scorcher of foes — I am a dwelling-place of manifold sorrows, O Janārdana; the period of incognito life, and the confinement of the young ones, O Keśava.

**5.88.58**  No affliction would be too great for me if I were with my sons, O scorcher of enemies — yet I have been deceived by Duryodhana for now fourteen years.

**5.88.59**  If meritorious deeds bear no fruit, even happiness would not arise from suffering. I never made any distinction between the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra and the Pāṇḍavas.

**5.88.60**  By that truth, O Kṛṣṇa, may I see you, the slayer of enemies, adorned with glory, freed from this battle — together with the Pāṇḍavas. They cannot be defeated, for their innate nature is such.

**5.88.61**  I would blame only my father, not myself, not Suyodhana — by whom I was given to Kuntibhoja like goods handed over by gamblers.

**5.88.62**  My grandfather gave me, a little girl playing with a ball in my hands, to Kuntibhoja as a friend gives to a friend — the great-souled one to one like him.

**5.88.63**  I, thus deceived by my father and by my fathers-in-law, O scorcher of enemies, in extreme grief — O Kṛṣṇa, what is the fruit of my life?

**5.88.64**  The voice that spoke to me at night at the birth of Savyasācī: 'Your son will conquer the earth and his fame will touch the heavens' —

**5.88.65**  — 'Having slain the Kurus in the village battle, Dhanaṃjaya, the son of Kuntī, will obtain the kingdom together with his brothers and perform three great sacrifices' —

**5.88.66**  I do not resent that voice. Homage to dharma, to the Creator, to the great Kṛṣṇa (the dark one); eternally dharma upholds all creatures.

**5.88.67**  If dharma exists, O Vārṣṇeya, then truth will prevail; and you, O Kṛṣṇa, will also bring all that to fulfillment.

**5.88.68**  O Mādhava, widowhood does not grieve me, nor the loss of wealth, nor enmity — nothing grieves me as much as being separated from my sons.

**5.88.69**  I who do not see Dhanaṃjaya, the Gāṇḍīva-bowman, the foremost among all bearers of weapons — what peace can there be for my heart?

**5.88.70**  This is the fourteenth year that I have not seen Yudhiṣṭhira, O Govinda, nor Dhanaṃjaya, nor the twins, nor that Vṛkodara.

**5.88.71**  Men perform the śrāddha rite for those whose lives are lost and gone — in truth they are as dead to me, and I am as dead to them, O Janārdana.

**5.88.72**  Tell the king, the righteous-souled Yudhiṣṭhira, O Mādhava: 'Your dharma diminishes more and more — do not, my son, act in vain.'

**5.88.73**  Shame on me, O Vāsudeva, that I live dependent on another's support — a life of homelessness is better than a livelihood wretchedly obtained.

**5.88.74**  Tell Dhanaṃjaya too, and the ever-striving Vṛkodara: the time for which a kṣatriya woman gives birth has now arrived.

**5.88.75**  If this moment passes you by, your time will slip away — you who are held in high esteem by the world will commit a most cruel act.

**5.88.76**  If you are tainted by cruelty I would forsake you for all eternity — for when the right moment has arrived, even life itself must be surrendered.

**5.88.77**  The two sons of Mādrī, ever devoted to the warrior's duty, must also be told: choose the pleasures won by valor even over life itself.

**5.88.78**  For wealth gained through valor, living by the code of the warrior, ever delights the heart of a man, O Puruṣottama.

**5.88.79**  Go and tell that best of all weapon-bearers, the heroic Pāṇḍava Arjuna, O mighty-armed one: follow the path of Draupadī.

**5.88.80**  For Bhīma and Arjuna, fully known to you, enraged like twin destroyers, could bring even the gods to their final end.

**5.88.81**  This was the insult to both of them — that Kṛṣṇā was dragged into the assembly and Duḥśāsana and Karṇa spoke harsh words to her.

**5.88.82**  Duryodhana went against the high-minded Bhīmasena before the eyes of the Kuru chiefs — he shall see the fruit of that act.

**5.88.83**  For Vṛkodara does not calm down once enmity has been taken up — Bhīma's enmity does not cool even after long ages, not until he brings his enemies to their final end, that crusher of foes.

**5.88.84**  The taking of the kingdom is not my grief, nor the defeat at dice, nor the exile of my sons — those are not the cause of my sorrow.

**5.88.85**  But that she — tall, dark, clad in a single garment — was dragged into the assembly and heard those harsh words: what could be more painful than that?

**5.88.86**  Though a virtuous woman of the finest form, ever devoted to the warrior's code, Kṛṣṇā found no protector then, though she had protectors and was a chaste wife.

**5.88.87**  You are the protector of her and my sons, O Madhusūdana, and Rāma the foremost of the strong, and Pradyumna the great warrior.

**5.88.88**  I endure such grief even now, O Puruṣottama, while Bhīma the unconquerable lives, and Vijaya the swift.

**5.88.89**  Then Śauri, the friend of the Pārthas, comforted Pṛthā his father's sister, who was overwhelmed with grief for her sons and weeping.

**5.88.90**  What woman in all the worlds is like you, O father's sister? Daughter of the king Śūra, who has entered the lineage of Ājamīḍha.

**5.88.91**  You are of the highest lineage, having passed from one illustrious family to another like a lake flowing into a lake, a sovereign lady, blessed with every auspiciousness, supremely honored by your husband.

**5.88.92**  Mother of heroes, wife of a hero, endowed with every virtue — one such as you, O greatly wise one, is fit to endure both joy and sorrow.

**5.88.93**  The Pārthas have conquered sleep and sloth, anger and exultation, hunger and thirst, cold and heat — ever heroic, they delight in well-being.

**5.88.94**  The Pārthas have abandoned common pleasures and always love the joy of heroes — those of great enterprise and great strength would not be satisfied with little.

**5.88.95**  The resolute pursue extremes; lovers of common pleasure pursue the middle — the greatest hardships and pleasures far surpassing the human.

**5.88.96**  The steadfast delight in the extremes, not in the middle — they say that reaching the extreme is bliss, and that the space between extremes is suffering.

**5.88.97**  The Pāṇḍavas salute you together with Kṛṣṇā, and report themselves well and free from illness.

**5.88.98**  You will soon see the Pāṇḍavas in full health, with all their purposes achieved, lords of all the world, their enemies slain, and surrounded by prosperity.

**5.88.99**  Thus comforted, Kuntī, overwhelmed by grief for her sons but restraining the darkness born of the unwise mind, replied to Janārdana.

**5.88.100**  Whatever may be beneficial for them, O mighty-armed Madhusūdana, whatever you think proper, O Kṛṣṇa — do precisely that.

**5.88.101**  Without transgressing dharma, without deceit, O scorcher of foes — I know your power, O Kṛṣṇa, and your truth and your lineage.

**5.88.102**  In steadfastness toward friends, in wisdom and valor alike — you alone are the dharma of our family, you are the truth, you are the great austerity.

**5.88.103**  You are the protector, you are the great brahman, in you everything is established — as you have said, so it will indeed prove true in you.

**5.88.104**  Having taken leave of her, and having circumambulated her, Govinda the mighty-armed set out toward the house of Duryodhana.

**5.89.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Having taken leave of Pṛthā and circumambulated her, Govinda, Śauri the subduer of enemies, proceeded to the house of Duryodhana.

**5.89.2**  Endowed with supreme splendor, comparable to the dwelling of Purandara — he passed through its three inner courts unhindered by the gate-keepers.

**5.89.3**  Then the greatly glorious one ascended the palace that resembled a dense mass of clouds, that rose like a mountain peak, blazing with splendor.

**5.89.4**  There he saw Dhārtarāṣṭra, the mighty-armed, seated on his throne, surrounded by thousands of kings and the Kurus.

**5.89.5**  He saw Duḥśāsana, Karṇa, and Śakuni the son of Subala, all seated near Duryodhana.

**5.89.6**  As the Dāśārha approached, the greatly glorious Dhārtarāṣṭra rose with his ministers, honoring Madhusūdana.

**5.89.7**  Meeting with Dhārtarāṣṭra and his ministers, Keśava the Vārṣṇeya came together with the kings in their proper order of precedence.

**5.89.8**  There Acyuta seated himself on a couch made of gold, excellently adorned and spread with various coverings.

**5.89.9**  Offering him a cow and madhuparka, the Kaurava then presented to Janārdana his house and his kingdom.

**5.89.10**  There all the Kurus together with the kings attended upon Govinda seated there, resplendent as the clear sun.

**5.89.11**  Then king Duryodhana invited the Vārṣṇeya, the foremost of the victorious, to dine — but Keśava did not accept with delight.

**5.89.12**  Then Duryodhana spoke to Kṛṣṇa in the royal assembly, the Kaurava addressing Karṇa first with soft and then with crafty words.

**5.89.13**  Why have you not accepted, O Janārdana, the food and drink, the robes and beds that have been brought for your sake?

**5.89.14**  You give your help to both sides, you are devoted to the good of both — you are also a relative and a beloved one of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, O Mādhava.

**5.89.15**  For you, O Govinda, know dharma and artha truly in every respect — I wish to hear the reason, O bearer of discus and mace.

**5.89.16**  Thus addressed, Govinda the great-souled replied, his voice like the roar of surging clouds, raising his broad arm at the right moment.

**5.89.17**  The lotus-eyed one spoke to the king a supremely reasoned speech — unhurried, uninterrupted, unrejected, unconfused.

**5.89.18**  Envoys eat and receive honor when their mission is accomplished — when my mission is accomplished, you with your ministers shall honor me then, O Bhārata.

**5.89.19**  Thus spoken to, Dhārtarāṣṭra replied to Janārdana: It is not right for you to behave toward us in this unseemly manner.

**5.89.20**  Whether your purpose is accomplished or not, we endeavor to honor you, O Madhusūdana, O Govinda — and we cannot refrain from it.

**5.89.21**  Nor do we know the reason, O Madhusūdana, why you, O Puruṣottama, do not accept the honor done to you by us with joy.

**5.89.22**  We have no enmity with you, O Govinda, nor any quarrel — you yourself, upon reflection, ought not to say such a thing.

**5.89.23**  Thus addressed, Janārdana replied to Dhārtarāṣṭra, the Dāśārha glancing at him and his ministers with something like a smile.

**5.89.24**  I would never abandon dharma out of desire, out of passion, out of hatred, for the sake of gain, on account of argument, or out of greed — never in any way.

**5.89.25**  Food is eaten in joy or in distress — but you are not joyful toward us, O king, nor are we in distress.

**5.89.26**  Without cause, O king, you have hated the Pāṇḍavas from birth — brothers who follow after you with affection, endowed with every virtue.

**5.89.27**  And such causeless hatred of the Pārthas is not fitting — the Pāṇḍavas are established in dharma; who could rightfully say anything against them?

**5.89.28**  Whoever hates them hates me; whoever follows them follows me — know that I am one in soul with the Pāṇḍavas who walk in dharma.

**5.89.29**  One who, following desire and anger, out of delusion seeks to injure, and who hates the virtuous — they call him the vilest of men.

**5.89.30**  One who, out of delusion and greed, looks upon his kinsmen who possess noble qualities with ill will — he who has not conquered himself and not conquered his anger does not long hold on to prosperity.

**5.89.31**  But one who treats those endowed with virtue, even those not dear to his heart, with affection and makes them his own — he long abides in glory.

**5.89.32**  All this food, offered with wicked intent, is not to be eaten — only at Kṣattā's alone should one eat, so my mind is resolved.

**5.89.33**  Having spoken thus, the mighty-armed one departed from the splendid dwelling of Dhārtarāṣṭra, leaving the impassioned Duryodhana behind.

**5.89.34**  And having departed, Vāsudeva the mighty-armed, the great-souled one, proceeded to lodge at the house of the great-souled Vidura.

**5.89.35**  Droṇa, Kṛpa, Bhīṣma, Bāhlika, and the Kurus came to him, the mighty-armed one, as he stood in Vidura's house.

**5.89.36**  The Kurus, having approached, said to Madhusūdana there: We present to you, O Vārṣṇeya, our houses along with their jewels.

**5.89.37**  Madhusūdana, of great splendor, said to the Kauravas: You may all depart — all honor has been done to me.

**5.89.38**  When the Kurus had departed, Kṣattā then diligently honored the invincible Dāśārha with all desired objects.

**5.89.39**  Then Kṣattā offered many pure and excellent foods and drinks to the great-souled Keśava.

**5.89.40**  Having first satisfied the brahmins with those, Kṛṣṇa Madhusūdana also gave the finest of riches to those who knew the Vedas.

**5.89.41**  Then, together with his followers like Vāsava with the Maruts, he partook of Vidura's food — pure and excellent.

**5.90.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: When he had eaten and was at ease, Vidura said to him in the night: This coming of Keśava is not a well-considered undertaking of yours.

**5.90.2**  He transgresses artha and dharma, he is deluded and passionate, O Janārdana — he is a destroyer of honor and craver of honor, and he transgresses the counsel of elders.

**5.90.3**  He transgresses the dharmaśāstras, he is dull and evil-souled, obstinate and unyielding — Dhārtarāṣṭra is wicked and incapable of being led toward what is good, O Janārdana.

**5.90.4**  He is enslaved to desire, fancies himself wise, a betrayer of friends, suspicious of everyone, a man of no action, ungrateful, one who has abandoned dharma and is fond of falsehood.

**5.90.5**  Possessed of these and many other faults, even if spoken to by you about what is better, he will not accept it out of arrogance.

**5.90.6**  Seeing the assembled army of kings, O Madhusūdana, the foolish and undiscerning one considers his purpose already accomplished.

**5.90.7**  It is the firm conviction of that evil-minded son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra that Karṇa alone is capable of conquering the enemy — he will not resort to peace.

**5.90.8**  He places great reliance on Bhīṣma, Droṇa, Kṛpa, Karṇa, the son of Droṇa, and Jayadratha; his mind is not inclined toward peace.

**5.90.9**  O Janārdana, it is the settled conviction of the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, along with Karṇa, that the Pāṇḍavas are incapable of standing before Bhīṣma, Droṇa, and Kṛpa.

**5.90.10**  And the understanding of all the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, O Keśava, regarding you who strive for peace and desire fraternal harmony —

**5.90.11**  — is that 'We need not return to the Pāṇḍavas what is rightfully theirs.' To them your words will be fruitless.

**5.90.12**  Where a word well spoken and a word ill spoken are treated alike, O Madhusūdana, a wise man should not speak there — like a singer performing before the deaf.

**5.90.13**  O Mādhava, you would not do right in speaking among those who are ignorant, foolish, and devoid of restraint — like a brahmin addressing outcastes.

**5.90.14**  This one, resting on his strength and being foolish, will not follow your words; whatever you say to him will turn out to be fruitless.

**5.90.15**  O Kṛṣṇa, I do not like your entering in the midst of all those assembled there, whose minds are wicked.

**5.90.16**  O Kṛṣṇa, I do not like your speaking contrary words in the midst of those many evil-minded, ill-mannered, and wicked-hearted ones.

**5.90.17**  Arrogant because he has not attended upon his elders, intoxicated by prosperity, proud of his youth, and full of impatience — he will not accept what is good from you.

**5.90.18**  Even if you speak of his strength as strong, O Mādhava, his great suspicion of you will prevent him from heeding your words.

**5.90.19**  'This cannot be taken from us today in battle even by Indra together with the immortals' — thus are all the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra resolved, O Janārdana.

**5.90.20**  Since they are thus disposed, being led by desire and anger, even your capable speech will prove incapable among them.

**5.90.21**  Standing in the midst of his elephant divisions, of his forces equipped with chariots and horses, the fool Duryodhana — devoid of real ire — thinks: 'All this earth has been conquered by me.'

**5.90.22**  The son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra hopes for sovereign rule of the earth without a rival; in him peace alone is not to be found — he considers what he seeks as already bound and delivered.

**5.90.23**  This earth, ripened by time, is ready to be overturned; all warriors of the earth, eager to fight for Duryodhana's sake against the Pāṇḍavas, have gathered, and kings along with their protectors.

**5.90.24**  All these kings who formerly had enmity with you, O Kṛṣṇa, and who had their essence taken away by you — out of dread of you they have joined the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra; those heroes are firmly united together with Karṇa.

**5.90.25**  All those warriors, who have abandoned themselves along with Duryodhana and are set loose to fight the Pāṇḍavas — O hero of the Dāśārha clan, it is not my wish that you enter into their midst.

**5.90.26**  How could you, O crusher of enemies, go into the middle of those many enemies of wicked minds who are assembled there?

**5.90.27**  O great-armed one, you are difficult to overcome in every way even by the gods — I know your power, your valor, and your intelligence, O slayer of enemies.

**5.90.28**  The affection I have for the Pāṇḍavas is even greater for you, O Mādhava; it is out of love, high regard, and friendship that I speak thus.

**5.91.1**  The Blessed Lord said: As a man of great wisdom would speak, as a discerning man would speak, as a friend like yourself should address a friend like me —

**5.91.2**  — so indeed, as befits you, words endowed with righteousness and purpose, truthful — thus have you addressed me, like a father or mother.

**5.91.3**  It is true, fitting, and appropriate — just as you have told me. Now listen attentively, O Vidura, to the reason for my coming; be attentive.

**5.91.4**  O Kṣattā, knowing all of this — the wickedness of the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra and the hostility of the Kṣatriyas — I have come today to the Kauravas.

**5.91.5**  One who could free the entire earth, with its horses, chariots, and elephants, from the noose of death would attain the highest merit.

**5.91.6**  A person who strives to the best of his ability for a righteous deed, even if he fails to accomplish it, still attains that merit — of this I have no doubt.

**5.91.7**  One who contemplates evil in the mind but does not delight in carrying it out by deed does not reap its fruit — thus do those who know righteousness understand.

**5.91.8**  So I shall strive, O Kṣattā, without guile, to bring about peace for the Kurus and the Sṛñjayas who will perish in battle.

**5.91.9**  This terrible calamity has arisen among the Kurus themselves, created by Karṇa and Duryodhana — all others simply follow in their wake.

**5.91.10**  One who does not approach a friend being afflicted by calamities to pacify him according to his ability — the wise know such a person to be cruel.

**5.91.11**  One who restrains a friend from improper action up to the point of seizing him by the hair, having made effort to the best of his ability, is not reproachable by anyone.

**5.91.12**  That capable, auspicious word, endowed with righteousness and purpose and beneficial — the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra along with his ministers ought to receive it.

**5.91.13**  For what is beneficial to the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, to the Pāṇḍavas, and likewise to the Kṣatriyas of the earth — for that I shall strive without guile.

**5.91.14**  Even if Duryodhana suspects me while I am striving for his benefit, still my heart will have joy and I shall be free of obligation.

**5.91.15**  For when kinsmen are divided among themselves, a friend who does not step forward with every effort to act as mediator — the wise do not consider that to be a true friend.

**5.91.16**  Let not those ignorant of righteousness, those foolish, those who are no friends say of me: 'Kṛṣṇa, though capable, did not restrain the Kurus and Pāṇḍavas when they were in fury.'

**5.91.17**  Rather, let it be said that I came serving the interests of both sides, and having made effort there, I may depart free of blame among men.

**5.91.18**  For if the foolish one, having heard my word that is endowed with righteousness and purpose and free from harm, does not accept it — he will come under the power of fate.

**5.91.19**  Without abandoning the cause of the Pāṇḍavas, if I were to bring about peace for the Kurus as is proper, my conduct would be meritorious and of great purpose, and the Kurus would be freed from the noose of death.

**5.91.20**  May the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra heed my speech — poetic, delighting in righteousness, meaningful, harmless, and capable — and may the Kurus honor me who has come to them.

**5.91.21**  And all the kings assembled together are not sufficient to stand before me when I am angered — just as other animals cannot stand before a lion.

**5.91.22**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Having thus spoken these words, the bull among the Vṛṣṇis, who was the source of joy for the Yadus, lay down on a pleasant soft bed.

**5.92.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: While those two wise ones were thus conversing, that auspicious night filled with stars passed away.

**5.92.2**  Various words of that great-souled Vidura — connected with righteousness, purpose, and desire, with varied and wondrous meanings and syllables — were heard.

**5.92.3**  With tales befitting each theme, that night passed away for Kṛṣṇa of boundless splendor as if he desired no sleep.

**5.92.4**  Then many bards and panegyrists endowed with melodious voices awakened Keśava with the sounds of conches and kettledrums.

**5.92.5**  Then, having risen, Janārdana, that scion of the Dāśārha line, the bull among all the Sātvatas, performed all the necessary morning duties.

**5.92.6**  Having completed his water rites and silent prayers, having offered oblations into the fire, and having adorned himself, Mādhava then stood in reverence before the rising sun.

**5.92.7**  Then Duryodhana and Śakuni, son of Subala, approached Kṛṣṇa, the unconquered scion of the Dāśārha clan, as he stood at his twilight prayers.

**5.92.8**  They informed Kṛṣṇa that Dhṛtarāṣṭra had gone to the assembly hall, along with the Kurus headed by Bhīṣma, all the kings, and all the ruling lords.

**5.92.9**  'They desire your presence, O Govinda, as the immortals desire Śakra in heaven.' Govinda greeted them both with supremely gentle and conciliatory words.

**5.92.10**  Then, when the sun had risen clear, Janārdana, that scorcher of enemies, gave to the brahmins gold, garments, cows, and horses.

**5.92.11**  Then his charioteer approached the unconquered scion of the Dāśārha line as he stood there having distributed the jewels, and paid his respects.

**5.92.12**  Knowing him to have arrived, the great-minded one called for his divine chariot — roaring like great clouds and thunderstorms, adorned with every kind of jewel.

**5.92.13**  Janārdana circumambulated the sacred fire and the brahmins, donned the Kaustubha gem, and blazed with supreme glory.

**5.92.14**  Surrounded by the Kurus and guarded by the Vṛṣṇis, Kṛṣṇa, the son of Śūra, delight of all the Yādavas, mounted his chariot.

**5.92.15**  Vidura, knower of all righteousness, best among all breathing beings, foremost among all upholders of virtue, also mounted alongside the scion of the Dāśārha line.

**5.92.16**  Then Duryodhana and Śakuni, son of Subala, followed behind that scorcher of foes in a second chariot.

**5.92.17**  Sātyaki, Kṛtavarmā, and the great chariot-warriors of the Vṛṣṇis followed behind Kṛṣṇa with chariots, horses, and elephants.

**5.92.18**  Their chariots, adorned with gold trappings and yoked to the finest steeds, gleamed beautifully as they went, resounding and magnificent.

**5.92.19**  With the dust swept and sprinkled down, the wise Kṛṣṇa, blazing with glory, proceeded along the great highway in the manner proper to royal sages, befitting the time.

**5.92.20**  As the scion of the Dāśārha line set forth, single-drumhead kettledrums were sounded, conches were blown, and various other instruments were played there.

**5.92.21**  The foremost heroes of all the world — young men of lion-like gait, scorchers of foes — proceeded surrounding the chariot of the son of Śūra.

**5.92.22**  Then others, many thousands of them, wearing varied and wondrous garments and bearing swords, lances, and weapons, marched ahead of Kṛṣṇa.

**5.92.23**  More than a hundred elephants and thousands of the finest horses followed behind that hero, the unconquered scion of the Dāśārha line, as he advanced.

**5.92.24**  The city of the Kurus was crowded with people — elders, children, and women together — who had come into the streets wishing to see Janārdana, that subduer of enemies, as he passed.

**5.92.25**  The houses too were crowded with women who had taken their places at the terraces and balconies — as if swaying under their weight.

**5.92.26**  Being honored by the Kurus, listening to various conversations, responding to each as was fitting, and gazing about, he proceeded slowly.

**5.92.27**  Then, upon reaching the assembly hall, the followers of Keśava filled all the directions with the sounds of conches and flutes.

**5.92.28**  Then that entire assembly of kings of boundless splendor trembled with joy in anticipation of Kṛṣṇa's arrival.

**5.92.29**  Then, as Kṛṣṇa drew near, the lords of men rejoiced, hearing the sound of his chariot which was like the roar of a storm cloud.

**5.92.30**  Having reached the door of the assembly hall, the bull among all the Sātvatas, the son of Śūra, descended from his chariot which was like the peak of Mount Kailāsa.

**5.92.31**  He then entered that assembly hall which resembled a mountain and a cloud, blazing as if with energy, resembling the palace of great Indra.

**5.92.32**  Taking Vidura and Sātyaki by the hand, O king, the greatly renowned one — like the sun outshining other luminaries — overwhelmed the Kurus with his radiance.

**5.92.33**  Both Karṇa and Duryodhana were in front of Vāsudeva, while the Vṛṣṇis and Kṛtavarmā were behind Kṛṣṇa.

**5.92.34**  Then Bhīṣma, Droṇa, and the others, with Dhṛtarāṣṭra at their head, all rose from their seats, honoring Janārdana.

**5.92.35**  When the scion of Dāśārha drew near, the great-minded one whose eyes are wisdom, together with Bhīṣma and Droṇa, the greatly renowned one rose to his feet.

**5.92.36**  When the great king Dhṛtarāṣṭra, lord of men, rose, those thousands of kings rose on all sides.

**5.92.37**  A seat auspicious on all sides, adorned with gold, had been prepared there for Kṛṣṇa by Dhṛtarāṣṭra's command.

**5.92.38**  Smiling, the Mādhava, righteous of soul, addressed the king, Bhīṣma and Droṇa, and the other kings each according to his seniority.

**5.92.39**  There all the kings, the lords of earth, and the Kurus honored Keśava, Janārdana, who had arrived properly in the assembly.

**5.92.40**  Standing there in the midst of the kings, that scorcher of foes, the scion of Dāśārha, conqueror of enemy cities, saw sages stationed in the sky.

**5.92.41**  Then, having looked toward those sages headed by Nārada, the scion of Dāśārha addressed Bhīṣma, son of Shantanu, quietly.

**5.92.42**  "The sages have come to witness this assembly of kings, O lord. Let them be invited in and honored greatly with seats and with due respect."

**5.92.43**  "While these have not yet been seated, it is not fitting for anyone to remain seated. Let worship be offered at once to these sages of purified souls."

**5.92.44**  Shantanu's son, seeing the sages standing at the door of the assembly, hastened and urged his attendants, saying "Bring seats!"

**5.92.45**  Then polished seats, great and wide, adorned with gems and gold, were brought from here and there.

**5.92.46**  When they had been seated there and had received their respectful offerings, O Bharata, Kṛṣṇa took his seat, and the kings took their seats each in his proper place.

**5.92.47**  Duḥśāsana gave an excellent seat to Sātyaki; Viviṃśati gave a golden stool to Kṛtavarman.

**5.92.48**  Not far from Kṛṣṇa, Karṇa and Duryodhana, both great-souled and wrathful, sat together on one seat.

**5.92.49**  Śakuni, king of Gāndhāra, guarded by the Gāndhāras, sat on his seat together with his son, O lord of men.

**5.92.50**  Vidura, the great-minded one, took his seat on a jeweled stool spread with a white antelope skin, touching the seat of the Shaura.

**5.92.51**  Beholding the scion of Dāśārha after a long time, all the kings, all the lords of earth, were not satiated in gazing at Janārdana, as if looking upon nectar.

**5.92.52**  Janārdana, resembling the blossom of the flax flower and clad in yellow garments, shone in the midst of the assembly like a jewel set in gold.

**5.92.53**  Then all fell silent, their minds fixed on Govinda; there no man anywhere spoke a single word.

**5.93.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: When all those kings had been seated and fallen silent, Kṛṣṇa of fine teeth, whose voice is like a kettledrum, began to speak.

**5.93.2**  Like a rain-cloud at summer's end, making the entire assembly hear his words, the Mādhava, looking toward Dhṛtarāṣṭra, spoke.

**5.93.3**  "I have come here to attempt, without great effort, that there might be peace between the Kurus and the Pāṇḍavas, O Bharata."

**5.93.4**  "O king, there is no other word to speak for your ultimate good — for everything that ought to be known is already known to you, O subduer of foes."

**5.93.5**  "This lineage stands today as foremost among all kings, O lord of earth — endowed with learning and good conduct, complete in all virtues."

**5.93.6**  "Compassion, fellow-feeling, tender-heartedness, harmlessness, straightforwardness, forbearance, truth — these are pre-eminent among the Kurus, O Bharata."

**5.93.7**  "In so great a family endowed with such qualities, O king, what is unseemly and improper is not fitting here — most especially on your account."

**5.93.8**  "For you are the foremost restrainer of the Kurus, O best of Kurus — of those who go astray in external and internal matters, dear king."

**5.93.9**  "Those sons of yours, O Kauravya, headed by Duryodhana, having cast dharma and artha behind them, conduct themselves cruelly."

**5.93.10**  "Ill-mannered, having abandoned all limits, their minds stolen by greed — toward their own foremost kinsmen — this you know, O bull of the Bharatas."

**5.93.11**  "This most terrible calamity that has arisen among the Kurus themselves — if ignored, O Kauravya, will destroy the earth."

**5.93.12**  "And this can be pacified, if you are willing, O Bharata — for in my view the peace here is not difficult, O bull of the Bharatas."

**5.93.13**  "Peace depends on you, O king, and on me, O lord of men. You restrain your sons, O Kauravya, and I shall restrain the others."

**5.93.14**  "For your command, O king of kings, must be carried out by your sons and kinsmen together; even the powerful ones among them would benefit from abiding by your rule."

**5.93.15**  "And what is beneficial to you, O king, is equally beneficial to the Pāṇḍavas — who strive for peace and who await my guidance."

**5.93.16**  "Seeing yourself as whole and undivided, make your arrangements, O lord of men — let the Bharatas be united and they shall be yours alone, O lord of the people."

**5.93.17**  "Abide in dharma and artha, O king, protected by the Pāṇḍavas — for beings of such stature cannot be overcome even with effort, O lord of men."

**5.93.18**  "Indeed, even Indra together with the gods could not overcome you protected by those great-souled Pāṇḍavas — how much less mere kings?"

**5.93.19**  "Where Bhīṣma and Droṇa and Kṛpa, Karṇa and Viviṃśati, Aśvatthāmā and Vikarṇa, Somadatta and Bahlika —"

**5.93.20**  "— and Saindhava, and the king of Kaliṅga, and Sudakṣiṇa of Kāmboja, and Yudhiṣṭhira, Bhīmasena, Savyasācī, and the twins as well —"

**5.93.21**  "— and the greatly resplendent Sātyaki and Yuyutsu the great chariot-warrior — what perverse soul would fight against them, O bull of the Bharatas?"

**5.93.22**  "Lordship over the world and invincibility before enemies — this you shall again attain, O slayer of foes, united with the Kurus and Pāṇḍavas."

**5.93.23**  "With you as their lord, O protector of the earth, kings who are your equals and those even greater will make alliance with you, O scorcher of foes."

**5.93.24**  "So you, guarded on all sides by sons and grandsons, by brothers and fathers, and by friends, shall be able to live in happiness."

**5.93.25**  "With these Pāṇḍavas placed before you and honored as before, you shall enjoy the entire earth without remainder, O protector of the earth."

**5.93.26**  "For united with all these Pāṇḍavas and your own men, O Bharata, you shall conquer other enemies — this is your complete self-interest."

**5.93.27**  "And you shall enjoy the earth won by those very ones, O scorcher of foes, if you come to terms with them, together with your sons and ministers, O lord of men."

**5.93.28**  "In battle, O great king, a very great destruction is seen; and with destruction on both sides, O king, what dharma do you perceive?"

**5.93.29**  "Whatever happiness you would find, O king, from the Pāṇḍavas being slain in battle, or from your own mighty sons being slain — tell me that, O bull of the Bharatas."

**5.93.30**  "For all are heroes and trained in weapons, all longing for battle — the Pāṇḍavas and your own men. Protect them from this great peril."

**5.93.31**  "May we not see all the Kurus and the Pāṇḍavas destroyed in battle, the heroes on both sides slain from their chariots by chariot-warriors."

**5.93.32**  "For the kings assembled on the earth, O best of kings, having fallen under the sway of wrath, would destroy these people."

**5.93.33**  "Save this world, O king, let these people not perish — if you return to your true nature, a remnant shall survive, O joy of the Kurus."

**5.93.34**  "Pure, generous, modest, noble, born of auspicious lineage, mutually allied as counselors — protect them, O king, from this great danger."

**5.93.35**  "May these lords of the earth meet one another auspiciously, eat and drink together, and return home each to his own house."

**5.93.36**  "Clad in fine garments and wearing garlands, having been properly honored, O bull of the Bharatas, and with wrath and enmities set aside, O scorcher of foes."

**5.93.37**  "Whatever affection was in your heart toward the Pāṇḍavas before this wasting of life's span arrived — let that same be yours now and always, O bull of the Bharatas."

**5.93.38**  "They were children deprived of their father and were raised by you yourself — protect them as is right and just, as your own sons, O bull of the Bharatas."

**5.93.39**  "For they are to be protected by you especially in times of adversity — let not your dharma and likewise your artha perish, O bull of the Bharatas."

**5.93.40**  "The Pāṇḍavas say this to you, O king, paying homage and seeking to propitiate you: 'Under your command we have endured suffering together with our followers.'"

**5.93.41**  "'These twelve years have been spent by us in the forest; and the thirteenth year — a full year unrecognized among people.'"

**5.93.42**  "'Being resolved that you would stand by the agreement as a father, we did not abandon the covenant, dear father — and the brahmanas know this of us.'"

**5.93.43**  "'Stand by that covenant with us who have stood by it, O bull of the Bharatas; though we have ever been afflicted, O king, may we obtain our share of the royal domain.'"

**5.93.44**  "'You, who are engaged rightly in dharma and artha, ought to protect us properly; looking to your greatness, we have endured many afflictions.'"

**5.93.45**  "'May you act toward us as a mother and father; greater than a teacher's conduct is that of a disciple, O Bharata.'"

**5.93.46**  "'For we who have taken to a wrong path are to be set right by a father — establish us in the right paths; abide yourself, O king, in your own way.'"

**5.93.47**  "And your sons say regarding this assembly, O bull of the Bharatas: 'Among those seated in the assembly who know dharma, what is unseemly and improper is not fitting here.'"

**5.93.48**  "Where dharma is slain by adharma, and where truth is slain by falsehood, while those present look on — those members of the assembly are themselves slain."

**5.93.49**  "Where dharma, wounded by adharma, enters an assembly, and they do not remove its dart — the members of that assembly are themselves wounded; dharma roots them out as a river uproots trees growing on its bank."

**5.93.50**  "Those who, perceiving dharma, sit silent in contemplation — they speak the truth and dharma and what is right, O bull of the Bharatas."

**5.93.51**  "What else can be said to you, O lord of men, besides 'give'? Let the lords of earth who are seated in this assembly speak — I speak truly, having fully weighed dharma and artha."

**5.93.52**  "Free these kshatriyas from the noose of death, O bull among kshatriyas; be pacified, O foremost of the Bharatas — do not follow the path of anger."

**5.93.53**  "Having given to the Pāṇḍavas their paternal share as is fitting, then enjoy the pleasures with your sons, your purpose accomplished, O scorcher of foes."

**5.93.54**  "You know Ajātaśatru — ever established in the dharma of the good; and the conduct that he shows toward you and your sons, O lord of men."

**5.93.55**  "Though he was burned and expelled, he again took refuge in you; he was banished from Indraprastha by you yourself with your sons."

**5.93.56**  "He, dwelling there, having brought all the kings under his sway, made them look to you as their head, O king, and did not transgress against you."

**5.93.57**  "Against him who was conducting himself thus, the son of Subala, wishing to seize his kingdoms and wealth and grain, deployed the supreme stratagem of deceit."

**5.93.58**  "He, having reached that condition, seeing Draupadī brought into the assembly, Yudhiṣṭhira of immeasurable soul, true to the dharma of the kshatriya, did not waver."

**5.93.59**  "I desire the good of both you and them, O Bharata — in dharma, in artha, and in happiness. Let the people not be destroyed, O king."

**5.93.60**  "Your sons, who consider what is harmful to be beneficial and what is beneficial to themselves to be harmful, who have gone too far into greed — restrain them, O lord of men."

**5.93.61**  "The Pārthas stand ready to serve you; those subduer of foes stand ready to fight. Whatever is most beneficial to you, O king — abide in that, O scorcher of foes."

**5.93.62**  All the kings honored that speech with their hearts; no one there stepped forward to utter a word in reply.

**5.94.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: When that speech had been delivered by the great-souled Keśava, all the members of the assembly sat still, their body hair bristling with awe.

**5.94.2**  "Who indeed among men would dare to speak in answer to this?" — so all the kings reflected in their minds.

**5.94.3**  And while all those kings sat thus in silence, the son of Jamadagni spoke these words in the assembly of the Kurus.

**5.94.4**  "Hear this one parable, O king — it is true, so receive it without doubt; having heard it, adopt the better course, if you consider it sound."

**5.94.5**  "There was in former times a king named Dambhodbhava, sovereign of the whole earth; he enjoyed dominion over all the land entire — so we have heard."

**5.94.6**  "That mighty warrior, rising each morning at the end of night, would sit and question the Brāhmaṇas and Kṣatriyas alike."

**5.94.7**  "'Is there anyone superior to me, or even my equal in battle — whether a Śūdra, a Vaiśya, a Kṣatriya, or even a Brāhmaṇa bearing arms?'"

**5.94.8**  "Saying this, that king roamed across the earth, intoxicated with great pride, giving no thought to anyone else."

**5.94.9**  "Brāhmaṇas who were wise and fearless on all sides repeatedly tried to restrain that king as he boasted again and again."

**5.94.10**  "Though restrained, he kept asking the twice-born more than once, arrogant and intoxicated by his prosperity; and then the Brāhmaṇas spoke to him thus."

**5.94.11**  "Those ascetics, great-souled and endowed with the vows of the Vedas, the twice-born, blazing with anger at the king who kept boasting —"

**5.94.12**  "'There are two lion-like men whose friendship spans many births; to them, O king, you shall never be equal — not ever.'"

**5.94.13**  "So spoken to, the king again asked those twice-born: 'Where are those two heroes? Where were they born? What are their deeds? Who are they?'"

**5.94.14**  "The Brāhmaṇas said: 'Nara and Nārāyaṇa — they are ascetics, so we have heard; they have descended into the world of men. Go and fight with them, O king.'"

**5.94.15**  "'It is said that those two great-souled ones, both Nara and Nārāyaṇa, are practicing dreadful and indescribable austerities on Mount Gandhamādana.'"

**5.94.16**  Rāma said: "That king, unable to bear this, mustered a great army with its six divisions and marched to where those two unconquered ones dwelt."

**5.94.17**  "Reaching that rugged and terrible mountain Gandhamādana, he followed in search of those two unconquered ascetics."

**5.94.18**  "Seeing those two foremost of men, emaciated by hunger and thirst, their veins prominent, worn thin by cold wind and sun, he approached, greeted them, and inquired after their well-being."

**5.94.19**  "Honouring him with roots, fruits, a seat, and water, the two invited the king, asking: 'What is to be done? What service may we render?'"

**5.94.20**  "Dambhodbhava said: 'I have conquered the earth with my own arms and slain all my enemies; I have come to this mountain seeking battle with you two. Grant me this hospitality — it is what I have long desired.'"

**5.94.21**  "Nara and Nārāyaṇa said: 'This hermitage is free of anger and greed, O best of kings; in this hermitage there is no room for battle, no room for weapons, no room for falsehood. Seek battle elsewhere — there are many Kṣatriyas on earth.'"

**5.94.22**  "Rāma said: Though spoken to thus, and though forgiven and soothed again and again, O Bharata, Dambhodbhava, intent on battle, challenged the two ascetics once more."

**5.94.23**  "Then Nara, taking up a handful of grass-stalks, O Kaurava, said: 'Come then and fight, O Kṣatriya who craves battle!'"

**5.94.24**  "'Take up all your weapons and array your army; I shall rid you of your faith in battle from this day forward.'"

**5.94.25**  "Dambhodbhava said: 'If you think this weapon fit to use against us, O ascetic, I will fight with even this; I have come here for battle.'"

**5.94.26**  "Rāma said: Having spoken thus, Dambhodbhava, wishing to slay the ascetic, showered arrows from every side upon him together with his army."

**5.94.27**  "As he let fly those dreadful arrows that could pierce an enemy's body, the sage made them of no account and warded them off with his grass-stalks."

**5.94.28**  "Then the unconquered one released against him a dreadful grass-stalk weapon, a weapon beyond all counter-measure — and it seemed like a wonder."

**5.94.29**  "That sage, striking at vital points by his power of illusion, pierced their eyes, their ears, and their nostrils with his grass-stalks."

**5.94.30**  "Seeing the sky turned white and filled with grass-stalks, the king fell at Nara's feet and cried: 'Let me be spared!'"

**5.94.31**  "Nara said to him: 'O king, one who seeks refuge must be granted refuge. Be devoted to the Brāhmaṇas and righteous in spirit; and do not ever act in this way again.'"

**5.94.32**  "'And do not, seized by arrogance, ever despise anyone, whether lesser or greater — that, O king, is your supreme good.'"

**5.94.33**  "'Possessed of discernment, free from greed, without ego, self-controlled, tamed, patient, gentle, and peaceful — thus protect your subjects, O king.'"

**5.94.34**  "'You are dismissed — go in peace, and do not act in this manner again. Pay heed to the well-being of the Brāhmaṇas, as we earnestly command you.'"

**5.94.35**  "Then the king, having bowed at the feet of those two great-souled ones, returned to his own city, and he earnestly cultivated righteousness."

**5.94.36**  "That deed which Nara performed in former times was also very great; and by virtue of his many qualities, Nārāyaṇa became the most excellent of all."

**5.94.37**  "Therefore, before weapons are fitted to the bow Gāṇḍīva, that best of bows, go to Dhanañjaya, O king, laying aside your pride."

**5.94.38**  "The kakudika, the shuka, the naka, the eye-terrorizer — the santana, the nartana the dreadful, and the asyamodaka — these are eight."

**5.94.39**  "Struck by these, all men go to their death; they writhe about maddened, their consciousness and wits destroyed."

**5.94.40**  "Men struck by them fall asleep, leap about, vomit, discharge urine continually, weep, and laugh uncontrollably."

**5.94.41**  "The virtues of Pārtha are beyond counting, and Janārdana surpasses even him. You yourself know Dhanañjaya, the son of Kuntī, better than I."

**5.94.42**  "Know, O great king, that those two — Nara and Nārāyaṇa — are none other than Arjuna and Keśava, those two foremost heroes, those two bulls among men."

**5.94.43**  "If you know this to be true and do not greatly distrust me, then, taking recourse to a noble resolve, make peace with the Pāṇḍavas, O Bharata."

**5.94.44**  "Or if you consider it best that there be no breach between us, then, O best of the Bharatas, make peace and do not set your mind on war."

**5.94.45**  "Your family, O best of the Kurus, is highly esteemed on earth — let it remain so. May you fare well; consider your own true interest."

**5.95.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Having heard the words of Jamadagni's son, the venerable sage Kanva also spoke these words to Duryodhana in the assembly of the Kurus.

**5.95.2**  "Brahmā, the grandfather of the worlds, is imperishable and immutable; and likewise those two lordly sages Nara and Nārāyaṇa are the same."

**5.95.3**  "Among all the Ādityas, Viṣṇu alone is eternal, unconquerable, immutable, everlasting, the lord and sovereign."

**5.95.4**  "Others die when their occasion comes — the moon and sun, the earth and water, wind and fire and space, the planets, and the multitudes of stars."

**5.95.5**  "At the dissolution of the universe they all perish, abandoning the three worlds forever, and they are created again and again."

**5.95.6**  "Others die in but a moment — humans, beasts and birds, and those born in animal wombs, and whatever other creatures are known to roam the world of living beings."

**5.95.7**  "Kings for the most part, after enjoying prosperity, go to their death at the end of their allotted life, to reap the fruits of their good and evil deeds."

**5.95.8**  "You, sir, should make peace with the son of Dharma here and now; let the Pāṇḍavas and the Kurus together protect the earth."

**5.95.9**  "Do not think only 'I am strong,' O Suyodhana; the powerful are measured against the powerful, O bull among men."

**5.95.10**  "Strength is not strength in the midst of the strong, O Kaurava; all those Pāṇḍavas are strong, their valor equal to that of the gods."

**5.95.11**  "In this connection they also cite this ancient tale — of Matali, who wished to give away his daughter and was searching for a bridegroom."

**5.95.12**  "Matali was the name of the charioteer of the king of the three worlds; he had a single daughter in his family, renowned throughout the world for her beauty."

**5.95.13**  "She was famed by the name Guṇakeśī, divine in her form, surpassing all other women in beauty and in bodily grace."

**5.95.14**  "When Matali and his wife perceived that the time for her marriage had come, O king, he fell into deep reflection, pondering with full attention."

**5.95.15**  "'How lamentable it is indeed that a daughter is born into the family of men who are of lofty character, exalted station, fame, and abundant virtue."

**5.95.16**  "'A noble daughter puts three families in jeopardy — her mother's family, her father's family, and the family into which she is given.'"

**5.95.17**  "'I have searched through both the world of gods and the world of men with the eye of my mind alone, and yet no bridegroom pleases me.'"

**5.95.18**  "'Neither the gods, nor the sons of Diti, nor the Gandharvas, nor humans — not these, nor the many sages — did any please me as a worthy match.'"

**5.95.19**  "Then, having consulted at night with his wife Sudharma, Matali resolved to journey to the realm of the Nāgas."

**5.95.20**  "'Among gods and men I see no bridegroom worthy of Guṇakeśī; such a one will surely be found among the Nāgas.'"

**5.95.21**  "Having thus taken leave of Sudharma and circumambulating her, he smelled the head of his daughter in farewell and entered the depths of the earth."

**5.96.1**  Kanva said: "Now, as Matali was traveling, he chanced to meet on the road the great sage Nārada, who was himself going to see Varuṇa."

**5.96.2**  "And Nārada said to him: 'Where are you bound, sir? Is it on your own business, or on the command of Śatakratu?'"

**5.96.3**  "Matali, thus questioned by Nārada as they traveled the road, recounted everything exactly as it was — his own business and his errand toward Varuṇa's realm."

**5.96.4**  "Then the sage said to him: 'Let us go together; I too have set forth from heaven, wishing to see the lord of the waters.'"

**5.96.5**  "'I shall show you all, revealing the depths of the earth, and having looked there together, perhaps we shall find some bridegroom who pleases us, O Matali.'"

**5.96.6**  "Then plunging into the earth, both Matali and Nārada, those two great-souled ones, beheld the guardian of the world, the lord of the waters."

**5.96.7**  "There Nārada received worship befitting a divine sage, and Matali in turn was honored with worship befitting the great Indra himself."

**5.96.8**  "Those two, their hearts gladdened, declared the purpose of their visit, and having been given leave by Varuṇa, they roamed about the realm of the Nāgas."

**5.96.9**  "Nārada, knowing all the creatures who dwell within the earth, explained everything to him who was going — leaving nothing out."

**5.96.10**  "Nārada said: 'Varuṇa has been seen by you, dear friend, surrounded by his sons and grandsons; now look upon the abode of the lord of the waters — auspicious on all sides and full of riches.'"

**5.96.11**  "'This is the greatly wise son of Varuṇa, the lord of cattle here; this one excels even him in nobility of character, conduct, and purity.'"

**5.96.12**  "'This is his beloved son Puṣkara, lotus-eyed, handsome and fair to look upon — he has been chosen as husband by the daughter of Soma.'"

**5.96.13**  "'She whom they call Jyotsnakali, a second beauty of form — she is the eldest daughter, born of the sun's own celestial cow, so it is said.'"

**5.96.14**  Behold this dwelling of Varuṇa, entirely of gold, friend of the lord of gods — having reached which, the gods attained their pleasures.

**5.96.15**  These, O Mātali, are the blazing weapons of all kinds belonging to the Daityas whose kingdoms were seized — they are still visible here.

**5.96.16**  These are said to be imperishable, O Mātali, kept in motion by the power of their influence, conquered indeed by the gods.

**5.96.17**  Here, O Mātali, were divine weapons of the Rākṣasa kind and of the Bhūta kind as well, fashioned in former times by the gods.

**5.96.18**  This fire of great flames keeps watch in Varuṇa's lake, and the discus of Viṣṇu is set whirling here by the smokeless, oblation-bearing fire.

**5.96.19**  This bow made of Gāṇḍīva wood, charged with the power to destroy the worlds, is perpetually guarded by the gods — hence it is called the Gāṇḍīva bow.

**5.96.20**  When occasion arises, this bow steadily bears whatever strength is needed, with the power of hundreds of thousands, constantly and without fail.

**5.96.21**  This, the first-born rod of punishment, created by Brahmā the knower of Brahman even for chastising those kings who befriend Rākṣasas, punishes even those who would otherwise be unchastisable.

**5.96.22**  This great royal umbrella, proclaimed by the great discus, is held aloft by the sons of Varuṇa, the king of waters, in its full magnificence.

**5.96.23**  This umbrella of the king of waters stands in its canopy-hall and pours cool water on all sides like a rain-cloud.

**5.96.24**  Water dripping from this umbrella, pure as soma, falls into darkness and becomes invisible — one cannot discern it with sight.

**5.96.25**  Many wonders of marvellous form remain to be seen here, O Mātali, yet your task stands interrupted — therefore let us go without delay.

**5.97.1**  Nārada said: This city situated at the navel of the realm of the Nāgas is known as Pātāla, frequented by Daityas and Dānavas.

**5.97.2**  All beings, stationary and moving alike, that reach here along with the waters cry out a great cry as they enter, tormented by fear.

**5.97.3**  Here an Asuric fire ever blazes, feeding on water; it knew itself to be bound and restrained in its activity.

**5.97.4**  Here the gods, after drinking the nectar and vanquishing their foes, deposited what remained; from this the waning and waxing of Soma is observed.

**5.97.5**  Here a divine horse-head rises at each sacred lunar occasion, golden in brilliance, filling the world with waters.

**5.97.6**  Because all those forms of water fall down here, this excellent city is therefore celebrated as Pātāla.

**5.97.7**  Airāvata, for the benefit of the world, draws water from here and releases cool water into the clouds, which the great Indra then rains down.

**5.97.8**  Here dwell aquatic creatures of various forms, sea monsters of many shapes, drinking the soma-bright waters, moving through the deep.

**5.97.9**  Here those who have taken refuge in the floor of Pātāla, struck by the rays of the sun, die by day, O charioteer, and come back to life at night.

**5.97.10**  Here at every rising the moon, wreathed in rays, touches the nectar and by that touch revives all embodied beings.

**5.97.11**  Here dwell those Daityas devoted to unrighteousness, bound and oppressed by Time, their glory stripped away by Vāsava (Indra).

**5.97.12**  Here the Lord of Beings, the great lord of all creatures called Bhūtapati, performed the highest austerities for the welfare of all living things.

**5.97.13**  Here great seers who have taken the vow of the cow, emaciated by self-study and Vedic recitation, who have abandoned their vital breath and won heaven, continue to dwell.

**5.97.14**  He who sleeps wherever and on whatever, eats whatever comes, and covers himself with whatever is at hand — such a one is called a follower of the cow-vow here.

**5.97.15**  Airāvata the king of elephants, Vāmana, Kumuda, and Añjana — the finest of elephants — were born in the lineage of Supratīka.

**5.97.16**  Look, O Mātali, if any one of these pleases you as a worthy match on account of his virtues — let us go and make the effort to choose him.

**5.97.17**  This egg, placed in the water, blazing as if with splendour, has not burst open nor moved from the very nature of living creatures.

**5.97.18**  I do not hear its origin or nature spoken of, and no one knows its father or mother either.

**5.97.19**  From this, it is said, a great fire will arise at the end of time and will burn up all three worlds, the moving and the unmoving, O Mātali.

**5.97.20**  Kaṇva said: Then Mātali, having heard Nārada's words, spoke: "None here pleases me — let us go elsewhere without delay."

**5.98.1**  Nārada said: This great and excellent city is known as Hiraṇyapura, the city of the Daityas and Dānavas who wander through hundreds of illusions.

**5.98.2**  Built with no small effort by Viśvakarmā, created by Maya through thought alone, it rests on the floor of Pātāla.

**5.98.3**  Here dwell mighty Dānavas of great energy, transforming themselves through thousands of illusions, heroes who were formerly given boons.

**5.98.4**  These cannot be brought under control by Śakra, nor by anyone else — not by Varuṇa, not by Yama, and not by Dhanada (Kubera).

**5.98.5**  Asuras, Kālakhañjas, those born of Viṣṇu's footstep, Nairṛtas, Yātudhānas, and those born of the Vedic knowledge of Brahmā —

**5.98.6**  — the fanged and terrible-formed, self-protecting ones who possess the power of illusion — they all dwell here protecting themselves.

**5.98.7**  The Dānavas named Nivātakavacas, battle-maddened, — as you know, even Śakra is unable to overpower them.

**5.98.8**  Many times, O Mātali, you yourself, your son Gomukha, the king of gods Śacī's lord together with his sons — all were disgraced by them.

**5.98.9**  Behold, O Mātali, mansions of gold and silver, properly fashioned by skilled workmanship and well arranged.

**5.98.10**  Some green as beryl, some radiant as coral, some white as crystal in sunlight, and some ablaze with the essence of diamonds.

**5.98.11**  Some shine like earthen structures, others appear to be of mountains; some look like rocky crags, and some resemble stars.

**5.98.12**  Some gleam with the form of the sun, some resemble blazing fire, some are variegated with nets of gems — tall and densely packed.

**5.98.13**  These cannot be fully described in terms of appearance, substance, or quality — they are perfected in every respect and endowed with all measures of excellence.

**5.98.14**  Behold the playgrounds of the Daityas, and their sleeping quarters as well — vessels set with jewels of great worth, and seats.

**5.98.15**  Behold hills resembling rain-clouds, furnished with streams of water, and trees bearing flowers and fruits of desire, moving at will.

**5.98.16**  O Mātali, might any eligible match here please you? Or if you think fit, let us go to another region of the earth.

**5.98.17**  Kaṇva said: Mātali then spoke to him who was speaking thus: "O divine sage, I must not do what is displeasing to the dwellers of heaven."

**5.98.18**  "For the gods and Dānavas are brothers in perpetual enmity — how can I approve of an alliance with the enemy's side?"

**5.98.19**  "Let us wisely go elsewhere — I cannot look upon Dānavas. Yet I know myself as one who is of a giving nature, like a lotus that offers itself."

**5.99.1**  Nārada said: This is the world of the Suparṇas, the birds that feed on serpents — in valour, in flight, and in the bearing of loads, they know no fatigue.

**5.99.2**  O charioteer, this lineage is spread by six sons of Vainateyī — Sumukha, Sunāman, Sunetra, and Suvarcas —

**5.99.3**  — and by Surūpa the king of birds, and by Subala, O Mātali — grown and multiplied in birth by the founders of Vinatā's lineage.

**5.99.4**  Hundreds of thousands of those born of royal bird descent, born in the lineage of Kaśyapa — those increasers of living beings —

**5.99.5**  — all of them endowed with prosperity, all bearing the mark of Śrīvatsa, all desiring glory, they bear great strength.

**5.99.6**  By their actions they are Kṣatriyas — pitiless, eaters of those who enjoy — and because they cause destruction of their kin, they do not attain Brahminhood.

**5.99.7**  I will tell their names in order of their distinction — listen, O Mātali; this lineage is praiseworthy, being under the protection of Viṣṇu.

**5.99.8**  Viṣṇu is the deity of these, Viṣṇu alone is their highest refuge; Viṣṇu ever dwells in their hearts, and Viṣṇu alone is ever their goal.

**5.99.9**  Suvarṇacūḍa, Nāgāśī, Dāruṇa, Caṇḍatuṇḍaka, Anala, Anila, Viśālākṣa, and Kuṇḍalī —

**5.99.10**  — Kāśyapi, Dhvajaviṣkambha, Vainateya, Vāmana, Vātavega, Diśācakṣu, Nimeṣa, and likewise Nimiṣa —

**5.99.11**  — Trivāra, Saptavāra, Vālmīki, Dvīpaka, Daityadvīpa, Sariddvīpa, Sārasa, and Padmakesara —

**5.99.12**  — Sumukha, Sukhaketu, Citrabarhis, Anagha, Meghakṛt, Kumuda, Dakṣa, Sarpānta, and Somabhojana —

**5.99.13**  — Gurubhāra, Kapota, Sūryanetra, Cirāntaka, Viṣṇudhanvā, Kumāra, Paribarha, and Hari —

**5.99.14**  — Susvara, Madhuparka, Hemavarṇa, Malaya, Mātariśvā, and the pair Niśākara and Divākara —

**5.99.15**  These sons of Garuḍa have been named by me only partially — they have been celebrated in order of their pre-eminence, fame, and vitality.

**5.99.16**  If none here pleases you, come, let us go, O Mātali — I will take you to that region where you will find what you desire.

**5.100.1**  Nārada said: This is the seventh subterranean level named Rasātala, where dwells Surabhī, the mother of cows, born of nectar.

**5.100.2**  Perpetually flowing with milk born of the earth's essence — with the essence of the six tastes she yields one unsurpassable flavour.

**5.100.3**  She who is blameless arose in ancient times from the mouth of Pitāmaha (Brahmā) as he, thoroughly satisfied with nectar, breathed out the essence.

**5.100.4**  From the stream of her milk falling upon the surface of the earth, a lake was formed — a treasury of milk, supremely pure and excellent.

**5.100.5**  Surrounding it as if by blossoming foam all around, the foremost sages called Phenapas dwell here, drinking from it.

**5.100.6**  Named Phenapas, subsisting on foam, O Mātali, they are engaged in fierce austerities — even the gods fear them.

**5.100.7**  Four other cows born of her, O Mātali, dwell in all the directions — they are known as the guardian-cows that support the quarters.

**5.100.8**  Surūpā, daughter of Surabhī, upholds the eastern quarter; Haṃsakā upholds the southern quarter and also the western.

**5.100.9**  The western, Vāruṇī quarter is upheld by the ever great-souled, all-formed Subhadrā, O Mātali.

**5.100.10**  A cow named Sarvakāmadughā upholds the northern quarter, O Mātali — the righteous one also known by the name Eilavila.

**5.100.11**  The gods together with the Asuras, using Mount Mandara as the churning rod, churned the ocean along with the milk of these cows —

**5.100.12**  — and drew forth Vāruṇī, Lakṣmī, nectar, the horse-king Uccaiḥśravas, and the jewel-gem Kaustubha, O Mātali.

**5.100.13**  To those who feed on sudhā she yields sudhā, to those who eat svadhā she yields svadhā, and to those who drink nectar she flows with milk — such is Surabhī.

**5.100.14**  Here an ancient song was sung by the dwellers of the nether world — that primordial verse which is heard and sung in the world by the wise.

**5.100.15**  Neither in the serpent world, nor in heaven, nor in the celestial chariot of Triviṣṭapa is there dwelling as pleasant as on the floor of Rasātala.

**5.101.1**  Nārada said: This city named Bhogavatī, guarded by Vāsuki, is like the foremost city of the king of gods — Amarāvatī.

**5.101.2**  This serpent Śeṣa stands here — he by whose great tapas and immense power this earth is ever upheld, he who is foremost in all the worlds.

**5.101.3**  Resembling a great white heap, adorned with ornaments of every kind, bearing a thousand heads, flame-tongued and of immense strength.

**5.101.4**  Here dwell the sons of Surasā — serpents of various forms and diverse ornaments — free from all distress.

**5.101.5**  Marked with signs of jewel, svastika, and wheel, bearing the marks of the water-vessel — thousands in number, all powerful, and fierce by nature.

**5.101.6**  Some bear a thousand heads, some five hundred mouths, some a hundred heads, and some even three heads.

**5.101.7**  Some have ten heads, some seven mouths; of vast coils and gigantic bodies, they are serpents whose coils rival mountains.

**5.101.8**  Here dwell many thousands, millions, and hundreds of millions of serpents of a single lineage — hear from me of the most eminent among them.

**5.101.9**  Vāsuki, Takṣaka, Karkoṭaka, Dhanaṃjaya, Kālīya, Nahuṣa, and both Kambala and Aśvatara —

**5.101.10**  Bāhyakuṇḍa, the serpent Maṇi, Āpūraṇa, Khaga, Vāmana, Elapatra, Kukura, and Kukuṇa —

**5.101.11**  Āryaka, Nandaka, Kalaśa and Potaka, Kailāsaka, the serpent Piñjaraka, and Airāvata —

**5.101.12**  Sumanomukha, Dadhimukha, Śaṅkha, Nanda and Upananda, Āpta, Koṭanaka, Śikhī, and Niṣṭhūrika —

**5.101.13**  Tittiri, Hastibhadra, Kumuda, Mālyapiṇḍaka, the two Padmas, Puṇḍarīka, Puṣpa, and Mudgaraparṇaka —

**5.101.14**  Karavīra, Pīṭharaka, Saṃvṛtta, Vṛtta, Piṇḍāra, Bilvapatra, Mūṣikāda, and Śirīṣaka —

**5.101.15**  Dilīpa, Śaṅkhaśīrṣa, Jyotiṣka, and Aparājita, Kauravya, Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Kumāra, and Kuśaka —

**5.101.16**  Virajā, Dhāraṇa, Subāhu, Mukhara, Jaya, Badhira, Andha, Vikuṇḍa, Virasa, and Surasā —

**5.101.17**  These and many others are remembered as the sons of Kaśyapa. Mātali, look — if any among them pleases you as a suitable choice.

**5.101.18**  Kaṇva said: Mātali, calm and intent, having gazed steadily at one of them, questioned Nārada there, appearing as one filled with affection.

**5.101.19**  "Who is this one standing before Kauravya and Āryaka — so radiant and handsome? Whose son is this, the joy of what lineage?"

**5.101.20**  "Who is his father, who his mother? Of which serpent family does he belong? Whose lineage does this great one stand as a banner?"

**5.101.21**  "By his self-possession, steadiness, beauty, and youth, O divine sage, he has entered my mind — he seems a worthy husband for Guṇakeśī."

**5.101.22**  Seeing Mātali pleased at the sight of Sumukha, Nārada then described his greatness, birth, and deeds.

**5.101.23**  "Born in the lineage of Airāvata, this serpent-prince is named Sumukha — regarded as the grandson of Āryaka and the daughter's son of Vāmana."

**5.101.24**  "The father of this serpent, named Cikura, O Mātali, was not long ago reduced to the five elements by Vainateya."

**5.101.25**  Then, with a pleased mind, Mātali said to Nārada: "This foremost of serpents is agreeable to me, dear friend, as a son-in-law."

**5.101.26**  "Let effort be made in this matter — I am delighted with him. To give this lord of serpents my beloved daughter in marriage, O sage."

**5.102.1**  Nārada said: "This charioteer named Mātali is the beloved and trusted friend of Śakra — pure, endowed with good character and virtue, resplendent, valiant, and strong."

**5.102.2**  "He is the companion, counselor, and charioteer of Śakra, hardly differing in power from Vāsava in battle after battle."

**5.102.3**  "In the wars between gods and demons, he controls by mere thought alone the victorious foremost chariot yoked to a thousand tawny horses."

**5.102.4**  "Through him, Vāsava wins with his horses and arms those who are conquered; when this one strikes first, then Balabhit strikes."

**5.102.5**  "His daughter, of noble bearing, has no equal on earth in beauty; endowed with virtue, character, and quality — she is known as Guṇakeśī."

**5.102.6**  "While this one searches the three worlds with effort, O resplendent among the immortals, your grandson Sumukha pleases him as a husband for his daughter."

**5.102.7**  "If this pleases you, O gentle one, foremost of serpents, then without delay, O Āryaka, quickly form a decision to accept the girl."

**5.102.8**  "As Lakṣmī is in Viṣṇu's lineage, as Svāhā is in the lineage of Vibhāvasu — so let Guṇakeśī of the lovely waist be in your family."

**5.102.9**  "For the sake of your grandson, therefore let your honor accept Guṇakeśī — she who is comparable in beauty to Śacī herself, fitting consort of Vāsava."

**5.102.10**  "Even though fatherless, we choose him for his qualities — out of great respect for your honor, for Airāvata, and for Sumukha's own qualities of good conduct, purity, and self-restraint."

**5.102.11**  "Having approached of his own accord, Mātali stands ready to give his daughter; you too, O Āryaka, should honor him in return."

**5.102.12**  Kaṇva said: Āryaka, both sorrowful and joyful at once, spoke to Nārada — his grandson now being sought in marriage, while his own son had gone to death.

**5.102.13**  "O divine sage, who would not desire this? Your word is highly esteemed by me — he who is connected to Śakra's own companion, who would not welcome this?"

**5.102.14**  "But because of the weakness of our situation I reflect, O great sage — the progenitor of this one, O dear, my son was devoured by Vainateya, O greatly resplendent one; we are afflicted with that grief."

**5.102.15**  "And again, as Vainateya departed, he declared: within another month I shall devour Sumukha, O lord."

**5.102.16**  "That shall certainly come to pass — we know his resolve. Thus my joy is destroyed by the words of Suparṇa."

**5.102.17**  Mātali then said to him: "My mind is resolved in this matter. Sumukha, the son of your son, has been chosen by me as a son-in-law."

**5.102.18**  "Let this serpent accompanied by me and by Nārada go to see Vāsava, the lord of the gods, the ruler of the three worlds."

**5.102.19**  "Through the remaining matter of his life I shall provide wisdom, O best of beings, and I shall strive to thwart Suparṇa."

**5.102.20**  "And let Sumukha accompany me to the lord of gods, for the accomplishment of this purpose. May all be well with you, O serpent."

**5.102.21**  Then all those mighty ones, taking Sumukha with them, beheld Śakra seated — the king of gods, of great splendor.

**5.102.22**  By chance the blessed Viṣṇu of four arms was present there; and then Nārada recounted everything to Mātali.

**5.102.23**  Then Viṣṇu spoke to Puraṃdara, lord of the worlds: "Let amṛta be given to him; let him be made equal to the immortals."

**5.102.24**  "Let Mātali, Nārada, and Sumukha obtain this wished-for boon, O Vāsava, through your grace, as each desires."

**5.102.25**  Puraṃdara then, reflecting on the prowess of Vainateya, said to Viṣṇu himself: "Let your honor bestow it."

**5.102.26**  Viṣṇu said: "You are the lord of all the worlds — of those that move and those that do not. What you give or withhold, O mighty one, who dares to undo?"

**5.102.27**  Kaṇva said: Then Śakra bestowed upon that serpent the highest span of life; yet Balavṛtrahā did not make him partake of the amṛta.

**5.102.28**  Having received the boon, Sumukha became truly Sumukha — of beautiful countenance; he took a wife as he wished and returned to his home.

**5.102.29**  Nārada and Āryaka, their purpose accomplished, filled with joy, returned after honoring the great-splendored king of gods.

**5.103.1**  Kaṇva said: Garuḍa, of immense power, heard what had transpired — the grant of long life bestowed on the serpent by Śakra, O Bhārata.

**5.103.2**  Blocking the three worlds with the mighty wind of his wings, that bird Suparṇa, supremely enraged, rushed toward Vāsava.

**5.103.3**  Garuḍa said: "O lord, out of disregard, have you given away freely a boon concerning my food, and thus moved against me while I was in need? Have you abandoned our agreement?"

**5.103.4**  "By nature, the ordainer who is lord of all beings ordained food for me — why then is it being withheld by you?"

**5.103.5**  "This great serpent was claimed, an agreement was established by me; and through this one, O god, a great multitude of dependents must be supported by me."

**5.103.6**  "With this one denied to me, I would not venture to harm another; yet you sport capriciously, O king of gods, entirely as you please."

**5.103.7**  "Thus I shall relinquish my life, and so too will my retinue and the servants in my household. Be pleased, O Vāsava."

**5.103.8**  "This and more I deserve, O slayer of Bala and Vṛtra — I who am lord of the three worlds have been reduced to servitude under another."

**5.103.9**  "With you standing here, O lord of gods, Viṣṇu is not the cause of my grievance — for the sovereignty of the three worlds, O Vāsava, rests eternally with you."

**5.103.10**  "My mother too is the daughter of Dakṣa, Kaśyapa is my father; I too am capable of bearing all the worlds with ease."

**5.103.11**  "My immense power too is unbearable by all beings; great deeds were performed by me as well in the battle against the Daityas."

**5.103.12**  "Śrutaśrī, Śrutasena, Vivasvān, Rocanāmukha, Prasabha, and Kālakākṣa — these sons of Diti were also slain by me."
