**5.139.29**  The son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra's weapon-sacrifice shall take place, O Vārṣṇeya; you shall be the knower of this sacrifice, O Janārdana; and you, O Kṛṣṇa, shall perform the role of the Adhvaryu priest in this rite.

**5.139.30**  Bībhatsu, girt for battle and bearing the monkey-banner, shall be the hotar-priest; the Gāṇḍīva shall be the sacrificial ladle, valor the clarified butter.

**5.139.31**  The Aindra, the Pāśupata, the Brahmā, and the Sthūṇākarṇa weapons, O Mādhava — these shall be the mantras deployed there by Savyasācī.

**5.139.32**  Abhimanyu, the son of Subhadrā — who follows his father or surpasses him in valor — shall properly perform the role of the Gravastotrin chanter there.

**5.139.33**  Bhīma of immense strength shall again be the Udgatar singer there, the Prostota — that tiger among men who roars and destroys armies of elephants in battle.

**5.139.34**  And that ever-righteous king Yudhiṣṭhira himself, engaged in prayer and fire-offerings, shall perform the office of the Brahmin priest there.

**5.139.35**  The sounds of conches, the roll of drums, and the kettledrums, O Madhusūdana, and the exalted lion-roars — these shall be the Subrahmania chants of this sacrifice.

**5.139.36**  Nakula and Sahadeva, the glorious sons of Mādrī, those two of great valor, shall duly perform the Shamitra rite there.

**5.139.37**  Let the sacrificial posts be prepared for this sacrifice, O Govinda, O Janārdana — the speckled-shafted, pure chariot-javelins.

**5.139.38**  Karṇa-arrows, reed shafts, iron arrows, and Vatsadanta-tipped bolts, lances, Soma-vessels, straining-cloths, and bows — let these be the implements of this sacrifice.

**5.139.39**  The swords here are the sacrificial ladles, the oblation-cakes are the severed heads, O Kṛṣṇa, and blood shall be the clarified butter-offering in this sacrifice.

**5.139.40**  The firewood and enclosing sticks are the spears and bright maces, and the priests at the session are the disciples of Droṇa and of Kṛpa, son of Sharadvan.

**5.139.41**  The arrows shot by the wielder of the Gāṇḍīva bow, and those launched by great warriors and urged on by Droṇa and Aśvatthāmā, are the sacrificial straw-bundles here.

**5.139.42**  Sātyaki shall perform the assistant's rite here; the consecrated sacrificer is the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, and his great army serves as the sacrificial wife.

**5.139.43**  The mighty Ghaṭotkaca shall perform the slaughtering rite here, O great-armed one, when the sacrifice is extended into the all-night ceremony.

**5.139.44**  The sacrificial fee of this sacrifice is the valiant Dhṛṣṭadyumna, O Kṛṣṇa — he who was born from fire when the Vedic ritual was performed.

**5.139.45**  Whatever harsh words I spoke to the Pāṇḍavas for the sake of pleasing the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra — O Kṛṣṇa, I am now burned by that deed.

**5.139.46**  When you shall see me slain, O Kṛṣṇa, by Savyasācin, then shall the re-piling of the altar fire of this sacrifice take place.

**5.139.47**  When the Pāṇḍava drinks the blood of Duḥśāsana, roaring as he roars aloud — then shall the soma-pressing day of this sacrifice arrive.

**5.139.48**  When the two sons of Pāñcāla shall lay low both Droṇa and Bhīṣma, that shall be the concluding rite of this sacrifice, O Janārdana.

**5.139.49**  When the mighty Bhīmasena shall slay Duryodhana, then the sacrifice of the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra shall be complete, O Mādhava.

**5.139.50**  The daughters-in-law and granddaughters-in-law of Dhṛtarāṣṭra assembled together, their lords slain, their sons slain, their protectors slain, O Keśava —

**5.139.51**  — weeping together with Gāndhārī amid jackals, vultures, and ospreys: that shall be the concluding bath of this sacrifice, O Janārdana.

**5.139.52**  Kṣatriyas advanced in learning and advanced in age should not be caused to die in vain on your account, O bull among kshatriyas, O Madhusūdana.

**5.139.53**  Let the full circle of the kshatriya order find death by the sword at Kurukṣetra, the holiest of all three worlds, O Keśava.

**5.139.54**  Therefore, O lotus-eyed one, O Vārṣṇeya, arrange what you desire, so that the entire kshatriya class may attain heaven.

**5.139.55**  As long as the mountains and rivers shall stand, O Janārdana, so long shall this fame-born, eternal renown endure.

**5.139.56**  Brahmins shall tell of the great Bharata battle at their assemblies, O Vārṣṇeya — that battle which upholds the glory of the kshatriyas.

**5.139.57**  Bring the son of Kuntī to battle against me, O Keśava, always keeping your counsels concealed, O scorcher of foes.

**5.140.1**  Sañjaya said: Having heard the words of Karṇa, Keśava, the slayer of enemy heroes, then spoke this speech laughing, beginning with a smile.

**5.140.2**  Does not the gaining of a kingdom burn you, O Karṇa? The earth that has been offered to you, you do not wish to rule.

**5.140.3**  The victory of the Pāṇḍavas is certain — of this there is no doubt whatsoever here; the victory-banner of the Pāṇḍava is seen raised aloft, with the fierce monkey-king upon it.

**5.140.4**  A divine illusion has been arranged by the lord of the worlds; it is raised up, blazing like Indra's banner; divine beings, terrifying and fearsome, are seen there as well.

**5.140.5**  It does not catch on mountains or forest trees; extending a yojana upward and across, the glorious banner of Dhanañjaya is raised up, O Karṇa, blazing like fire.

**5.140.6**  When in battle you shall see the white-horsed one with Kṛṣṇa as charioteer, wielding the Aindra weapon and both the fire and wind weapons —

**5.140.7**  — and hear the twanging of the Gāṇḍīva bow like the crash of a thunderbolt, then there shall be neither the Tretā age nor the Kṛta nor the Dvāpara.

**5.140.8**  When in battle you shall see Yudhiṣṭhira, son of Kuntī, engaged in muttered prayer and fire-offerings, protecting his great army —

**5.140.9**  — irresistible like the sun, scorching the enemy host, then there shall be neither the Tretā age nor the Kṛta nor the Dvāpara.

**5.140.10**  When in battle you shall see the mighty Bhīmasena drinking the blood of Duḥśāsana and dancing in the fight —

**5.140.11**  — like a rogue elephant, a slayer of rival elephants, then there shall be neither the Tretā age nor the Kṛta nor the Dvāpara.

**5.140.12**  When in battle you shall see the two great chariot-warriors, sons of Mādrī, shaking the army of the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra like two elephants —

**5.140.13**  — plunging into the thick of weapons, smashing the chariots of enemy heroes, then there shall be neither the Tretā age nor the Kṛta nor the Dvāpara.

**5.140.14**  When in battle you shall see Droṇa, Śāntanava Bhīṣma, Kṛpa, King Suyodhana, and Jayadratha the Sindhu king —

**5.140.15**  — rushing swiftly into combat, held at bay by Savyasācin, then there shall be neither the Tretā age nor the Kṛta nor the Dvāpara.

**5.140.16**  Go from here, O Karṇa, and tell Droṇa, Śāntanava, and Kṛpa: 'This mild month is at hand, well-supplied with barley, food, and fuel.'

**5.140.17**  'The forests of ripened herbs are full, there is fruit, few insects; the water is free of mud and full of flavor; the weather is neither too hot nor too cold — it is pleasant.'

**5.140.18**  'From the seventh day, the new moon will come — let battle be joined then; they say that day is under the lordship of Indra.'

**5.140.19**  'Also tell the king and all those who have come ready for war: whatever you have wished for, I shall accomplish all of it for you.'

**5.140.20**  Kings and princes who follow Duryodhana's command, attaining death by the sword, shall reach the highest state.

**5.141.1**  Sañjaya said: Having heard that beneficial and auspicious speech of Keśava, Karṇa, honoring Kṛṣṇa the slayer of Madhu, said: 'Knowing me as you do, O great-armed one, why do you wish to bewilder me?'

**5.141.2**  'This destruction of the whole earth that has now come upon us — the occasions for it were Śakuni, myself, Duḥśāsana, and King Duryodhana, the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra.'

**5.141.3**  'Without doubt, O Kṛṣṇa, this great battle is at hand between the Pāṇḍavas and the Kurus — terrible, a mire of blood.'

**5.141.4**  'Kings and princes who follow Duryodhana's command, consumed by the fire of weapons in battle, shall reach the abode of Yama.'

**5.141.5**  'For many dreadful dreams are seen, O Madhusūdana, and dreadful omens, and fearful portents too.'

**5.141.6**  'Various hair-raising signs seem to proclaim defeat for the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra and victory for Yudhiṣṭhira, O Vārṣṇeya.'

**5.141.7**  'For the fierce planet of great brilliance, Saturn, afflicts the constellation of Prajāpati, oppressing living beings exceedingly.'

**5.141.8**  'And Mars, having turned retrograde in Jyeshtha, O Madhusūdana, moves toward Anuradha, as though appeasing Mitra.'

**5.141.9**  'Surely a great terror has come upon the Kurus, O Kṛṣṇa; for particularly, O Vārṣṇeya, a planet afflicts the star Chitra.'

**5.141.10**  'The mark of the moon has shifted; Rāhu will approach the sun; and from the sky these meteors fall with thunderclaps and tremors.'

**5.141.11**  'Elephants groan and horses shed tears; they do not take joy even in water and barley, O Mādhava.'

**5.141.12**  'When these omens appear, they say that a dreadful peril is at hand, O great-armed one — a destruction of living beings.'

**5.141.13**  'Even with little eaten, abundant excrement is seen here from horses, from elephants, and from men, O Keśava.'

**5.141.14**  'In all the armies of the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, O Madhusūdana — the wise declare that to be a sign of defeat.'

**5.141.15**  'The mounts of the Pāṇḍavas are described as joyful, O Kṛṣṇa, and deer circle them clockwise — that is the sign of victory for them.'

**5.141.16**  'All the deer circle counter-clockwise around the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, O Keśava, and there are disembodied voices as well — that is the sign of defeat.'

**5.141.17**  'Peacocks, flower-birds, geese, sarus cranes, chataka birds, and flocks of jivanjivaka birds follow the Pāṇḍavas.'

**5.141.18**  'Vultures, crows, herons, hawks, rakshasas, jackals, and swarms of flies follow the Kauravas.'

**5.141.19**  'In the armies of the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, the war-drums make no sound; but the kettledrums of the Pāṇḍavas sound unbeaten on their own.'

**5.141.20**  'Wells bellow like bulls in the armies of the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra — that is the sign of defeat.'

**5.141.21**  'A rain of flesh and blood has been rained down by heaven, O Mādhava, and a celestial city of the Gandharvas has appeared near the sun — complete with ramparts, moat, altar-mound, and beautiful gateways.'

**5.141.22**  'A dark halo stands there encircling the sun; at the morning and evening twilight it portends great terror. A lone she-jackal howls terribly — that is the sign of defeat.'

**5.141.23**  'Black-necked birds, hanging in a frightful manner, fly toward the twilight — that is the sign of defeat.'

**5.141.24**  'He first comes to hate brahmins and then his teachers, O Madhusūdana, and also servants who are devoted to him — that is the sign of defeat.'

**5.141.25**  'The eastern quarter has the color of blood, the southern has the color of weapons, and the western, O Madhusūdana, resembles an unripe vessel.'

**5.141.26**  'And all the quarters blaze for the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, O Mādhava; these portent-signs proclaim great terror.'

**5.141.27**  'At the end of a dream, I saw Yudhiṣṭhira ascending a thousand-pillared palace together with his brothers, O Acyuta.'

**5.141.28**  'All of them are seen wearing white turbans, dressed in white garments; and I observe white seats for all of them.'

**5.141.29**  'And you too, O Kṛṣṇa, were seen by me at the end of a dream encircling the earth, covered in blood, with entrails, O Janārdana.'

**5.141.30**  'Yudhiṣṭhira of boundless valor, having climbed a heap of bones, joyfully ate rice cooked in clarified butter from a golden vessel.'

**5.141.31**  'Yudhiṣṭhira was seen by me devouring the earth — clearly he shall enjoy this earth given to him by you.'

**5.141.32**  'Bhīma of fierce deeds, Vṛkodara, having climbed a high mountain, club in hand, tiger among men, looks over this earth as if surveying it.'

**5.141.33**  'He will very clearly annihilate all of us in the great battle. I know this, O Hrishikesha: where dharma is, there is victory.'

**5.141.34**  'That Dhanañjaya, the Gāṇḍīva-wielder, mounted on a white elephant, blazing with supreme glory together with you, O Hrishikesha —'

**5.141.35**  '— you shall all slay the kings led by Duryodhana in battle, O Kṛṣṇa. Of this I have no doubt.'

**5.141.36**  'Nakula and Sahadeva and the great chariot-warrior Sātyaki, their armlets and neck-guards gleaming, clad in white garlands and white garments —'

**5.141.37**  '— these three tigers among men, great ministers, sheltered by white parasols and white garments, were mounted on the finest human vehicle.'

**5.141.38**  'And three are seen wearing white turbans in the armies of the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, O Janārdana — know them, O Keśava.'

**5.141.39**  'Aśvatthāmā, Kṛpa, and Kṛtavarmā the Sātvata — and all the kings are seen wearing red turbans, O Mādhava.'

**5.141.40**  'Bhīṣma and Droṇa were seen by me mounted on a camel-drawn vehicle, O Janārdana, together with me and the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, O great-armed, O resplendent one.'

**5.141.41**  'We were going toward the quarter governed by Agastya, O Janārdana — in no long time we shall reach the abode of Yama.'

**5.141.42**  I and the other kings and all the circle of warriors shall enter the fire of Gāṇḍīva — of this I have no doubt.

**5.141.43**  Kṛṣṇa said: The earth must surely be facing destruction today, for my words, O Karṇa, do not reach your heart.

**5.141.44**  When destruction is at hand for all creatures, O dear one, what looks like wise counsel does not depart from the hearts of men — it is wrongdoing disguised as policy.

**5.141.45**  Karṇa said: May we see you again, O Kṛṣṇa, having survived this great battle, O mighty-armed one, having crossed through the destruction and ruin of heroes.

**5.141.46**  Or else, O Kṛṣṇa, our reunion shall surely be in heaven — there we shall meet again, together with you, O sinless one.

**5.141.47**  Sañjaya said: Having spoken thus to Mādhava and embraced him tightly, Karṇa, dismissed by Keśava, descended from the floor of the chariot.

**5.141.48**  Then, mounting his own chariot adorned with gold, Rādheya returned with us, his heart despondent.

**5.141.49**  Then Keśava drove away swiftly, together with Sātyaki, repeatedly calling out to his charioteer, "Go, go!"

**5.142.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: When Kṛṣṇa had departed from the Kurus to the Pāṇḍavas with his mission of conciliation unfulfilled, Vidura approached Pṛthā and spoke to her gently, as if grieving.

**5.142.2**  O you whose sons are living, you know my disposition, always devoted to your welfare — yet Suyodhana does not accept my words, though I cry out repeatedly.

**5.142.3**  That king is supported by the Cedis, Pāñcālas, and Kekayas, by Bhīma and Arjuna, by Kṛṣṇa, Yuyudhāna, and the Yamas as well.

**5.142.4**  Even while encamped at Upaplavya, Yudhiṣṭhira — powerful as he is — desires only righteousness out of affection for his kinsmen, like a weak man.

**5.142.5**  But this king Dhṛtarāṣṭra, though old in years, does not calm himself — intoxicated solely by pride in his son, he walks the path of unrighteousness.

**5.142.6**  Through the evil counsel of Jayadratha, Karṇa, Duḥśāsana, and Śakuni's son, mutual strife is being fanned.

**5.142.7**  The kingdom has indeed been seized by unrighteous means from those who are most righteous — this very unrighteousness, with all its consequences, shall return upon those who took it.

**5.142.8**  Who would not burn with indignation when righteousness is forcibly wrested away by the Kurus? With Keśava gone without conciliation, the Pāṇḍavas shall make ready for war.

**5.142.9**  Then the Kurus will meet with ruin and the slaughter of their heroes. Thinking of this, I find no sleep by day or by night.

**5.142.10**  Kuntī, hearing those words spoken with genuine purpose, unwilling to accept what she did not desire, afflicted with grief, reflected deeply in her mind.

**5.142.11**  Shame on wealth, for the sake of which this great destruction among kinsmen will occur — for in this war the defeat of these friends shall surely come to pass.

**5.142.12**  If the Pāṇḍavas, the Cedis, the Pāñcālas, and the Yādavas, assembled together, fight against the Bhāratas, what sorrow could be greater than this?

**5.142.13**  I see only certain calamity in this war, and defeat in this war as well. Death is better than poverty, for victory is no victory when it comes through the slaughter of kinsmen.

**5.142.14**  Bhīṣma the son of Śāntanu, the preceptor who is lord of warriors, and Karṇa — all fighting for the sake of Dhṛtarāṣṭra's son — increase my fear.

**5.142.15**  Surely teacher Droṇa, who has no desire for personal gain, would never willingly fight against his pupils — and how could the grandsire Bhīṣma not feel affection for the Pāṇḍavas?

**5.142.16**  But this one alone — evil, holding perverse views, always following delusion, the wicked man who serves the evil-minded Dhārtarāṣṭra — constantly hates the Pāṇḍavas.

**5.142.17**  Karṇa is particularly insistent on great calamity, and he is especially powerful; he always opposes the Pāṇḍavas — this burns me now.

**5.142.18**  But today I hope to appease Karṇa's mind toward the Pāṇḍavas, by approaching him and showing him the truth of the matter.

**5.142.19**  When I had pleased the revered Durvāsas, he gave me a boon — a divine invocation connected with the gods, while I was living in my father's house.

**5.142.20**  I was then in the inner quarters of king Kuntibhoja's palace, my heart aching, thinking in many ways.

**5.142.21**  I kept thinking again and again about the strength and weakness of the mantras, about the power of a brahmin's word, and about the weaknesses of a woman and a child.

**5.142.22**  Protected then by a trusted nurse, surrounded by my circle of companions, guarding against transgression, and preserving my father's honor.

**5.142.23**  Thinking "How may I do what is right? How may I be without guilt?" and having bowed to that brahmin,

**5.142.24**  Out of curiosity upon receiving it, and acting foolishly out of childishness, I, a chaste girl, then invoked the god Sūrya.

**5.142.25**  He who was born of me before wedlock and has been treated as a son — why would he not heed words that are wholesome and beneficial to his brothers?

**5.142.26**  Having thus resolved upon this as the highest and most certain course of action, Kuntī set out on her errand and went to the Bhāgīrathī river.

**5.142.27**  On the bank of the Gaṅgā, Pṛthā then heard the sound of her own son's recitation — that compassionate and truth-devoted man.

**5.142.28**  That ascetic woman stood behind him, who faced east with upraised arms, waiting for the end of his silent recitation so she could accomplish her purpose.

**5.142.29**  She stood on the northern robe of Karṇa, scorched by the sun, the wife of a Kaurava and daughter of the Vṛṣṇis, withering like a garland of lotuses.

**5.142.30**  Having recited until his back too was scorched by the sun, that disciplined man turned around; seeing Kuntī, the great-spirited and honorable Karṇa, best among upholders of righteousness, approached and greeted her with joined palms as propriety required.

**5.143.1**  Karṇa said: I am Karṇa, son of Rādhā, son of Adhiratha — I salute you. What brings you here, noble lady? Tell me, what may I do for you?

**5.143.2**  Kuntī said: You are a son of Kuntī, not of Rādhā; Adhiratha is not your father. You were not born in a sūta family, O Karṇa — know this from my words.

**5.143.3**  You were born of me before my marriage, carried in my womb as my firstborn, in the house of Kuntibhoja — you are a son of Pṛthā, dear child.

**5.143.4**  This god who performs brilliant deeds, the sun, who illumines all — he begot you in me, O Karṇa, best among bearers of weapons.

**5.143.5**  Born wearing earrings, with armor fastened on, conceived of a god, and endowed with splendor, you, O invincible one, were born of me, my son, in my father's house.

**5.143.6**  That you, failing to recognize your brothers, serve the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra out of delusion — that is not fitting for you, my son, especially not for you.

**5.143.7**  This is the fruit of righteousness for men when righteousness is their aim, O son — that his fathers are pleased, and his mother who shares the same vision.

**5.143.8**  Seize back from the Dhārtarāṣṭras the royal glory of Yudhiṣṭhira, which was earned by Arjuna and taken by wicked men out of greed, and enjoy it.

**5.143.9**  Today let the Kurus behold the union of Karṇa and Arjuna; witnessing that brotherhood, let the wicked ones bow their heads.

**5.143.10**  If Karṇa and Arjuna were as Rāma and Janārdana together, what in the world would be unachievable for you two with united hearts?

**5.143.11**  O Karṇa, you will surely shine, surrounded by five brothers, as Brahmā is surrounded by the Vedas together with the five Vedāṅgas.

**5.143.12**  Endowed with the finest qualities, the eldest among the most excellent kinsmen — let the epithet "son of a charioteer" not be spoken; you are a son of Pṛthā, O valiant one.

**5.144.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Then Karṇa heard words issuing from the sun — irresistible, affectionate, urging him as a father would, sent by Bhāskara.

**5.144.2**  Pṛthā speaks the truth, O Karṇa — heed your mother's words. It shall be for your good, O tiger among men, if you act accordingly in everything.

**5.144.3**  Although thus addressed by his mother and by the sun himself, his father, the resolve of Karṇa, steadfast in truth, did not waver even then.

**5.144.4**  Karṇa said: I do not disbelieve the words you, O kṣatriya lady, have spoken to me — this your commission may be the gate of righteousness for me.

**5.144.5**  The great transgression you committed against me — by that act you have cast me out, and it destroyed my fame and reputation.

**5.144.6**  I was born a kṣatriya yet did not attain the rites proper to a kṣatriya — because of you. What worse harm could an enemy do to me?

**5.144.7**  Not showing compassion for me at the time of the rites, you left me then in a state deprived of proper sacraments — and now today you urge me.

**5.144.8**  You did not act toward me before as a mother concerned with my welfare — and now you counsel me, seeking only your own benefit.

**5.144.9**  Who would not tremble before Dhanaṃjaya, who is united with Kṛṣṇa? Who would not know me today as afraid, if I went over to the assembly of the Pārthas?

**5.144.10**  Unknown as a brother before, revealed only at the time of war — if I now go to the Pāṇḍavas, what will the kṣatriyas say of me?

**5.144.11**  I have been given every pleasure and been constantly and greatly honored by the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra — how can I render their efforts fruitless?

**5.144.12**  Those who have incurred the enmity of others on my account, who always attend upon me and ever bow to me as the Vasus bow to Indra —

**5.144.13**  Those who believe themselves able, through my life's effort, to withstand their enemies — how today could I shatter their heart's desire?

**5.144.14**  Those who wish to cross the impassable battle with me as their boat, who desire the other shore of the shoreless sea — how could I abandon them?

**5.144.15**  For this is the time that has come for those who live in dependence on Dhārtarāṣṭra — I must discharge my debt there, without sparing my life.

**5.144.16**  Those who, having been well maintained and their purposes fulfilled, act treacherously when the time of need arrives, disregarding what was done for them — they are wicked and unstable.

**5.144.17**  For those sinful men who are guilty of betraying their king and who steal the master's bread — neither this world nor the next exists.

**5.144.18**  I shall fight for the sake of the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra against your sons, employing all my strength and power — truly I do not speak falsely to you.

**5.144.19**  Protecting compassion and the conduct befitting an honorable man, even though your proposal would benefit me, I shall not follow your words today.

**5.144.20**  Yet your undertaking shall not be in vain with respect to me — in battle I shall not slay your sons who are worthy of being slain and who can be defeated: Yudhiṣṭhira, Bhīma, and the twins — save only for Arjuna.

**5.144.21**  My combat with Arjuna in the army of Yudhiṣṭhira will be even — for by slaying Arjuna in battle the goal shall be achieved by me, and I shall also be united with glory even if slain by Savyasācin.

**5.144.22**  Your five sons, O glorious lady, shall never perish — whether without Arjuna but with Karṇa alive, or with Arjuna but with me slain.

**5.144.23**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Hearing Karṇa's words, Kuntī trembled with grief; she embraced her son Karṇa, who remained unshaken in his steadfastness, and spoke.

**5.144.24**  "So it must be — through this the Kurus shall meet their destruction. As you speak, O Karṇa, fate is the more powerful."

**5.144.25**  "The safety you have granted to my four sons, O vanquisher of foes — pledge that promise, the release from battle."

**5.144.26**  "Be well, be safe," said Pṛthā to Karṇa; Karṇa responded to her with affection, and then the two departed separately.

**5.145.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Having come from Hāstinapura to Upaplavya, the subduer of enemies, Keśava, told the Pāṇḍavas everything that had occurred.

**5.145.2**  Having conversed for a long time and deliberated again and again, Śauri went to his own quarters to rest.

**5.145.3**  Having dismissed all the kings headed by Virāṭa, the five Pāṇḍava brothers, when the sun had set,

**5.145.4**  having performed the twilight prayer and meditating, their minds fixed on that same matter, brought Kṛṣṇa of the Dāśārha clan once more and deliberated in counsel again.

**5.145.5**  Yudhiṣṭhira said: Having gone to the elephant city, O Lotus-eyed one, what did you say to the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra in the assembly? Please tell us all of it.

**5.145.6**  Vāsudeva said: Having gone to the elephant city, I spoke to the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra in the assembly words that were truthful, beneficial, and good — yet that wicked-minded one does not accept them.

**5.145.7**  Yudhiṣṭhira said: When that one had gone astray, O Hṛṣīkeśa, what did the elder of the Kurus, the grandsire, say? And what did the preceptor, the mighty-armed son of Bharadvāja, say to the impatient Duryodhana?

**5.145.8**  And what did our younger father Vidura, the foremost among upholders of righteousness, afflicted with grief over his son, say to the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra?

**5.145.9**  And what did all the kings assembled in that hall say truthfully? Tell us all of this, O Janārdana.

**5.145.10**  For you have spoken everything to the two foremost of the Kurus — to that dull-witted one overwhelmed by desire and greed, who fancies himself wise.

**5.145.11**  That unpleasant news does not rest easy in my heart, O Keśava. I wish to hear their words, O Govinda, O lord.

**5.145.12**  And do act, dear one, so that the critical moment does not arrive. You are our refuge, O Kṛṣṇa; you are our master; you are our guide.

**5.145.13**  Vāsudeva said: Listen, O king, to how King Suyodhana was addressed in the midst of the Kurus in the assembly. Hear it from me, O lord of kings.

**5.145.14**  When my words were spoken, the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra laughed. Then Bhīṣma, greatly enraged, spoke these words:

**5.145.15**  "Duryodhana, understand what I say to you for the sake of your lineage. Having heard it, O tiger among kings, do what is beneficial for your own clan.

**5.145.16**  My father, dear boy, was King Śaṃtanu, renowned throughout the world. I was his sole son, the foremost among those who have sons.

**5.145.17**  A thought arose in his mind: how might a second son come to be? For the wise declare that one with only one son is as good as sonless.

**5.145.18**  And lest the line become extinct, and lest his fame cease to spread — understanding his wish, I brought Kālī as his mother.

**5.145.19**  Having made a terrible vow for my father's sake and for the sake of the lineage — remaining celibate and kingless, as is well known to you — I have lived here faithfully, keeping that vow.

**5.145.20**  From her was born the mighty-armed, glorious uplifter of the Kuru lineage — Vicitravīrya, righteous of soul, the younger king, my brother.

**5.145.21**  When my father had ascended to heaven, I installed King Vicitravīrya in his own kingdom, myself serving as a subordinate, walking humbly below.

**5.145.22**  I brought suitable wives for him, O lord of kings, having conquered an assembly of kings — this too you have heard many times.

**5.145.23**  Then I met Rāma in single combat on the battlefield. For being driven out by the inhabitants of that city through fear of Rāma, and being overly attached to his wives, Vicitravīrya contracted consumption.

**5.145.24**  When the lord of the gods did not rain upon the kingless realm, the subjects, tormented by hunger and fear, came rushing to me alone.

**5.145.25**  The subjects said: All the people are perishing. Become our king for our welfare. Drive away our afflictions. Good fortune to you, O increaser of Śaṃtanu's lineage.

**5.145.26**  All those subjects are afflicted by greatly terrible diseases and are nearly spent. O son of the Gaṅgā, you must protect them.

**5.145.27**  Drive away the diseases, O hero, and protect the subjects righteously. While you are alive, let not the kingdom come to ruin.

**5.145.28**  Bhīṣma said: Even as the subjects were lamenting, my mind was not shaken — protecting my vow, and remembering likewise my good conduct.

**5.145.29**  Then the citizens, O great king, and my auspicious mother Kālī, the servants, the chaplain, the teachers, and the greatly learned Brahmins, all deeply grieved, spoke to me repeatedly: 'Become king.'

**5.145.30**  'The kingdom protected by Pratīpa will perish now that it has come to you. For our welfare, become king, O great-minded one.'

**5.145.31**  Thus addressed, I folded my hands, deeply grieved and distressed. Out of reverence for my father, I declared my vow to them repeatedly — that I was celibate and without kingship, for the sake of the lineage.

**5.145.32**  Then, with folded hands, I appeased my mother, saying: 'No, mother — I, born of Śaṃtanu and bearing up the Kaurava lineage, would never make my vow false' — thus, O king, again and again.

**5.145.33**  'And especially for your sake — do not yoke me to this burden. I am your servant and your slave, O mother who loves your son.'

**5.145.34**  Having thus appeased my mother and the people, I then besought the great sage Vyāsa regarding wives for my brothers.

**5.145.35**  Together with my mother, O great king, having propitiated that sage, I begged him for offspring. He granted the grace, and he then fathered three sons, O best of the Bharatas.

**5.145.36**  Because he was blind and lacking faculties, your father was not king. Rather, the great-souled Pāṇḍu became king, renowned throughout the world.

**5.145.37**  He was the king, and his sons — your cousins — are the heirs to their father's inheritance. Do not quarrel, dear boy; let half the kingdom be given to them.

**5.145.38**  While I still live, what man should rule the kingdom here? Do not disregard my words. I always desire peace for you all.

**5.145.39**  There is no distinction for me between you and them, O king, my son. This is the view of your father, of Gāndhārī, and of Vidura.

**5.145.40**  If the words of elders are to be heeded, do not be suspicious of my counsel. You will not destroy everything — yourself and the earth alike."

**5.146.1**  Vāsudeva said: After Bhīṣma had spoken, Droṇa then addressed Duryodhana in the midst of the kings — he who was capable of speech spoke words of good counsel:

**5.146.2**  "Just as Prātīpa's son Śaṃtanu arose for the sake of his lineage, so too Devavrata Bhīṣma stood firm for the sake of the lineage.

**5.146.3**  Then Pāṇḍu, the lord of men, true to his word, master of his senses, the king of the Kurus, righteous in soul, of noble vows, and composed of mind —

**5.146.4**  — that increaser of the Kuru lineage gave the kingdom to the elder Dhṛtarāṣṭra, the wise one, and likewise acknowledged the younger Vidura.

**5.146.5**  Then, O king, having installed that unfailing one upon the lion-throne, the Kaurava prince departed to the forest together with his two wives, O sinless one.

**5.146.6**  And Vidura, that tiger among men, stood humbly in a low place attending like a servant, waving the yak-tail fan.

**5.146.7**  Then all the subjects, dear boy, followed Dhṛtarāṣṭra as their lord of men, duly, just as they had followed Pāṇḍu, the ruler of men.

**5.146.8**  Having handed the kingdom over to Dhṛtarāṣṭra and Vidura, Pāṇḍu, the conqueror of enemy cities, ranged over the entire earth.

**5.146.9**  In building up the treasury, in giving gifts, in overseeing the servants, and in maintaining everything — Vidura, true to his word, took charge.

**5.146.10**  Bhīṣma, of great splendor and conqueror of enemy cities, superintended the matters of peace and war and attended to the affairs of the king.

**5.146.11**  King Dhṛtarāṣṭra of great might sat upon the lion-throne, constantly attended upon by the great-souled Vidura.

**5.146.12**  Being born in that lineage, how do you resolve to divide it? Unite with your brothers and enjoy your pleasures together, O lord of the people.

**5.146.13**  I say this not out of pity, nor for any material reason whatsoever. I eat what is given by Bhīṣma, not by you, O best of kings.

**5.146.14**  I shall not seek from you any means of livelihood, O lord of the people. Where Bhīṣma is, there is Droṇa. Do what Bhīṣma has said.

**5.146.15**  Let half the kingdom be given to the sons of Pāṇḍu, O scourge of enemies. My preceptorship is equal to both you and them at all times, dear boy.

**5.146.16**  As Aśvatthāmā is to me, so too is the white-horsed Arjuna to me. What need is there for much talk? Where there is dharma, there is victory."

**5.146.17**  When the immeasurably splendid Droṇa had thus spoken, O great king, Vidura, true to his word and knower of dharma, turning and looking at his father's face, then spoke these words:

**5.146.18**  "Devavrata, hear these words I speak: the Kaurava lineage had been all but lost, and you raised it up again.

**5.146.19**  Yet you disregard my lamentation's words. Who is this Duryodhana, this defiler of the clan, in this lineage?

**5.146.20**  You follow the will of one overwhelmed by greed, ignoble and ungrateful, whose mind is destroyed by greed — one who transgresses the counsel of his father who sees dharma and artha.

**5.146.21**  These Kurus are perishing through what Duryodhana has done. O great king, act so that they do not perish.

**5.146.22**  Having made me and Dhṛtarāṣṭra as mere paintings at the outset, O greatly radiant one, do not destroy us. As Prajāpati, having created beings, withdraws them, so too would you.

**5.146.23**  Do not be indifferent, O mighty-armed one, seeing the destruction of the lineage. Or if your mind is now lost, with ruin at hand, then go to the forest together with me and with Dhṛtarāṣṭra.

**5.146.24**  Or else, having bound up that wicked-minded son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra of treacherous intellect, let this kingdom now be well protected by the Pāṇḍavas.

**5.146.25**  Be gracious, O tiger among kings. Great destruction is foreseen — of the Pāṇḍavas, the Kurus, and kings of immeasurable splendor."

**5.146.26**  Having spoken thus, Vidura fell silent, his mind despondent, brooding, and sighing again and again.

**5.146.27**  Then the daughter of King Subala, fearful of the destruction of the lineage and speaking in accordance with dharma and artha, addressed in anger her wicked-minded and cruel son Duryodhana in the presence of the kings:

**5.146.28**  "Let all the kings who have entered this royal assembly, and the brahmin seers and other members of the assembly, hear — I shall speak of the transgression of this sinful one along with his ministers and entourage.

**5.146.29**  The kingdom of the Kurus is to be enjoyed in proper succession — this is our lineage's custom handed down in order. You of wicked mind and extremely cruel deeds are unjustly destroying the Kurus' kingdom.

**5.146.30**  Dhṛtarāṣṭra the wise stands in the kingdom; his younger brother Vidura is far-sighted. Passing over these two, how do you today out of delusion seek kingship, Duryodhana?

**5.146.31**  Both the king and Vidura, those of great majesty, would be subservient while Bhīṣma stands. Yet that great-souled best of men, the river-born, desires no kingdom through his knowledge of dharma.

**5.146.32**  This kingdom of Pāṇḍu is inviolable; his sons alone are the lords of it today, none others. That entire kingdom belongs to the Pāṇḍavas, the ancestral inheritance passing to sons and grandsons.

**5.146.33**  Whatever the great-souled foremost of the Kurus, Devavrata, true to his word and wise, has said — all of that is to be accepted by us, laying aside other dharma, while upholding our own dharma.

**5.146.34**  And whatever the king Vidura would say with the permission of that great-vowed one — that too should be done, appointed by friends, placing dharma foremost, for a very long time.

**5.146.35**  Let Yudhiṣṭhira, the son of Dharma, rule this kingdom of the Kurus which has come to him by right — urged on by King Dhṛtarāṣṭra and honored also by the son of Śaṃtanu."

**5.147.1**  Vāsudeva said: When Gāndhārī had spoken thus, Dhṛtarāṣṭra, the lord of men, addressed Duryodhana with these words in the midst of the kings, O lord of the people:

**5.147.2**  "Duryodhana, understand what I shall say to you, dear son. Do accordingly — good fortune to you — if you have reverence for your father.

**5.147.3**  Soma the Prajāpati was the primordial increaser of the Kuru lineage. From Soma in the sixth generation was born Yayāti, the son of Nahuṣa.

**5.147.4**  He had five sons, the best of royal sages. Among them, the mighty-souled Yadu became the eldest and the lord.

**5.147.5**  And then there was Pūru, the younger, who is the increaser of our lineage, born of Śarmiṣṭhā, daughter of Vṛṣaparvan.

**5.147.6**  And Yadu, O best of the Bharatas, was the son of Devayānī, the grandson of Śukra Kāvya of immeasurable splendor, dear boy.

**5.147.7**  He, the founder of the Yādava lineage, was mighty and esteemed for his valor. But filled with pride and of very dull wit, he disrespected the warrior class.

**5.147.8**  Deluded by pride in his strength, he did not abide by his father's instruction. He also disrespected his father and his brothers — that undefeated one.

**5.147.9**  Yadu alone became the mighty one on the earth bounded by the four horizons. Having subjugated the kings, he dwelt in the city named after the elephant.

**5.147.10**  His father Yayāti, son of Nahuṣa, in extreme fury cursed that son, O Gāndhāra king, and removed him from the kingdom.

**5.147.11**  And those brothers who followed him in his arrogant pride — Yayāti, enraged, cursed those sons as well.

**5.147.12**  Then that best of kings installed his younger son Pūru, who was obedient and compliant, upon the throne.

**5.147.13**  Thus even a proud elder son does not obtain the kingdom, while younger sons obtain it through service to the elders.

**5.147.14**  Similarly, my father's grandfather Pratīpa, the earth-protector, knower of all dharma, was renowned in all three worlds.

**5.147.15**  Of that lion among kings, ruling his kingdom righteously, three illustrious sons were born, equal to the gods in glory.

**5.147.16**  Devāpi was the eldest, Bāhlīka came next, and the third, O father, was the steadfast Śaṃtanu, my grandfather.

**5.147.17**  But Devāpi, though of great splendor and best of kings, had a skin disease; he was righteous, truthful, and devoted to serving his father.

**5.147.18**  Devāpi was honored by the people of the city and countryside, respected by the virtuous, and dear to the hearts of all — young and old alike.

**5.147.19**  He was wise, true to his word, devoted to the welfare of all beings, and lived by the teachings of his father and the brahmaṇas.

**5.147.20**  He was dear as a brother to Bāhlīka and to the great-souled Śaṃtanu, and there was supreme fraternal harmony among those noble souls united together.

**5.147.21**  Then in the course of time, the aged king, that best of rulers, had the materials for the consecration prepared according to scripture, and had all the auspicious rites performed as well — he who was sovereign over all.

**5.147.22**  But the brahmaṇas and elders, together with the people of city and countryside, all prevented the consecration of Devāpi.

**5.147.23**  Hearing that the consecration was barred, the king's throat choked with tears, and he grieved deeply for his son.

**5.147.24**  Although so generous, so learned in dharma, so true to his vow, and so dear to the people, he was disqualified by the blemish of a skin disease.

**5.147.25**  "The gods do not favor a king with a bodily defect" — on this ground the foremost of twice-born men barred that best of kings.

**5.147.26**  Then that king, his soul deeply troubled and grief-stricken for his son, died; and seeing him dead, Devāpi took refuge in the forest.

**5.147.27**  Bāhlīka, abandoning the kingdom, settled in his mother's family, renouncing his father's brothers, and attained a prosperous city.

**5.147.28**  With Bāhlīka's permission, O king, the world-renowned Śaṃtanu ruled the kingdom as king after his father's death.

**5.147.29**  In the same way, O Bhārata, I — the elder — was removed from the kingdom by the wise Pāṇḍu on the ground that I am defective in a limb.

**5.147.30**  Pāṇḍu, though younger, obtained the kingdom as king. Now with his sons destroyed, this kingdom belongs here; I am unfit for kingship — how then, O subduer of foes, can you desire this kingdom?

**5.147.31**  Yudhiṣṭhira is a noble prince of great soul; this kingdom has come to him by rightful succession. He is the lord of this Kaurava people, and a ruler of great majesty.

**5.147.32**  He is true to his vow, ever vigilant, steadfast in his duty, virtuous toward his kin, beloved of the people, compassionate to friends, master of his senses, and protector of the good.

**5.147.33**  Forbearance, patience, self-control, uprightness, adherence to the vow of truth, learning, heedfulness, compassion for all beings, and the power of command — all the virtues of a king are united in Yudhiṣṭhira.

**5.147.34**  You are no true prince, you follow ignoble conduct, you are greedy, you harbor wicked thoughts toward your own kin — how can you, so ill-behaved, seize this kingdom that belongs by succession to another?

**5.147.35**  Free yourself from delusion and give up half the kingdom — with its cavalry and all its furnishings. Then the remainder of your life, together with your brother's, may still be secured, O lord of men.

**5.148.1**  Vāsudeva said: Though Bhīṣma, Droṇa, Vidura, Gāndhārī, and Dhṛtarāṣṭra spoke thus, the foolish one did not come to his senses.

**5.148.2**  He shook off their words, rose up in anger, his eyes red with rage; and the kings, reckless of their lives, ran after him.

**5.148.3**  And that evil-minded king commanded those rulers again and again: "March to Kurukṣetra — today is the auspicious Puṣya asterism!"

**5.148.4**  Then those lords of the earth marched out with their armies, making Bhīṣma their commander, rejoicing, driven on by fate.

**5.148.5**  Eleven akṣauhiṇī armies of the kings assembled; at their head Bhīṣma, bearing the palm-tree banner, shone resplendent. Do now, O lord of the earth, whatever is fitting and proper here.

**5.148.6**  O Bhārata, the words spoken by Bhīṣma, Droṇa, Vidura, Gāndhārī, and Dhṛtarāṣṭra in my presence — all that transpired in the Kuru assembly, O king, I have now told you.

**5.148.7**  Conciliation was first employed by me, O king, as I desired brotherly harmony — for the preservation of the unity of the Kuru lineage and the prosperity of the people.

**5.148.8**  Then division too was my instrument when conciliation was not accepted — along with the recounting of deeds that combined the divine and the human.

**5.148.9**  When Suyodhana did not honor speech beginning with conciliation, then I, having summoned all the kings together, drove them apart.

**5.148.10**  And I demonstrated marvellous, terrifying, dreadful, superhuman deeds, O Bhārata, O lord.

**5.148.11**  And after threatening those kings and making Suyodhana seem as nothing as straw, and frightening Rādheya and Śakuni again and again —

**5.148.12**  — proclaiming again and again the inferiority and censure of the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, and dividing all the kings through speech and counsel repeatedly —

**5.148.13**  — I then again spoke of conciliation combined with the gift, for the unity of the Kuru lineage and also for the sake of what needed to be done.

**5.148.14**  "Let all the Pāṇḍavas abide by the words of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Bhīṣma, and Vidura, setting aside pride, and living humbly subordinate."

**5.148.15**  "Let them yield up the kingdom and become powerless, as the river-born king Bhīṣma and Vidura have declared — let it be so."

**5.148.16**  "Let all the kingdom be yours — but give up five villages; for your father's sons must certainly be provided for, O best of kings."

**5.148.17**  But spoken to thus, the evil-souled one did not relent at all. I see the fourth expedient — force — as the only recourse against those sinful ones; there is no other way.

**5.148.18**  And the lords of men have marched out to Kurukṣetra for their destruction. All that occurred in the Kuru assembly I have now recounted to you.

**5.148.19**  They will not give you the kingdom without war, O Pāṇḍava. All of them are causes of destruction, with death standing ready before them.

**5.149.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Having heard the words of Janārdana, the righteous king Yudhiṣṭhira, whose soul was in dharma, spoke to his brothers in the presence of Keśava.

**5.149.2**  "You have all heard what transpired in the assembly of the Kuru council, and every word that Keśava spoke has been well taken in."

**5.149.3**  "Therefore, O best of men, carry out for me the division of the army. These seven akṣauhiṇī forces have assembled here for victory."

**5.149.4**  "The seven commanders of these forces are well known — hear them: Drupada and Virāṭa, Dhṛṣṭadyumna and Śikhaṇḍin,"

**5.149.5**  "Sātyaki, Cekitāna, and the mighty Bhīmasena — these are the army's leading heroes, all ready to lay down their lives."

**5.149.6**  "All are versed in the Vedas and valiant, all of pure and observed vows, all endowed with modesty and wisdom, all skilled in battle, expert in archery, and fighters with every weapon."

**5.149.7**  "But who shall be the one leader over all seven armies, skilled in their distribution — one who in battle could withstand Bhīṣma, who is like a blazing fire of arrows?"

**5.149.8**  "You, Sahadeva, speak up here, O delight of the Kurus, tiger among men — tell us your opinion: who is fit to be our commander?"

**5.149.9**  Sahadeva said: He is allied with us, shares in our grief, is mighty, a lord of the earth, a knower of dharma — relying on him we seek to recover our own portion.

**5.149.10**  The Matsya king Virāṭa, strong, skilled in arms, intoxicated with battle-fury — he will be able to withstand Bhīṣma and those great chariot-warriors in combat.

**5.149.11**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: When Sahadeva had spoken thus, Nakula, the eloquent in speech, immediately took up the matter after him with these words.

**5.149.12**  "By age, by learning in the scriptures, by steadfastness, by lineage and noble birth, he is endowed with modesty, graced with high family, possessed of glory, expert in all the sacred sciences —"

**5.149.13**  "— he has learned weaponcraft from Bharadvāja's son, he is indomitable and true to his pledge, and he perpetually rivals Droṇa and the mighty Bhīṣma —"

**5.149.14**  "— praiseworthy as the foremost commander before the assembly of kings, surrounded by sons and grandsons like a tree with a hundred branches —"

**5.149.15**  "— that hero and lord of the earth, together with his wife, performed fierce austerities out of fury for the destruction of Droṇa, and he shines in every assembly —"

**5.149.16**  "— he who always provides for us like a father, that bull among kings — let our father-in-law Drupada lead the army at the vanguard."

**5.149.17**  "He, I believe, could withstand both Droṇa and Bhīṣma as they advance in battle — for he is a king who knows divine weapons and is a friend and disciple of the descendant of Aṅgiras."

**5.149.18**  When the two sons of Mādrī had spoken their views, the son of Vāsava, equal to Vāsava himself, the left-handed archer Arjuna, spoke up.

**5.149.19**  "He who was born a divine person through the power of austerity and the satisfaction of the sages, blazing in color and of great strength —"

**5.149.20**  "— bearing a bow, clad in armor, wielding a sword, mounting his chariot in full battle-gear, drawn by the finest divine horses, risen up from the sacrificial fire-pit —"

**5.149.21**  "— roaring like a great storm-cloud with the thunder of his chariot, that valiant hero with a lion's build and a lion's mighty stride —"

**5.149.22**  "— lion-chested, long-armed, with a lion's breast and great strength, roaring like a lion, with a lion's shoulders, of great radiance —"

**5.149.23**  "— with beautiful brows, beautiful teeth, a beautiful jaw, beautiful arms, a beautiful face, not lean, a beautiful clavicle, wide beautiful eyes, beautiful feet, and standing beautifully —"

**5.149.24**  "— impenetrable to all weapons, like a rutting elephant in his force, born for the destruction of Droṇa, truthful in speech, master of his senses —"

**5.149.25**  "— I believe Dhṛṣṭadyumna could withstand Bhīṣma's arrows, which are like the touch of the thunderbolt and lightning, like fire-mouthed serpents —"

**5.149.26**  "— equal to Yama's messengers in speed and descent, like fire in their fury, borne up even by Rāma in battle, dreadful as the crushing stroke of the thunderbolt —"

**5.149.27**  "I see no man who could withstand the great-vowed Bhīṣma other than Dhṛṣṭadyumna, O king — such is my considered opinion."

**5.149.28**  "Quick of hand, varied in his fighting — he is my choice for commander, his armor impenetrable, glorious, like a great elephant leading the herd."

**5.149.29**  Bhīma said: Śikhaṇḍin, son of Drupada, was born for the purpose of killing — so declare the Siddhas and the sages assembled together, O king of kings.

**5.149.30**  In the midst of battles, when he deploys his divine weapon, men will behold his form as they behold the great-souled Rāma himself.

**5.149.31**  I see no one in battles, O king, who could pierce Śikhaṇḍin with a weapon in combat, when he stands armored upon his chariot.

**5.149.32**  None other could withstand Bhīṣma of the great vow in single chariot combat, O king, save the hero Śikhaṇḍin — he is my choice for commander.

**5.149.33**  Yudhiṣṭhira said: O father, the soul of dharma Keśava knows the essence and the non-essence, the strength and weakness, of all the world — past and future alike.

**5.149.34**  Whoever Kṛṣṇa of the Dāśārha clan names — whether skilled in arms or not, whether old or young — let that one be our commander of the army.

**5.149.35**  He is the root of our victory, O father, and of our defeat as well. In him lie our lives and our kingdom, our being and non-being, our joy and sorrow.

**5.149.36**  He is the creator and the ordainer; success is established here. Whom Kṛṣṇa of the Dāśārha clan has named — he is fit to be our commander. Let the best among speakers speak; the night is passing.

**5.149.37**  Then, having installed as commander one who follows Kṛṣṇa's will, when the remainder of the night has passed, we shall march to the battlefield — our weapons consecrated and auspicious rites performed with joy.

**5.149.38**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Having heard those words of the wise Dharma-king, the lotus-eyed one, glancing toward Dhanañjaya, spoke.

**5.149.39**  These leaders of your army whom you have named, O great king — they are heroes, mighty warriors. All of them are capable of crushing your enemies.

**5.149.40**  In great battle, these men could strike fear even in Indra himself — what then of the greedy, evil-minded sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra?

**5.149.41**  I too, O mighty-armed slayer of foes, made great effort for your sake — hoping there might be peace, O Bharata. The debt of dharma has been paid; those who wish to speak cannot reproach us.

**5.149.42**  That childish, unwise Dhṛtarāṣṭra's son considers himself accomplished. The afflicted one considers himself strong — a fool's self-delusion.

**5.149.43**  Let the army be marshalled well — for in my judgment those foes are to be subdued by slaughter. The sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra will not be able to stand firm when they behold Dhanañjaya.

**5.149.44**  And furious Bhīmasena, and the twins who are like Yama himself, and Yuyudhāna as his second, and the wrathful Dhṛṣṭadyumna —

**5.149.45**  Abhimanyu, the sons of Draupadī, Virāṭa and Drupada as well, and the other lords of akshauhini divisions — kings of firm valor —

**5.149.46**  Our army, full of essence and irresistible, impregnable — without doubt it will slay the Dhṛtarāṣṭra forces in battle.

**5.149.47**  When Kṛṣṇa had spoken thus, the foremost of men rejoiced greatly; and from those whose hearts were delighted a great roar arose.

**5.149.48**  "To arms!" — and the armies rushed forward in haste. The cries of horses and elephants, the clatter of wheel-rims from every direction, the tumultuous blast of conches and kettledrums arose on all sides.

**5.149.49**  As the Pāṇḍavas set forth with their armies in every direction, the host appeared like the Gaṅgā in flood — full and irresistible.

**5.149.50**  In the vanguard marched Bhīmasena and the armored sons of Mādrī, Subhadrā's son and the sons of Draupadī, Dhṛṣṭadyumna the son of Pṛṣata, and the Prabhadrakas and Pāñcālas — all with Bhīmasena at their head.

**5.149.51**  Then a sound arose like the ocean at high tide; the clamor of the joyful marching host seemed to touch the sky.

**5.149.52**  Joyful and armored, those warriors, destroyers of enemy ranks, marched; and in their midst went the king, Yudhiṣṭhira, son of Kuntī.

**5.149.53**  The wagon-shops and market stalls, all manner of conveyances and draft animals, the treasury, siege-engines and weapons, as well as physicians and healers —

**5.149.54**  Whatever weak or feeble auxiliary force there was, however lean and infirm — the king gathered all of it, together with the attendants and camp-followers, and marched on.

**5.149.55**  But at Upaplavya, the Pāñcālī Draupadī, the truth-speaker, remained behind with the other women, surrounded by her maidservants and male servants.

**5.149.56**  Having made arrangements for the base camp with stationary and mobile guard detachments, the sons of Pāṇḍu marched forth with a great cantonment-army.

**5.149.57**  Bestowing cows and gold, surrounded by brahmins who chanted their praises, they marched, O king, in chariots adorned with jewels.

**5.149.58**  The Kekayas, Dhṛṣṭaketu, the son of the king of Kāśi named Abhibhū, Śreṇimān, Vasudāna, and the unvanquished Śikhaṇḍī —

**5.149.59**  Joyful and content, clad in armor, armed and adorned, all of them followed the king, surrounding Yudhiṣṭhira.

**5.149.60**  In the rear guard were Virāṭa, Yajñasena the Somaka, Sudharma, Kuntibhoja, and the sons of Dhṛṣṭadyumna.

**5.149.61**  Four times ten thousand chariots, horses five times as many, infantry ten times that number, and sixty thousand cavalry.

**5.149.62**  Anadhrishti, Cekitāna, the king of the Cedis, and Sātyaki — all of them marched surrounding Vāsudeva and Dhanañjaya.

**5.149.63**  Having reached Kurukṣetra, the Pāṇḍavas appeared with their armies arrayed and ready to strike, roaring like bulls.

**5.149.64**  Those slayers of foes, having entered Kurukṣetra, blew their conches; and likewise Vāsudeva and Dhanañjaya both blew their conches.

**5.149.65**  Hearing the blast of the Pāñcajanya conch, like the crack of a thunderbolt, all the armies thrilled with joy on every side.

**5.149.66**  The lion-roars of those swift warriors, mingled with the sound of conches and kettledrums, made the earth, the sky, and the oceans resound in echo.

**5.149.67**  Then king Yudhiṣṭhira encamped his army in a level, pleasant region, rich in barley, fodder, and fuel.

**5.149.68**  Avoiding cremation grounds and temples of the gods, the hermitages of great sages, sacred fords, and holy sites —

**5.149.69**  — in a pleasant, lightly saline tract, auspicious and holy, the lord of the earth, Yudhiṣṭhira son of Kuntī, had the camp laid out.

**5.149.70**  Then, rising again, at ease and with rested mounts, he marched forward, surrounded by kings in their hundreds of thousands.

**5.149.71**  Routing hundreds of outpost detachments of Dhṛtarāṣṭra's soldiers, Keśava together with Pārtha ranged about on all sides.

**5.149.72**  Dhṛṣṭadyumna, son of Pṛṣata, and the noble chariot-warrior Sātyaki — the valiant Yuyudhāna — measured out and marked the camp.

**5.149.73**  Having reached the sacred river Hiraṇvatī in Kurukṣetra — blessed with good fords, with pure water, free of gravel and mire —

**5.149.74**  — there, O Bharata, Keśava had a moat dug for protection, and, having stationed guards, he settled the army in that place.

**5.149.75**  Whatever the arrangement of the camp had been for the great-souled Pāṇḍavas — Keśava had the camps of all the kings laid out in the same manner.

**5.149.76**  Abundantly supplied with water and firewood, made even more difficult to assail, stocked with food and provisions — by the hundreds and thousands —

**5.149.77**  — the splendid camps of the kings were pitched there, each separately from the others, like celestial palaces set down upon the earth, O lord of kings.

**5.149.78**  There were skilled craftsmen by the hundreds, paid their wages, furnished with all implements, and physicians highly expert in their art.

**5.149.79**  Heaps like mountains were amassed — of bowstrings, bows, armor, and weapons, and likewise of honey, clarified butter, medicinal juices, and powders.

**5.149.80**  Plentiful water, good fodder, chaff and charcoal — king Yudhiṣṭhira caused all of this to be gathered in camp after camp.

**5.149.81**  Great siege-engines, iron arrows, javelins, lances, battle-axes, bows, armor and the rest — all of these were close to the hearts of the men in that hour.

**5.149.82**  Elephants harnessed in mail-armor, covered with iron-plate barding, were seen there — mountain-like, each capable of fighting hundreds and thousands of foes.

**5.149.83**  Learning that the Pāṇḍavas were encamped there, O Bharata, their allies came streaming in as appointed, with their forces and vehicles.

**5.149.84**  Those kings who had observed celibacy, drunken the soma, and performed sacrifices with lavish gifts came together for the victory of the sons of Pāṇḍu.

**5.150.1**  Janamejaya said: Yudhiṣṭhira approaching with his army, eager for battle, encamped at Kurukṣetra, protected by Vāsudeva —

**5.150.2**  — accompanied by Virāṭa and Drupada with their sons, surrounded by the Kekayas and the Vṛṣṇis, and by hundreds of kings —

**5.150.3**  — guarded by great chariot-warriors like Mahendra guarded by the Ādityas — hearing of this, what course of action did king Duryodhana adopt?

**5.150.4**  This I wish to hear in full detail, O ascetic sage — what transpired in that tumult and commotion on the Kuru plain.

**5.150.5**  For in battle the Pāṇḍavas, Vāsudeva, and likewise Virāṭa and Drupada could torment even the army of the gods when they are assembled.

**5.150.6**  And Dhṛṣṭadyumna of the Pāñcālas, and Śikhaṇḍī the great chariot-warrior, and the valiant Yuyudhāna — unapproachable even by the gods.

**5.150.7**  All of this I wish to hear in full, O ascetic sage — whatever the Kurus and the Pāṇḍavas did and how they each conducted themselves.

**5.150.8**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: When the Dāśārha hero had departed, king Duryodhana then spoke this to Karṇa, Duḥśāsana, and Śakuni:

**5.150.9**  "Adhokṣaja has gone to the Pārthas with the mission unaccomplished. Filled with anger at them, he will certainly incite them — there is no doubt."

**5.150.10**  "For conflict with me is dear to Vāsudeva's Pāṇḍavas; both Bhīmasena and Arjuna stand in agreement with the Dāśārha's counsel."

**5.150.11**  "Even Ajātaśatru today follows the will of Bhīma and Arjuna; and he was previously deceived by me along with all my brothers."

**5.150.12**  "Virāṭa and Drupada too have been made enemies of mine, and they are the leaders of that army, followers of Vāsudeva's will."

**5.150.13**  "This war that is coming will be tumultuous, hair-raising. Therefore, without delay, have all war preparations made."

**5.150.14**  "Let camps be established at Kurukṣetra, O lords of the earth — with ample space, and made difficult for enemies to attack."

**5.150.15**  "Close to water and firewood, by the hundreds and thousands; with supply lines that cannot be cut; piled with heaps of gems; filled with all manner of weapons; adorned with banners and pennants."

**5.150.16**  "And let level roads be made for them outside the city. Let the departure be announced today: 'tomorrow at dawn' — delay no longer."

**5.150.17**  They pledged "So be it," and at the next dawn the great-souled warriors did as commanded, their faces joyful — though it was for the destruction of the kings of earth.

**5.150.18**  Then all those kings, hearing the royal command, rose from their magnificent seats, burning with impatience.

**5.150.19**  They stroked their arms — like iron bolts, blazing with golden armlets, adorned with sandalwood and aloe —

**5.150.20**  — tightening their turbans with lotus-like hands, arranging inner and outer garments and all their ornaments —

**5.150.21**  — the finest chariot-warriors prepared their chariots; those expert in horses prepared their horses; those trained in the lore of elephants prepared their elephants.

**5.150.22**  Then they readied their variegated armor of gold in great quantity, and all manner of weapons — making everything ready on every side.

**5.150.23**  And foot-soldiers, men bearing all kinds of weapons, attached to their bodies gold-inlaid arms in manifold ways.

**5.150.24**  Like a great festival, the city of Dhṛtarāṣṭra's son was thronged with delighted men, joyfully astir, O Bharata.

**5.150.25**  A whirlpool of the waters of human multitudes, filled with chariots, elephants, and horses as fish — resounding with conches and kettledrums — heaped with treasuries of gems —

**5.150.26**  — its waves the variegated ornaments and armor, its foam the gleaming weapons, its mountains the rows of palaces, its great lake the streets and markets —

**5.150.27**  — stirred by the rising moon of warriors, the great ocean that was the Kuru king's realm appeared then, O king, like the ocean at the rising of the moon.

**5.151.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Recalling the words of Vāsudeva, Yudhiṣṭhira again questioned the Vṛṣṇi hero — how that foolish one had spoken thus:

**5.151.2**  "Now that this time has come upon us, what is proper for us, O Acyuta? And how, conducting ourselves rightly, shall we not swerve from our own dharma?"

**5.151.3**  "You know the minds of Duryodhana, Karṇa, Śakuni the son of Subala, O Vāsudeva — and of me with my brothers as well."

**5.151.4**  "The words of Vidura you have heard, and of both Bhīṣma as well; and the wisdom of Kuntī in its entirety has been heard by you, O one of vast intelligence."

**5.151.5**  Setting all of this aside, and having pondered again and again, O mighty-armed one, tell us without hesitation what is fitting for us.

**5.151.6**  Having heard these words of the Dharma King, which were filled with righteousness and purpose, Kṛṣṇa, whose voice resounds like the thunder of clouds and drums, spoke.

**5.151.7**  I have spoken words that were beneficial, full of righteousness and purpose — but they do not take hold in that Kaurava whose mind is bent on wickedness.

**5.151.8**  That fool of wicked intellect heeds not the counsel of Bhīṣma, nor of Vidura, nor of me — he transgresses all of it entirely.

**5.151.9**  He desires neither righteousness nor glory; that wicked soul, relying on Karṇa, believes he has already conquered all.

**5.151.10**  Suyodhana even ordered my arrest, yet that wicked transgressor of all order did not obtain his desire.

**5.151.11**  Neither Bhīṣma nor Droṇa uttered a fitting word on that occasion; all follow him except Vidura, O Acyuta.

**5.151.12**  Śakuni, the son of Subala, and also Karṇa and Duḥśāsana — those fools spoke unseemly words against you, O impatient one, to a fool.

**5.151.13**  And what use was what I said? Whatever the Kauravas spoke, in brief — that wicked soul does not conduct himself fittingly toward you.

**5.151.14**  Among all the kings and among all these soldiers of yours, whatever is sinful and whatever is not wholesome — all of that is established in him.

**5.151.15**  Nor do we at all desire peace with the Kauravas through excessive concession — after that, war is the only recourse.

**5.151.16**  Having heard these words of Vāsudeva, all the kings, O Bharata, looked silently at the face of the king, saying nothing.

**5.151.17**  Yudhiṣṭhira, perceiving the intent of the kings, ordered the march together with Bhīma, Arjuna, and the twins.

**5.151.18**  Then the Pāṇḍava's army broke into a great tumult of joyful cries, and when the march was ordered, the soldiers rejoiced exceedingly.

**5.151.19**  The Dharma King Yudhiṣṭhira, seeing the slaughter of those who should not be slain, groaned and spoke thus to Bhīmasena and Vijaya:

**5.151.20**  That great calamity for whose sake the forest exile was endured, and the suffering I have borne — that very calamity now comes upon us despite all our efforts.

**5.151.21**  The goal for which we have exerted ourselves has failed us in our endeavour; yet without our effort, this great calamity would have returned to us all the same.

**5.151.22**  How indeed shall we wage battle with those who should not be slain? How shall victory be ours after killing our elders and venerable ones?

**5.151.23**  Hearing this from the Dharma King, Savyasācī, the scorcher of foes, caused him to hear again what Vāsudeva had said.

**5.151.24**  The son of Devakī has spoken, and O king, you have heard in full the counsels of Kuntī and of Vidura.

**5.151.25**  My firm conviction is that neither of those two would counsel unrighteousness; nor, O son of Kuntī, is it proper to turn back without fighting.

**5.151.26**  Hearing those words of Savyasācī, Vāsudeva too smiled and spoke to Pārtha, saying: "It is even so."

**5.151.27**  Then those Pāṇḍavas, O great king, resolved upon war, dwelt that night in comfort together with their soldiers.

**5.152.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: When the night had passed, King Duryodhana then arrayed those armies — eleven in all, O Bharata.

**5.152.2**  Among foot-soldiers, elephants, chariots, and horses — the excellent, the middling, and the inferior — the king of the earth assigned each to all these divisions.

**5.152.3**  Equipped with drag-ropes and quivers, with chariot bodies and lances, with sword-sheaths and javelins, with arrow-pouches and bludgeons —

**5.152.4**  With standards and banners, with bows and javelins, with variegated ropes, with nooses and protective armour —

**5.152.5**  With hair-grasping flings, with oil, molasses, and sand, all furnished with pots of venomous serpents, and with resin-sap and dust —

**5.152.6**  All equipped with bells and shields, with axes and wood-cutters, covered with tiger skins and clad in leopard hides —

**5.152.7**  With bandages and horns, with pikes and various weapons, with axes and mattocks, with oil, linen, and ghee —

**5.152.8**  The ranks were splendid, the men well-formed, blazing like fires — those armoured heroes, practiced and skilled in weapons.

**5.152.9**  Of noble birth, versed in the breeding of horses, installed as charioteers — with protective charms bound on, with girths fastened, with standards and banners secured.

**5.152.10**  All the chariots were drawn by four horses, all were fully armed with weapons, all had spirited mounts, and all were equipped with a hundred bows.

**5.152.11**  One man managed the two draft horses, two others served as rear-guard charioteers, and those two were the finest among chariot warriors — and the chariot warrior himself was likewise an expert in horses.

**5.152.12**  There were thousands of chariots, garlanded with gold all around, like well-guarded cities difficult for enemies to assail.

**5.152.13**  Just as the chariots, so too the elephants — with girths fastened and richly adorned — had seven men each, like gem-bearing mountains.

**5.152.14**  Of these seven, two bore elephant-hooks, two bore the finest bows, two bore excellent swords, O king, and one bore a lance and a banner.

**5.152.15**  That force of the Kaurava, O king, was filled with thousands of maddened elephants bearing armour, weapons, and housings.

**5.152.16**  There were tens of thousands of horses, adorned with variegated armour and ornaments, bearing banners, and accompanied by cavalry.

**5.152.17**  Easily handled and well-contented, with golden trappings and equipment — those many hundreds of thousands stood under the command of the cavalry.

**5.152.18**  There also were foot-soldiers of various forms and kinds, wearing armour of many types and bearing weapons, garlanded with gold.

**5.152.19**  To each chariot belonged ten elephants; to each elephant, ten horses; and ten men served as foot-guards around each horse on all sides.

**5.152.20**  Fifty elephants were allotted to each chariot; a hundred horses to each elephant; and seven men to each horse, skilled in mending and binding wounds.

**5.152.21**  A sena consists of five hundred elephants and the same number of chariots; ten senas make a pritana, and ten pritanas make a vahini.

**5.152.22**  Vahini, pritana, sena, dhvajini, sadini, camu — these are the synonymous terms for an akshauhini, also called varuthi. In this manner the armies were arrayed by the wise Kaurava.

**5.152.23**  Eleven akshauhinis in all, and also seven — the strength of the Pāṇḍavas was seven akshauhinis, while the strength of the Kauravas was eleven akshauhinis.

**5.152.24**  A patti consists of fifty-five men; three such senamukhas make a gulma, as it is named.

**5.152.25**  Ten gulmas made a gana; and ganas numbering ten thousand were the fighters ready for battle in Duryodhana's armies.

**5.152.26**  There, the mighty-armed King Duryodhana, having carefully considered brave and wise men, then appointed generals.

**5.152.27**  He gathered the leading commanders of the separate akshauhinis — the finest of men — and consecrated those kings with due rite.

**5.152.28**  Kṛpa, Droṇa, Śalya, the great chariot-warrior Saindhava, Sudakṣiṇa of Kāmboja, and Kṛtavarman —

**5.152.29**  The son of Droṇa, and Karṇa, and Bhūriśravas, and Śakuni the son of Subala, and the great chariot-warrior Bahlika —

**5.152.30**  Day after day, and at every watch, O Bharata, he arranged various signals among them, and confirmed them in his own presence again and again.

**5.152.31**  Thus all of them were disciplined, and those soldiers who followed in their footsteps, O king, were desirous of pleasing the king.

**5.153.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Then the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, with folded hands, along with all the kings of the earth, addressed Bhīṣma the son of Shantanu with these words:

**5.153.2**  Without a commander of the army, even the greatest force, when it meets battle, falls apart like an anthill.

**5.153.3**  The minds of two commanders are never alike at any time, and the valour of leaders by its very nature rivals itself.

**5.153.4**  It is heard, O greatly wise one, that against the Haihayas of immeasurable might, all the Brāhmaṇas advanced, bearing aloft banners of sacred kusha-grass.

**5.153.5**  After them followed the Vaiśyas and also the Śūdras, O Grandsire — on one side the three varnas together, and on the other the bulls among Kṣatriyas alone.

**5.153.6**  The three varnas were routed in battle again and again, while the Kṣatriyas alone, though a numerous force on one side, kept winning.

**5.153.7**  Then those best of twice-born ones questioned the Kṣatriyas themselves, and those who knew dharma declared to them the truth of the matter, O Grandsire.

**5.153.8**  We follow a single man of great intelligence in battle, but you are all separately following the dictates of your own individual minds.

**5.153.9**  Then those Brāhmaṇas appointed a single twice-born general, skilled in statecraft and brave, and thereafter they defeated the Kṣatriyas.

**5.153.10**  Thus, those who appoint as general a man skilled, brave, devoted to the good, and free from sin — they conquer their enemies in battle.

**5.153.11**  You are equal to Uśanas, and you are always a well-wisher of mine; immovable, established in dharma — be our general.

**5.153.12**  As the sun is to the rays of light, as the moon to the creeping plants, as Kubera to the yakshas, as Vāsava to the Maruts —

**5.153.13**  As Mount Meru is to mountains, as Suparṇa to birds, as Kumāra to beings, as Havyavat to the Vasus —

**5.153.14**  Protected by you, we shall be as the dwellers of heaven protected by Śakra — truly unassailable even by the thirty gods.

**5.153.15**  May you march ahead of us as Pavaki leads the gods; we shall follow you as the sons of Surabhī follow the bull.

**5.153.16**  Bhīṣma said: O mighty-armed one, it is even as you say, O Bharata; just as you are to me, so too are the Pāṇḍavas to me.

**5.153.17**  And yet, O lord of men, I must speak what is good for them; but I shall fight for your cause, as that agreement was made.

**5.153.18**  But I see no warrior on earth who is my equal, save only that tiger among men, the son of Kuntī, Dhanañjaya.

**5.153.19**  That mighty-armed one knows all the divine weapons in their entirety; yet the Pāṇḍava would never fight me openly in battle.

**5.153.20**  He and I together could in a single moment, by the sheer force of our weapons, empty this world of men — gods, asuras, and rakshasas included.

**5.153.21**  But the sons of Pāṇḍu are not to be annihilated by me, O lord of men; therefore I shall always slay ten thousand warriors each day through my skill.

**5.153.22**  Thus I shall bring about the destruction of their forces, O delight of the Kurus — unless they slay me first when battle is joined.

**5.153.23**  But I shall be your general, O king, upon another condition of my own choosing — you ought to hear it from me now.

**5.153.24**  Either Karṇa shall fight first, or I shall, O lord of the earth — for the son of a charioteer constantly and excessively rivals me in battle.

**5.153.25**  Karṇa said: I shall not fight in any manner, O king, while the son of the Gaṅgā lives; but when Bhīṣma is slain, I shall fight with the wielder of the Gāṇḍīva.

**5.153.26**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Then the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, with due rite and lavish gifts, installed Bhīṣma as general; and he, thus consecrated, shone resplendent.

**5.153.27**  Then, by royal command, men without distraction beat kettledrums and conch-shells and kettle-drums by the hundreds.

**5.153.28**  Diverse lion-roars arose and the cries of beasts of burden, and from a cloudless sky there fell a rain of blood and mud.

**5.153.29**  Thunderbolts, earthquakes, and the screaming of elephants arose and struck terror into the hearts of all warriors.

**5.153.30**  Bodiless voices were heard, and meteors fell from the sky; jackals that foretell evil cried out loudly with blazing voices again and again.

**5.153.31**  When the king consecrated Bhīṣma, son of the Ganges, as commander of the army, O king, these terrible omens manifested by the hundreds.

**5.153.32**  Then, having made Bhīṣma, the crusher of enemy forces, commander, and having caused the foremost brahmins to recite blessings and having given them golden coins and cattle in abundance,

**5.153.33**  swelling with benedictions of victory, Duryodhana marched out surrounded by soldiers, with his brothers and with Bhīṣma at the fore, and went with a great army to Kurukṣetra.

**5.153.34**  Having circumambulated Kurukṣetra together with Karṇa, the Kaurava king had his camp measured out on level ground.

**5.153.35**  In a region pleasant and not too saline, rich in barley and fodder, the camp shone just like Hāstinapura itself.

**5.154.1**  Janamejaya said: When the great-souled Bhīṣma, son of the river, foremost among weapon-bearers, grandfather of the Bharatas and banner of all kings of the earth,

**5.154.2**  equal to Bṛhaspati in wisdom, equal to the earth in forbearance, like the ocean in depth, and firm as the Himālayas,

**5.154.3**  like Prajāpati in generosity, like the sun in splendor, and like great Indra in destroying enemies with showers of arrows—

**5.154.4**  when king Yudhiṣṭhira heard that he had been consecrated for a long time to the sacrifice of battle, dreadful and hair-raising—

**5.154.5**  what did the mighty-armed one, skilled in all dharma, say? And what did Bhīmasena, Arjuna, or Kṛṣṇa do in response?

**5.154.6**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Yudhiṣṭhira, the great-minded one skilled in the duties of adversity and polity, gathered all his brothers and Vāsudeva of the Sātvata clan, and, foremost among speakers, said these words with gentle reassurance:

**5.154.7**  "Patrol the armies, stand ready, clad in armor. The battle with your grandfather will come first. Therefore look upon the seven leaders of my forces."

**5.154.8**  Vāsudeva said: "As befits you to speak at this time that has arrived, so too this meaningful speech has been uttered by you, O bull of the Bharatas."

**5.154.9**  "It pleases me, O mighty-armed one. Let what follows be done: let seven commanders be consecrated for your army."

**5.154.10**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Then, summoning Drupada and Virāṭa and the bull of the Śinis, and Dhṛṣṭadyumna the Pāñcāla prince, and the king Dhṛṣṭaketu, and Śikhaṇḍī the Pāñcāla, and Sahadeva the Magadha king,

**5.154.11**  these seven great archers, heroes who delighted in battle—Yudhiṣṭhira consecrated them as army commanders according to rite.

**5.154.12**  And as commander-in-chief over all he appointed Dhṛṣṭadyumna—he who was born from the blazing fire for the very purpose of slaying Droṇa.

**5.154.13**  But over all of them assembled, those great-souled ones, he made Guḍākeśa Dhanañjaya the lord of commanders.

**5.154.14**  And as guide and charioteer of Arjuna's horses stood the glorious and greatly wise Janārdana, younger brother of Sankarshana.

**5.154.15**  Seeing the battle imminent, the great calamity close at hand, Halayudha entered the dwelling of the Pāṇḍava king.

**5.154.16**  He came accompanied by Akrura and others, by Gada, Samba, Ulmuka and the rest, by the sons of Rukmiṇī and Ahuka, and with Cārudeṣṇa at the fore.
