**5.193.21**  Then Drupada, O best of the Bharatas, bowing in conciliation, said: "As for the speech your honour, O brāhmaṇa, has spoken to me at the bidding of my kinsman, an envoy of mine shall himself bear the answer in reply to it."

**5.193.22**  Then Drupada too sent forth to the great-souled Hiraṇyavarman an envoy, a brāhmaṇa who had reached the far shore of the Veda.

**5.193.23**  Having come into the presence of the king, the lord of the Daśārṇas, he then delivered, O king, the message that had been spoken by Drupada:

**5.193.24**  "Let an inquiry be made openly: my son is indeed a youth. This has been falsely said by someone; it is not to be believed."

**5.193.25**  Then that king, having heard Drupada's words, fell to pondering, and sent forth young women of the highest rank, of surpassing beauty, to learn whether Śikhaṇḍin was woman or man.

**5.193.26**  Those women, being sent, learned the truth of the matter, and gladly reported it all to the king—that Śikhaṇḍin, O lord of the Kurus, was a man, a mighty soul—to the Dāśārṇa king.

**5.193.27**  Then that king, having made the inquiry, was pleased, and meeting with his kinsman, he stayed there in joy and took up his lodging.

**5.193.28**  And to Śikhaṇḍin the lord of men, gladdened, gave wealth—elephants and horses and cattle, and many hundreds of female servants—and, having been honoured, he turned back his daughter and departed.

**5.193.29**  When the Dāśārṇa king Hemavarman, his rancour soothed and his heart pleased, had gone back, Śikhaṇḍin was a figure of delight.

**5.193.30**  Now after some time Kubera, borne by men, while making his circuit through the worlds, came to the dwelling of Sthūṇa.

**5.193.31**  Poised above that house, the guardian of riches looked down, and beheld the dwelling of the yakṣa Sthūṇa, finely adorned and bright with strands of garlands,

**5.193.32**  honoured with parched grain and perfumes and canopies, made fragrant with incense, decked with standards and banners, and with offerings of food, drink, and flesh laid out in oblation.

**5.193.33**  Seeing his abode thus adorned on every side, the lord of the yakṣas then spoke to those yakṣas, his attendants:

**5.193.34**  "Well adorned is this dwelling of Sthūṇa, O you of boundless valour. Yet why does that dull-witted one not approach me today?"

**5.193.35**  "Since that exceedingly dull-spirited one, though he knows I am here, does not approach me, it is my judgement that a great punishment must be meted out to him."

**5.193.36**  The yakṣas said: O king, a daughter, Śikhaṇḍinī, was born to King Drupada; and for some reason he gave to her the mark of manhood,

**5.193.37**  and took in exchange the mark of women; turned female, he stays within the house. For that reason, ashamed and bearing a woman's form, he does not approach.

**5.193.38**  For this reason, O king, does Sthūṇa not look upon you today. Having heard it, do as is fitting; let your chariot stay here a while.

**5.193.39**  Bhīṣma said: "Let Sthūṇa be brought," the lord of the yakṣas then said. "I will lay punishment upon him," he declared again and again.

**5.193.40**  Summoned, he came before the lord of the yakṣas, O lord of the earth; bearing a woman's form, O great king, he stood overcome with shame.

**5.193.41**  In great wrath the giver of wealth cursed him, O joy of the Kurus: "Even so let the womanhood of this sinful one remain, O guhyakas!"

**5.193.42**  Then the great-souled lord of the yakṣas spoke: "Since, scorning the yakṣas here, you gave your mark to Śikhaṇḍin, O evil-minded one, and took the mark of woman, O doer of sin,

**5.193.43**  since this unheard-of thing has been done by you, O most foolish one, therefore from this day onward you shall be a woman, and he a man."

**5.193.44**  Then the yakṣas sought to appease Vaiśravaṇa for Sthūṇa's sake, saying again and again: "Set a limit to the curse."

**5.193.45**  Then the great-souled lord of the yakṣas, my son, replied to all his attendant bands of yakṣas, wishing to set an end to the curse:

**5.193.46**  "When Śikhaṇḍin has been slain in battle, the yakṣa Sthūṇa shall regain his own form," said the high-minded one. "Let him be free of distress."

**5.193.47**  Having spoken thus, the blessed god, honoured by yakṣas and rākṣasas, departed together with all those who move in the span between two blinkings of the eye.

**5.193.48**  But Sthūṇa, having received the curse, dwelt on there in that place; and at the appointed time Śikhaṇḍin came to him, that wanderer of the night.

**5.193.49**  Drawing near, he spoke these words: "I have come, O blessed one." Then Sthūṇa said to him, again and again, "I am pleased."

**5.193.50**  Seeing the prince Śikhaṇḍin come in good faith, he told Śikhaṇḍin all that had befallen, just as it had happened.

**5.193.51**  The yakṣa said: I have been cursed by Vaiśravaṇa on your account, O son of a king. Go now, and roam the worlds at your will and your ease.

**5.193.52**  This, I deem, was ordained of old, and could not be overpassed—your coming, and likewise the visit of Paulastya.

**5.193.53**  Bhīṣma said: Thus addressed by the yakṣa Sthūṇa, O Bhārata, Śikhaṇḍin returned to the city filled with great joy.

**5.193.54**  With perfumes and garlands of many kinds, and with rich gifts, he honoured the twice-born and the gods, the sanctuaries, and the crossroad shrines.

**5.193.55**  And Drupada the Pāñcāla, together with his son Śikhaṇḍin whose purpose was now fulfilled, and with his kinsmen, attained the highest joy.

**5.193.56**  And he gave over Śikhaṇḍin as a pupil to Droṇa, O bull of the Kurus—that son, O great king, who had once been a woman.

**5.193.57**  That prince Śikhaṇḍin received the fourfold science of archery, together with you all, and so did Dhṛṣṭadyumna, son of Pṛṣata.

**5.193.58**  But to me, my son, all this was duly reported by my spies, who, in the guise of dullards, the blind, and the deaf, had been set by me upon Drupada.

**5.193.59**  Thus, O great king, this son of Drupada, woman-and-man, came into being—Śikhaṇḍin, O best of the Kauravas, the foremost of warriors on the car.

**5.193.60**  The eldest daughter of the lord of Kāśi, renowned by the name of Ambā, was born in the line of Drupada as Śikhaṇḍin, O bull of the Bharatas.

**5.193.61**  Even for a moment I would not look upon him, bow in hand and come forward eager to fight; nor would I strike him.

**5.193.62**  This vow of mine has ever been famed throughout the earth: that upon a woman, or upon one who was once a woman, one who bears a woman's name or a woman's form,

**5.193.63**  I would never loose my arrows, O joy of the Kurus. For this reason I will not slay Śikhaṇḍin.

**5.193.64**  I know the truth of Śikhaṇḍin's birth, my son; therefore I will not slay him in battle, even when he comes against me with weapon raised.

**5.193.65**  If Bhīṣma should slay a woman, he would as surely slay his own self. Therefore I will not slay him, even seeing him stand before me in the fray.

**5.193.66**  Saṃjaya said: Having heard this, King Duryodhana of the Kuru line then pondered for a moment, and deemed it fitting in Bhīṣma.

**5.194.1**  Saṃjaya said: When the night had brightened into dawn, your son once again, in the midst of all the host, questioned the grandsire:

**5.194.2**  "O son of Gaṅgā, this supreme army of the Pāṇḍava, abounding in men and elephants and horses, thronged with great chariot-warriors,

**5.194.3**  guarded by mighty bowmen of great strength, Bhīma and Arjuna and the rest, peers of the world-guardians, with Dhṛṣṭadyumna at their head—

**5.194.4**  unassailable, irresistible, surging like the swollen ocean, an ocean of an army not to be shaken even by the gods in great battle—

**5.194.5**  in what time, O son of Gaṅgā of great splendour, would you destroy it? Or the preceptor, that mighty bowman, or Kṛpa of exceeding strength,

**5.194.6**  or Karṇa who exults in battle, or Drauṇi, the best of the twice-born? For you are all versed in the divine weapons, and all in my host."

**5.194.7**  "This I wish to know, for a great curiosity dwells ever in my heart, O mighty-armed one; it behoves you to tell it to me."

**5.194.8**  Bhīṣma said: This befits you, O best of the Kurus, O lord of the earth—if you ask of the strength and weakness of your foes and of your own.

**5.194.9**  Hear, O king, what my utmost power in battle would be, and the might of my weapons in the fray, and the strength of my two arms, O mighty-armed one.

**5.194.10**  The honest foe is to be fought by open battle, the wielder of illusion by battle of illusion—such is the settled rule of dharma.

**5.194.11**  I would slay, O mighty-armed one, the army of the Pāṇḍavas day by day, setting myself a forenoon's portion each day.

**5.194.12**  Ten thousand warriors, O greatly resplendent one, set as my portion, and one thousand chariot-fighters besides—this is the share I reckon mine.

**5.194.13**  By this measure, armed and ever ready, I would destroy that great army in the span of such a time, O Bhārata.

**5.194.14**  But if, standing in the battle, I should loose the great weapons that slay by hundreds of thousands, I would make an end within a month, O Bhārata.

**5.194.15**  Saṃjaya said: Having heard those words of Bhīṣma, King Duryodhana then, O lord of kings, put his question to Droṇa, the best of the Aṅgirasas:

**5.194.16**  "O preceptor, in what time would you slay the soldiers of the son of Pāṇḍu?" To him Droṇa, as though with a smile, made answer:

**5.194.17**  "I am old, O best of the Kurus, slow now in breath and action; yet with the fire of my weapons I would burn up the army of the Pāṇḍavas,

**5.194.18**  in a month, even as Bhīṣma the son of Śaṃtanu has said—such is my judgement. This is my utmost power, this my utmost strength."

**5.194.19**  Kṛpa the son of Śaradvat said: "In two months only." But Drauṇi pledged the destruction of the host in ten nights; and Karṇa, the knower of the great weapons, pledged it in five.

**5.194.20**  Hearing those words of the sūta's son, the son of her who flows to the sea laughed aloud a ringing laugh, and spoke these words.

**5.194.21**  "Only because you have never yet met Pārtha in battle—bearing arrow, sword, and bow, joined with Vāsudeva, charging forward upon his chariot, that unfaltering one—

**5.194.22**  do you presume so, O Rādheya. For that reason you may go on boasting like this as much as you please."

**5.195.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Hearing this, the son of Kuntī, O best of the Bharatas, summoned all his brothers aside in private and spoke these words.

**5.195.2**  "The spies of mine who move among the armies of Dhṛtarāṣṭra's son bring me this report, now that the night has passed:

**5.195.3**  Duryodhana, it is said, asked the son of the river, the man of mighty vows: 'In what span of time, O lord, could you slay the army of the Pāṇḍavas?'

**5.195.4**  'In a month,' he was told; and the evil-minded son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra heard from Droṇa too that he would do it in just that span of time.

**5.195.5**  Gautama named twice that time, so we have heard; but Droṇa's son, knower of the great weapons, vowed to do it in ten nights.

**5.195.6**  And Karṇa, knower of celestial weapons, being asked in the assembly of the Kurus, vowed to destroy the army in five days."

**5.195.7**  "Therefore I too wish to hear your word, Arjuna: in how great a span of time could you annihilate the foes in battle?"

**5.195.8**  Thus addressed by the king, Dhanaṃjaya, the thick-haired one, looked toward Vāsudeva and answered with these words.

**5.195.9**  "All these are great souls, accomplished in arms, varied in their fighting; without doubt, O great king, they could indeed slay your host.

**5.195.10**  Let the anguish of your mind depart; I speak to you in accordance with truth. With a single chariot, having Vāsudeva for my comrade, I could slay them—

**5.195.11**  yes, the three worlds together with their gods, with all that is moving and unmoving, all that has been, that is, and that shall be—and in the blink of an eye, such is my conviction.

**5.195.12**  That terrible and mighty weapon which the Lord of Beasts granted me in our single combat, when he wore the mountaineer's form—that very weapon abides in me.

**5.195.13**  That which the Lord of Beasts wields at the end of an age, O tiger among men, when he gathers in all beings—that very weapon abides in me.

**5.195.14**  That neither the son of Gaṅgā knows, nor Droṇa, nor Gautama, nor Droṇa's son, O king—and how then the son of the sūta?

**5.195.15**  But it is not right in battle to slay common men with celestial weapons; by fair fighting alone shall we conquer the foe.

**5.195.16**  And these tigers among men, your allies, O king, are all of them knowers of celestial weapons, all of them rejoicers in battle.

**5.195.17**  All these unconquered ones, bathed in the final ablution of the Veda's culmination, could destroy in battle even the army of the gods, O Pāṇḍava.

**5.195.18**  Śikhaṇḍin and Yuyudhāna, and Dhṛṣṭadyumna the grandson of Pṛṣata, Bhīmasena and the twins both, Yudhāmanyu and Uttamaujas—

**5.195.19**  and Virāṭa and Drupada both, equal in battle to Bhīṣma and Droṇa; and you yourself are able even to wipe out the three worlds.

**5.195.20**  The man upon whom you cast your gaze in anger, O you whose splendor equals Vāsava's, that man would swiftly cease to be—this is plain, and so I know you, O Kaurava."

**5.196.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Then, when the cloudless dawn had broken, the kings, urged on by Dhṛtarāṣṭra's son Duryodhana, set forth against the Pāṇḍavas.

**5.196.2**  All of them purified by bathing, garlanded, clad in white raiment, weapons taken up, standards raised, the blessing pronounced, the fires offered into;

**5.196.3**  all knowers of the Veda, all heroes, all of upright observances, all doers of the rites, and all bearing the marks of battle;

**5.196.4**  the mighty ones, seeking through battle to win the higher worlds, all of single-pointed mind, and full of faith in what lies beyond.

**5.196.5**  Vinda and Anuvinda of Avantī, the Kekayas together with the Bāhlīkas—all these set forth with the son of Bharadvāja at their head.

**5.196.6**  Aśvatthāman, the son of Śaṃtanu, then Jayadratha of the Sindhus, and the warriors who came from the south, from the west, and from the mountains—

**5.196.7**  Śakuni the king of Gāndhāra, and those from the east and the north on every side, the Śakas, the Kirātas, the Yavanas, the Śibis, and then the Vasātis—

**5.196.8**  all these great chariot-warriors, joined each with his own troops, surrounding the great chariot-hero, marched forth in the second division of the host.

**5.196.9**  Kṛtavarman with his troops, and the mighty Trigartas, and King Duryodhana surrounded by his brothers;

**5.196.10**  Śala, Bhūriśravas, Śalya, and then Bṛhadbala of Kosala—these moved in the rear, with the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra at their head.

**5.196.11**  Having advanced along a level road, those great chariot-warriors, ready to give battle, took their stand armored in the western half of Kurukṣetra.

**5.196.12**  Duryodhana then had a camp made, O Bhārata, adorned to be like a second Hāstinapura.

**5.196.13**  Not even skilled city-dwelling men, O lord of kings, could tell the difference between the city and the camp.

**5.196.14**  Such strongholds, too, for the kings, the Kaurava lord of the earth had built by the hundreds and by the thousands.

**5.196.15**  Leaving clear a circle of five yojanas for the field of battle, the army's encampments, O king, filled it in their hundreds of companies.

**5.196.16**  There those guardians of the earth, according to their zeal and according to their strength, swiftly entered camps well stocked with goods, by the thousands.

**5.196.17**  For those great-souled ones, together with their armies and their attendants, King Duryodhana ordained provisions of food and drink beyond compare.

**5.196.18**  For the elephants, the horses, and the men, and those who lived by their crafts, and the others who had come along there—the bards, the heralds, and the panegyrists—

**5.196.19**  the merchants, the courtesans, the harlots, and the people who came merely to watch—all these the Kaurava king looked after duly.

**5.197.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: In just the same way the king, the son of Kuntī, Yudhiṣṭhira the son of Dharma, urged on the heroes led by Dhṛṣṭadyumna, O Bhārata.

**5.197.2**  He appointed Dhṛṣṭaketu—leader of the Cedis, the Kāśis, and the Karūṣas, firm in valor, slayer of foes—as commander of the host.

**5.197.3**  Virāṭa and Drupada, and Yuyudhāna and Śikhaṇḍin, and the two great archers of Pāñcāla, Yudhāmanyu and Uttamaujas—

**5.197.4**  those heroes of brilliant armor, wearing burnished earrings, blazing like fires fed with ghee upon their altars—those great archers shone like flaming planets.

**5.197.5**  Then that bull among men, that guardian of the earth, having honored the army each according to his due, commanded those troops to march.

**5.197.6**  Abhimanyu and Bṛhanta, and the sons of Draupadī all together, these led by Dhṛṣṭadyumna, the joy of Pāṇḍu sent forward.

**5.197.7**  Bhīma and Yuyudhāna and the Pāṇḍava Dhanaṃjaya—these Yudhiṣṭhira dispatched as the second wing of the army.

**5.197.8**  Of the warriors there loading the baggage, moving about and rushing to and fro, gladdened, the clamor seemed to touch the very heavens.

**5.197.9**  Then the lord of the earth himself, accompanied by Virāṭa and Drupada and the other guardians of the earth, marched in the rear.

**5.197.10**  That army, moving with its dreadful bows, with Dhṛṣṭadyumna at its head, appeared like the Gaṅgā in full flood, calm yet ever flowing on.

**5.197.11**  Then once more the wise one redeployed his divisions, baffling thereby the draining away of the wits of Dhṛtarāṣṭra's sons.

**5.197.12**  The sons of Draupadī, those great archers, and Abhimanyu, and Nakula and Sahadeva, and all the Prabhadrakas, the Pāṇḍava arrayed—

**5.197.13**  and ten thousand horses, and two thousand elephants, and ten thousand foot-soldiers, and five hundred chariots likewise.

**5.197.14**  And the unassailable Bhīmasena he assigned to the first force; in the center, Virāṭa, and Jayatsena the Māgadha;

**5.197.15**  and the two great chariot-warriors of Pāñcāla, Yudhāmanyu and Uttamaujas, valorous, great-souled, bearing mace and bow—and then in the center followed Vāsudeva and Dhanaṃjaya.

**5.197.16**  The men grew fiercely impassioned, their blows made ready; their banners, twenty thousand strong, were manned by heroes.

**5.197.17**  Five thousand elephants, and chariot-lines on every side, and the heroic foot-soldiers bearing bow, sword, and mace, followed by the thousands behind and by the thousands in front.

**5.197.18**  Where in that ocean of force Yudhiṣṭhira himself was stationed, there for the most part those guardians of the earth took up their places.

**5.197.19**  There were thousands of elephants and tens of thousands of horses, and thousands of chariots and of foot-soldiers, O Bhārata, relying upon which he went to do battle with Suyodhana, the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra.

**5.197.20**  Then others, by the hundreds behind, men by the thousands and tens of thousands, roaring as they went, marched on—their divisions numbering in the thousands.

**5.197.21**  There thousands of kettledrums and tens of thousands of conches were sounded by the men in their thousands and tens of thousands, exultant.
